1How to use this guide

This guide explains the A1.1, A1.2 and A1.3 solutions from the ground up, then walks through every line of the code in blocks (A1.3 is read from the A1_3 branch). The code shown is read from the repository when the guide is built, so the line numbers match the files.

  • Read the first chapters first: the three assignments, the system, one flight, the concepts, the state machine, the bugs we fixed, and A1.3 at a glance. Most exam questions are about these.
  • Then the code chapters, grouped by assignment (A1.1, A1.2, A1.3): code on the left, explanation on the right. The planner, the executor and the outdoor node are the cores.
  • Search (top bar) filters every block and flashcard, e.g. carrot, GUIDED, DISARM_DELAY, octile.
  • Quiz mode blurs all explanations: read the code, say aloud what it does, then click to check yourself.
  • The flashcards near the end are likely examiner questions with model answers.

Rebuild after changing code: python3 docs/study_guide/build_study_guide.py (run git fetch first to include the newest A1.3).

2The three assignments at a glance

A1.1 — map and planningA1.2 — indoor missionA1.3 — outdoor mission
GoalFrom the hangar point cloud, find collision-free 3D routesFly a mission file through the hangar: route per waypoint, tasks, landingsGPS + compass: takeoff 5 m, square with nose on a POI, figure-8 with tangent heading, return, land
Main codemap_planner_node, a_star_planner, voxel_occupancy_grid.hmission_executor_node, mission_modeloutdoor_mission_node, outdoor_math.h
Runswithout simulator (run_a1_1.sh)hangar world (run_a1_2.sh)outdoor world (run_a1_3.sh, branch A1_3)
Positionmap frame of the point cloudmap frame; MAVROS local is rotated 90°local ENU from a GPS origin (mean of stable fixes)
Setpoints—setpoint_position/local, 20 Hz, position + yaw, carrotsetpoint_raw/local, 30 Hz, position + velocity + yaw
Threads2 callback groups, MultiThreaded2 callback groups + mutex, MultiThreaded1 thread (no mutex)
Docsdocs/A1_1/docs/A1_2/docs/A1_3/ (branch A1_3)

What is shared: the ROS 2 / MAVROS / ArduPilot stack, the takeoff rule (no setpoints during NAV_TAKEOFF), unwrapped yaw, carrot following, and the "measure, don't guess" approach (logs, plots, rosbags).

3The big picture

Five programs cooperate. Think of a real drone: there is a flight controller board on it (ArduPilot) that keeps it stable and flies to a target you give it, and a companion computer (our ROS 2 nodes) that decides where to go. In simulation, Gazebo plays the role of the real world and the real sensors.

   ┌──────────── simulation (LRS-URK scripts) ────────────┐
   │  Gazebo  ◄──── lock-step ────►  ArduCopter SITL      │      physics ↔ flight controller
   │  (world, physics,               (EKF, controllers,    │
   │   sensors)                       modes, arming)       │
   └────────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────┘
                                        │ MAVLink (UDP 14550)
                                   ┌────▼────┐
                                   │ MAVROS  │  MAVLink  ⇄  ROS 2 topics / services
                                   └────┬────┘
          /mavros/state, pose, velocity │  ▲  /mavros/setpoint_position/local (20 Hz)
          GPS, FCU texts                ▼  │  set_mode, arming, takeoff, land
                         ┌──────────────────────────┐   plan_request    ┌──────────────────┐
                         │  mission_executor_node   │ ────────────────► │ map_planner_node │
                         │  (A1.2: state machine)   │ ◄──────────────── │ (A1.1: A* on     │
                         └────────────┬─────────────┘  planned_path,    │  voxel map)      │
                                      │                planning_status   └──────────────────┘
                             flight_log.csv, rosbag → plot_a1_2.py, view_3d_a1_2.py
ProgramIn one sentence
GazeboSimulates the hangar, the physics of the drone and its sensors (IMU, GPS).
ArduCopter SITLThe real autopilot firmware compiled for the PC: estimates the drone's state (EKF), stabilises it, flies to targets, checks if arming is safe.
MAVROSA bridge: converts ArduPilot's MAVLink messages to ROS 2 topics/services and back.
map_planner_nodeTurns the hangar point cloud into a 3D grid of blocked/free voxels (obstacles grown by a safety radius) and finds routes with A*.
mission_executor_nodeReads the mission, decides what to do 20× per second (state machine), sends targets and commands, logs everything.
Who flies the drone? ArduPilot does, always. Our executor never controls motors or attitude. It only says "be at this position with this heading" (a setpoint) or calls a command ("take off to 1.5 m", "land"). ArduPilot's own controllers do the rest. That is what the GUIDED mode means: an external computer guides it with targets.

4One flight, step by step (real log)

Real log lines from the test_east_aisle run, with what happens behind them. Times are seconds after the executor started; the simulation ran at 0.25–0.3× real time.

t [s]Log lineWhat is going on
0.0Mission state: IDLE → WAIT_FCUExecutor started, mission loaded and printed.
2.4Coordinate frames: MAVROS local=(-7.00, 12.99, 0.00), planner map=(12.99, 7.00, 0.00)First pose. Shows the 90° rotation between MAVROS's local frame and the map frame.
5–7Waiting for FCU readiness: GPS fix not stable yet → FCU ready (GPS fix stable)GPS fix held for 5 s.
7.7SET_MODE, GUIDED mode request sent, ARM/mavros/state confirms GUIDED; arming begins.
8–34(repeated) Arm attempt N rejected, FCU: Arm: Gyros inconsistentArduPilot's pre-arm check: both simulated IMUs must agree for ~10 s of simulated time; at 0.3× that takes ~30 s. The executor retries every 2 s.
34.6FCU: Arming motors → TAKEOFF, MAVROS accepted NAV_TAKEOFF to 1.50 mArmed. Takeoff command; no setpoints are sent now.
48.1Takeoff confirmed: z=1.43 m (threshold 1.35 m)Within 0.15 m of the target altitude.
48.1PLAN → WAIT_PLAN → NAVIGATE, Reached soft waypoint 1: error=0.071 mWaypoint 1 is directly above the start: a one-point route, already within the soft radius.
48.4WAIT_PREFETCH → ALIGN_PATHThe route to waypoint 2 was requested in advance. First turn towards the route.
53.8Aligned with path heading 53.1 deg → NAVIGATEFacing the direction of travel; the carrot starts sliding along the route.
74.0Settled at hard waypoint 2: error=0.041 m, speed=0.037 m/s for 4.00 sHard waypoint: within 0.15 m and slow for the settle time (1 s, ×4 for the slow simulation).
83.3Completed task 'yaw' at waypoint 2yaw-90 reached and held.
97.2Reached soft waypoint 3: error=0.336 m; continuing without settlingSoft: passed 0.34 m away, no stop.
143.9Aligned with path heading -540.0 degYaw is unwrapped: −540° is the same direction as 180°. The executor keeps the commanded yaw continuous so the drone never spins the long way round.
~191waypoint 6 landtakeoff: LAND → WAIT_LANDED → Touchdown → WAIT_DISARMED → FCU: Disarming motors → GROUND_WAIT → SET_MODE → ARM → RETAKEOFFLand; ArduCopter disarms itself; wait on the ground; GUIDED again; arm; take off; continue.
~271waypoint 8 land: ... Disarm confirmed on ground, Completed task 'land', DONEFinal landing and disarm.
+1 sMission DONE; executor exitsThe node shuts down; the run script stops the rosbag, plots, opens the windows.

5Concepts you must know

ROS 2

TermMeaning here
NodeA program that talks ROS. We have two: mission_executor_node, map_planner_node (plus MAVROS).
TopicA named stream of messages, one-way, many-to-many (publish/subscribe). Example: MAVROS publishes /mavros/local_position/pose; we subscribe.
ServiceA request → response call. Example: /mavros/cmd/arming with value=true answers success. We call them asynchronously (we do not block waiting).
ParameterA named setting of a node, set at start (-p name:=value or a YAML file).
QoSDelivery rules. Reliable (retransmit) vs best effort (drop is fine); transient local = late subscribers get the last message. Incompatible QoS = no messages at all.
ExecutorRuns the callbacks. MultiThreadedExecutor uses several threads.
Callback groupDecides which callbacks may run in parallel. MutuallyExclusive = one at a time within the group.
MutexA lock: only one thread at a time may touch the shared data. std::lock_guard locks for one scope.

MAVROS and MAVLink

MAVLink is the binary message protocol of ArduPilot/PX4 (e.g. HEARTBEAT, LOCAL_POSITION_NED, COMMAND_LONG, SET_POSITION_TARGET_LOCAL_NED, STATUSTEXT). MAVROS converts them to ROS and back. It also converts frames: MAVLink uses NED, ROS uses ENU.

ArduCopter

TermMeaning
GUIDED modeThe copter flies to targets sent by an external computer. Position targets are latched (kept until a new one comes); velocity/attitude targets time out after GUID_TIMEOUT.
LAND modeDescends, detects touchdown, disarms by itself.
ArmingMotors enabled. Refused if pre-arm checks fail (IMUs inconsistent, no position estimate, ...). The reason arrives as a STATUSTEXT (PreArm: .../Arm: ...).
DISARM_DELAYIf armed but sitting on the ground with no takeoff, the copter disarms after 10 s.
EKFExtended Kalman Filter: fuses IMU, GPS, compass, barometer into position/velocity/attitude. "EKF3 IMU0 is using GPS" = it has an absolute position.
NAV_TAKEOFFCommand: climb to an altitude above home. In GUIDED, a position setpoint during the climb cancels it.
SITL, lock-step, RTFSoftware In The Loop: firmware runs on the PC. Lock-step: SITL and Gazebo advance together, step by step, so if Gazebo pauses, SITL freezes ("no link"). RTF (real-time factor) = simulated seconds per real second; this PC: 0.2–0.3.

Frames and angles

  • ENU: x east, y north, z up (ROS). NED: x north, y east, z down (ArduPilot/MAVLink). MAVROS converts; we only see ENU.
  • map frame (planner, mission file, Gazebo world): rotated 90° from MAVROS's local ENU: map x = north, map y = west. Our code converts at the boundary (localToMapPose, mapToLocalPose).
  • Yaw: rotation about z. Ours: absolute in the map frame, 0° = +x, counter-clockwise positive.
  • Quaternion: (x, y, z, w) representation of a rotation; yaw only: (0, 0, sin ψ/2, cos ψ/2).
  • Wrapping keeps an angle in [−180°, 180°]; unwrapping picks the equivalent angle closest to the previous one, so commands never jump by 360°.

Planning

  • Point cloud: the hangar scanned as 620 k 3D points (map.pcd).
  • Voxel: a 0.15 m cube. Occupied if a map point falls into it.
  • Inflation: grow every obstacle voxel by a sphere of the safety radius (0.30 m). Then the drone can be treated as a point: any free voxel is safe for its centre.
  • A*: best-first graph search with f = g + h; with an admissible, consistent h the result is the shortest path on the grid.
  • 26-connectivity: each voxel has 26 neighbours (faces, edges, corners); steps cost 1, √2, √3 voxels.
  • Octile heuristic: the exact path length in empty space on that grid.
  • Line-of-sight simplification: replace the voxel staircase by the fewest straight segments that stay free.

Control

  • Setpoint: the position + yaw target streamed at 20 Hz to /mavros/setpoint_position/local.
  • Carrot: a setpoint that slides along the route ahead of the drone (max 1 m ahead), so the drone follows the straight line.
  • Hard waypoint: stop within 0.15 m and below 0.2 m/s for 1 s. Soft: pass within 0.55 m.
  • Pass radius: intermediate route points are taken within 0.45 m, without stopping.

6The state machine at a glance

IDLE ─► WAIT_FCU ─► SET_MODE ─► ARM ─► TAKEOFF ─► PLAN ─► WAIT_PLAN ─► ALIGN_PATH ◄──┐
           (GPS/EKF ok)  (GUIDED)  (armed)  (altitude)            (route ok)    │        │
                                                                                 ▼        │ sharp corner
                                                                             NAVIGATE ────┘
                                                                                 │ waypoint reached
                                                                                 ▼ (hard: settled)
              ┌──────────────────────── WAIT_PREFETCH ◄──────────────────── TASK ──► DONE (last, no land)
              │ (next route ready)                                            │
              └──► ALIGN_PATH ...                                             │ land / landtakeoff
                                                                              ▼
                     DONE ◄── (land) ── WAIT_DISARMED ◄── WAIT_LANDED ◄──── LAND
                                              │ (landtakeoff)
                                              ▼
                                         GROUND_WAIT ─► SET_MODE ─► ARM ─► RETAKEOFF ─► next waypoint
any state ──(timeout, refused service, lost link, unexpected disarm, mode change)──► ERROR (hold position)

Rules that hold in every state (checked at the top of tick()):

  • a service without an answer for 5 s → ERROR;
  • a state lasting longer than its timeout → ERROR;
  • armed but no pose for 5 s → ERROR ("telemetry stale");
  • in flight states: link lost → ERROR; disarmed → ERROR (or a takeoff retry on the ground); mode not GUIDED → ERROR.

Setpoints are streamed only in PLAN, WAIT_PLAN, WAIT_PREFETCH, ALIGN_PATH, NAVIGATE, TASK and ERROR.

7Problems we hit and how they were fixed

What did not work at the start, why, and how it was fixed. Examiners like "what problems did you have?" questions.

1. "Arms, then disarms, never takes off"

Cause: the executor streamed position setpoints during takeoff. In GUIDED mode a position target cancels ArduCopter's takeoff; position control does not lift a landed copter; after DISARM_DELAY it disarms. Fix: setpointsAllowed(), no setpoints in SET_MODE/ARM/TAKEOFF/LAND.

2. "Takes way too long to arm"

Cause: the run script waited for pre-arm good / EKF lines in the SITL log, but MAVProxy's --console prints them only in its GUI window, so the wait always hit its 240 s timeout (plus 60 s for the boot check). Fix: the script waits only for MAVProxy's own lines; the executor checks readiness through MAVROS (GPS fix, FCU texts) and logs the PreArm reasons. Startup went from ~5 min to ~1.5 min.

3. Mission died at the landtakeoff waypoint

Cause: after touchdown the executor sent a disarm before ArduCopter's land detector agreed; the FCU refused, and the refusal was treated as fatal. Fix: wait for LAND mode to disarm by itself; send our own disarm only after a grace period and retry it.

4. Too close to a rack

Observation: with safety_radius 0.30 m the clearance plot showed 0.37 m to a rack, 4 cm above the drone radius (0.33 m). 0.45 m gave 0.61 m on the same route, but blocks more waypoints near obstacles. Choice: 0.30 m (same as A1.1); raise it for more margin.

5. Drone left the straight line

Cause: the setpoint jumped to the segment end; ArduCopter mixed different horizontal and vertical speed limits (0.56 m off the route, descending early). Fix: carrot following.

6. Not a bug: "no link" in MAVProxy

Gazebo was paused with its ⏸ button; because of lock-step, SITL froze too. Do not click in Gazebo during a flight.

8Why some plot lines have gaps

In the overview plot some lines stop for a while (for example around 200–250 s in test_over_racks). Nothing was lost. Here is the simple version:

Analogy A car's cruise control graph shows "set speed" and "actual speed". While the car is parked, there is no set speed, because cruise control is off. The "set speed" line stops; the "actual speed" line continues at zero.
  • During takeoff, landing and the time on the ground in a landtakeoff, the executor sends no setpoint on purpose (bug 1). ArduCopter flies these phases by itself.
  • No setpoint means "commanded z", "commanded yaw" and "distance to target" do not exist, so their lines stop. The log column cmd_valid = 0 marks those rows.
  • "Deviation from the route" only exists while flying a route (NAVIGATE/ALIGN).
  • The measured lines (altitude, heading, speed) continue. Clearance on the ground is drawn grey. Every such period is labelled "on ground (landtakeoff)" or "landed (end)".

9A1.3 at a glance (outdoor mission)

Mission

Wait for a usable GPS fix → capture the origin → GUIDED, arm, take off to 5 m → fly to W1 → square W1→W2→W3→W4→W1 (5 m side) with the nose locked on the POI → fly to the POI → one lap of the figure-8 (A = 3 m) with the nose along the tangent → return above launch → LAND.

Key decisions (and the numbers)

TopicWhatWhy
GPS gatefix ≥ FIX, ≥ 8 satellites, σ ≤ 2 m, uninterrupted 3 snever take off on a fix that has not converged
Originmean of the fixes in those 3 s + the local pose at that momentno hard-coded coordinates; averaging removes single-fix noise
GPS ↔ metreslocal tangent plane with WGS-84 radii at the originerror 0.25 mm at 50 m, far below GPS noise; GPS is used only for the origin
Compassyaw_ENU = 90° − heading, wrappedcompass: 0 = N, clockwise; ENU: 0 = E, CCW. Verified: 0.14° mean difference
Altitudecommand local z = launch z + 5 m; check rel_alt tooAMSL, rel_alt and local z are different numbers
Squarecarrot with 0.5 m/s and 0.5 m/s² limits + velocity feed-forwardno velocity step at corners (nose dip); lag 0.8 m → 0.1 m
POI yawbearing from the measured position + 0.5 s look-ahead, every tickthe bearing changes fastest mid-side; max error 3.3°
Figure-8timer: θ(t), x = A sin θ, y = A sin θ cos θ; position + analytic velocitya continuous trajectory, not waypoints
ω0.30 rad/s (peak speed A·ω·√2 = 1.27 m/s, lap 21 s)limit 0.35 for 1.5 m/s; 0.35 would need 64°/s yaw, above ArduPilot's 60°/s
Soft start/stopθ̇ ramps 0 → ω → 0 over 3 sthe figure starts and ends at its fastest point
Tangent yawatan2(cos 2θ, cos θ), unwrapped, sent as an anglean angle corrects itself, a yaw rate would drift
Safetygeofence 4 m / 5.5 m checked before flight and in flight; tracking error > 2 m → abortthe mission must fit a small field (max 3.54 m from launch)
ExitsABORTING → abort mode (LAND); STOPPED = send nothing; FAULT on the groundnever fight the safety pilot

Results (simulation)

Square: nose vs POI mean 2.0°, max 3.3°; path error mean 0.09 m. Figure-8: path error mean 0.18 m, max 0.40 m; nose vs tangent mean 6.7°, max 23.7° (at the loop ends, where 54.5°/s of yaw are needed). Altitude 4.99–5.00 m. Farthest from launch 3.71 m (limit 4 m).

12A* search (a_star_planner.cpp, A1.1)

src/lrs_psenak_valko/src/a_star_planner.cpp · 349 lines · 24 explained blocks

The path search itself. It works on a dense 3D array of voxels in which every voxel is "free" or "blocked" (already inflated by the safety radius, so the drone can be treated as a point). It returns a list of 3D points from start to goal. It knows nothing about ROS, so it is easy to test.

A* in one paragraph For every voxel n, g(n) = cheapest known cost from the start, h(n) = an estimate of the remaining cost to the goal, f = g + h. Always expand the open voxel with the smallest f. If h never overestimates (admissible) the first time the goal is taken out of the queue its path is optimal; if h is also consistent (h(a) ≤ cost(a,b) + h(b)) no voxel ever needs to be expanded twice.
1// =============================================================================
2// a_star_planner.cpp
3//
4//  * Works on the INFLATED occupancy grid (inflation is done in VoxelOccupancyGrid).
5//  * Heuristic: 3D octile distance = exact shortest-path cost on an obstacle-free
6//    26-connected grid  ->  admissible AND consistent (see notes at the bottom).
7//  * Diagonal moves may not "squeeze" between blocked voxels (2x2x2 swept-block check).
8//  * Never hangs: goal reached / open list exhausted (NO_PATH) / time limit (TIMEOUT).
9//  * Reports planning time, number of expansions and path length.
10//
11// =============================================================================
L1–11

File summary

The header comment lists the design: inflated grid, octile heuristic (admissible and consistent), no corner squeezing, guaranteed termination, statistics.

27    case Status::TIMEOUT:             return "TIMEOUT";

L17–31

Status names

Text for each result: SUCCESS, START/GOAL_OUT_OF_BOUNDS, START/GOAL_OCCUPIED, NO_PATH (provably unreachable), TIMEOUT (limit hit, a path may exist).

32AStarPlanner::AStarPlanner(const GridInfo& info, OccupancyFn occupied, const Params& params)
33  : info_(info), occupied_fn_(std::move(occupied)), params_(params)
34{
35  // Precompute neighbour offsets and their Euclidean step cost in metres.
36  for (int dz = -1; dz <= 1; ++dz)
37    for (int dy = -1; dy <= 1; ++dy)
38      for (int dx = -1; dx <= 1; ++dx)
39      {
40        if (dx == 0 && dy == 0 && dz == 0) continue;
41        const int   nz   = std::abs(dx) + std::abs(dy) + std::abs(dz);
42        const float cost = static_cast<float>(info_.resolution * std::sqrt(static_cast<double>(nz)));
43        moves26_.push_back({dx, dy, dz, cost});
44        if (nz == 1) moves6_.push_back({dx, dy, dz, cost});
45      }
46  refresh();
47}
48
L32–48

Constructor: the 26 moves

Three nested loops over dx, dy, dz ∈ {−1, 0, 1} without (0, 0, 0) give the 26 neighbours. The cost of a move is its Euclidean length: resolution · √(number of non-zero axes) = 1, √2 or √3 voxels. The 6 face neighbours are stored separately (for allow_diagonal = false). Then the occupancy is copied.

49void AStarPlanner::refresh()
50{
51  blocked_.assign(static_cast<size_t>(info_.size_x) * info_.size_y * info_.size_z, 0);
52  for (int z = 0; z < info_.size_z; ++z)
53    for (int y = 0; y < info_.size_y; ++y)
54      for (int x = 0; x < info_.size_x; ++x)
55        blocked_[linear(x, y, z)] = occupied_fn_(x, y, z) ? 1 : 0;
56}
57
L49–57

refresh(): dense copy of the occupancy

Asks the occupancy function once per voxel and stores 1/0 in a flat byte array. During the search a lookup is then just an array index, which is fast. Called once at construction.

58bool AStarPlanner::inBounds(int x, int y, int z) const
59{
60  return x >= 0 && y >= 0 && z >= 0 && x < info_.size_x && y < info_.size_y && z < info_.size_z;
61}
62
63bool AStarPlanner::isBlocked(int x, int y, int z) const
64{
65  return !inBounds(x, y, z) || blocked_[linear(x, y, z)] != 0;
66}
67
L58–67

Bounds and blocked checks

inBounds: index inside the grid. isBlocked: outside counts as blocked, so the search can never leave the map. linear(x, y, z) = (z·sy + y)·sx + x is the position in the flat array.

68bool AStarPlanner::worldToIndex(const Vec3& p, int& x, int& y, int& z) const
69{
70  x = static_cast<int>(std::floor((p.x - info_.origin_x) / info_.resolution));
71  y = static_cast<int>(std::floor((p.y - info_.origin_y) / info_.resolution));
72  z = static_cast<int>(std::floor((p.z - info_.origin_z) / info_.resolution));
73  return inBounds(x, y, z);
74}
75
L68–75

World ↔ voxel index

index = floor((p − origin) / resolution); returns false if outside. indexToWorld returns the voxel centre (+0.5).

76Vec3 AStarPlanner::indexToWorld(int x, int y, int z) const
77{
78  return {info_.origin_x + (x + 0.5) * info_.resolution,
79          info_.origin_y + (y + 0.5) * info_.resolution,
80          info_.origin_z + (z + 0.5) * info_.resolution};
81}
82
L76–82

See above.

83bool AStarPlanner::isBlockedWorld(const Vec3& p) const
84{

86  if (!worldToIndex(p, x, y, z)) return true;
87  return isBlocked(x, y, z);

89
L83–89

Is a world point blocked?

Outside the grid = blocked. Used for segment checks.

90// Octile heuristic in 3D.  With sorted axis distances d1<=d2<=d3 (in cells):
91//   h = res * [ sqrt3*d1 + sqrt2*(d2-d1) + 1*(d3-d2) ]
92//     = res * [ (sqrt3-sqrt2)*d1 + (sqrt2-1)*d2 + d3 ]
93// This is the exact cost of the cheapest 26-connected path in EMPTY space, so
94// it can never overestimate -> admissible; it also satisfies the triangle
95// inequality over the move set -> consistent (closed set is safe).
96// For 6-connectivity the exact empty-space cost is the Manhattan distance.
97double AStarPlanner::heuristic(int x, int y, int z, int gx, int gy, int gz) const
98{
99  std::array<int, 3> d = {std::abs(gx - x), std::abs(gy - y), std::abs(gz - z)};
100  if (!params_.allow_diagonal)
101    return info_.resolution * (d[0] + d[1] + d[2]);
102  std::sort(d.begin(), d.end());
103  static const double S2 = std::sqrt(2.0), S3 = std::sqrt(3.0);
104  return info_.resolution * ((S3 - S2) * d[0] + (S2 - 1.0) * d[1] + d[2]);
105}
106
L90–106

The 3D octile heuristic

Sort the axis distances d1 ≤ d2 ≤ d3 (in voxels). In empty space the cheapest 26-connected path takes d1 steps diagonal in 3 axes (√3 each), then d2 − d1 steps diagonal in 2 axes (√2), then d3 − d2 straight steps (1):

h = res · [√3·d1 + √2·(d2 − d1) + (d3 − d2)] = res · [(√3 − √2)·d1 + (√2 − 1)·d2 + d3]

Obstacles can only make the real path longer, so h is admissible; it is also consistent, so the result is optimal on the grid. Euclidean distance would also be admissible but smaller (more expansions). Manhattan distance overestimates when diagonals are allowed (not admissible). With 6-connectivity, Manhattan is exact and is used.

107// A diagonal move is only legal if every voxel in the box spanned by the move is
108// free.  This stops the path from slipping through a shared edge/corner of two
109// obstacle voxels (which the physical drone body cannot do).
110bool AStarPlanner::moveIsFree(int x, int y, int z, int dx, int dy, int dz) const
111{
112  const int xs[2] = {0, dx}, ys[2] = {0, dy}, zs[2] = {0, dz};
113  for (int a = 0; a < (dx != 0 ? 2 : 1); ++a)
114    for (int b = 0; b < (dy != 0 ? 2 : 1); ++b)
115      for (int c = 0; c < (dz != 0 ? 2 : 1); ++c)
116        if (isBlocked(x + xs[a], y + ys[b], z + zs[c])) return false;
117  return true;
118}
119
L107–119

No squeezing through corners

A diagonal move is allowed only if every voxel in the 2×2(×2) box it crosses is free. Otherwise the path could pass between two blocked voxels that touch only at an edge or corner, a gap no real drone fits through.

120bool AStarPlanner::findNearestFree(int& x, int& y, int& z, int radius) const
121{
122  if (!isBlocked(x, y, z)) return true;
123  double best = std::numeric_limits<double>::infinity();
124  int bx = 0, by = 0, bz = 0;
125  for (int dz = -radius; dz <= radius; ++dz)
126    for (int dy = -radius; dy <= radius; ++dy)
127      for (int dx = -radius; dx <= radius; ++dx)
128      {
129        if (isBlocked(x + dx, y + dy, z + dz)) continue;
130        const double d = dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz;
131        if (d < best) { best = d; bx = x + dx; by = y + dy; bz = z + dz; }
132      }
133  if (!std::isfinite(best)) return false;
134  x = bx; y = by; z = bz;
135  return true;
136}
137
L120–137

findNearestFree(): snapping

If start/goal is blocked: search a cube of ±radius voxels for the free voxel with the smallest squared distance. Used when snap_radius_cells > 0 (5 in our config).

138// Sample the straight segment a->b every quarter of a voxel.  Since the grid is
139// inflated, a tiny sampling error at voxel corners is swallowed by the safety margin.
140bool AStarPlanner::segmentFree(const Vec3& a, const Vec3& b) const
141{
142  const double dx = b.x - a.x, dy = b.y - a.y, dz = b.z - a.z;
143  const double len = std::sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz);
144  const int n = std::max(1, static_cast<int>(std::ceil(len / (0.25 * info_.resolution))));
145  for (int i = 0; i <= n; ++i)
146  {
147    const double t = static_cast<double>(i) / n;
148    if (isBlockedWorld({a.x + t * dx, a.y + t * dy, a.z + t * dz})) return false;
149  }
150  return true;
151}
152
L138–152

segmentFree(): line of sight

Checks a straight segment by sampling it every quarter voxel. Thanks to the inflation, a tiny miss at a voxel corner is still inside the safety margin.

153// Greedy line-of-sight shortcutting: from waypoint i jump to the farthest
154// waypoint j that is still visible in the inflated map.
155std::vector<Vec3> AStarPlanner::simplify(const std::vector<Vec3>& in) const
156{
157  if (in.size() < 3) return in;
158  std::vector<Vec3> out;
159  size_t i = 0;
160  out.push_back(in[0]);
161  while (i < in.size() - 1)
162  {
163    size_t j = in.size() - 1;
164    while (j > i + 1 && !segmentFree(in[i], in[j])) --j;
165    out.push_back(in[j]);
166    i = j;
167  }
168  return out;
169}
170
L153–170

simplify(): fewer, longer segments

From point i, take the farthest later point j that is still directly visible (segment free), add it, continue from j. The staircase of voxel steps becomes a few straight segments (e.g. 39 → 5 points), which the drone flies smoothly.

171Result AStarPlanner::plan(const Vec3& start, const Vec3& goal) const
172{
173  using Clock = std::chrono::steady_clock;
174  const auto t0 = Clock::now();
175  auto elapsedMs = [&]() {
176    return std::chrono::duration<double, std::milli>(Clock::now() - t0).count();
177  };
178
179  Result res;
180  auto finish = [&](Status s) {
181    res.status = s;
182    res.planning_time_ms = elapsedMs();
183    return res;
184  };
185
L171–185

plan(): timing helpers

Start a timer; lambdas elapsedMs and finish(status) (sets status and planning time) keep the many return points short.

186  // ---- 1. validate & snap start / goal --------------------------------------
187  int sx, sy, sz, gx, gy, gz;
188  if (!worldToIndex(start, sx, sy, sz)) return finish(Status::START_OUT_OF_BOUNDS);
189  if (!worldToIndex(goal,  gx, gy, gz)) return finish(Status::GOAL_OUT_OF_BOUNDS);
190
191  bool start_snapped = false, goal_snapped = false;
192  if (isBlocked(sx, sy, sz))
193  {
194    if (params_.snap_radius_cells <= 0 || !findNearestFree(sx, sy, sz, params_.snap_radius_cells))
195      return finish(Status::START_OCCUPIED);
196    start_snapped = true;
197  }
198  if (isBlocked(gx, gy, gz))
199  {
200    if (params_.snap_radius_cells <= 0 || !findNearestFree(gx, gy, gz, params_.snap_radius_cells))
201      return finish(Status::GOAL_OCCUPIED);
202    goal_snapped = true;
203  }
204  res.start_snapped = start_snapped;
205  res.goal_snapped  = goal_snapped;
206  res.start_used = start_snapped ? indexToWorld(sx, sy, sz) : start;
207  res.goal_used  = goal_snapped  ? indexToWorld(gx, gy, gz) : goal;
208
L186–208

Step 1: check and snap the endpoints

Convert start/goal to indices (outside the grid: error). If one is blocked: snap to the nearest free voxel if allowed, else START/GOAL_OCCUPIED. Remember whether snapping happened (reported, and the executor checks how far the goal moved).

209  // ---- 2. A* ------------------------------------------------------------------
210  const size_t N = blocked_.size();
211  const float  INF = std::numeric_limits<float>::infinity();
212  std::vector<float>    g(N, INF);
213  std::vector<int32_t>  parent(N, -1);
214  std::vector<uint8_t>  closed(N, 0);
215
L209–215

Step 2: data structures

Per voxel: g (cost so far, ∞), parent (where we came from, −1), closed (already expanded): 4 + 4 + 1 bytes per voxel.

216  struct Entry { float f, g; uint32_t idx; };
217  // std::priority_queue is a max-heap: "less" == lower priority.
218  // Lower f first; on ties prefer larger g (deeper node) -> fewer expansions.
219  auto lower = [](const Entry& a, const Entry& b) {
220    if (a.f != b.f) return a.f > b.f;
221    return a.g < b.g;
222  };
223  std::priority_queue<Entry, std::vector<Entry>, decltype(lower)> open(lower);
224
225  const size_t s_idx = linear(sx, sy, sz);
226  const size_t g_idx = linear(gx, gy, gz);
227  const float  w     = static_cast<float>(params_.heuristic_weight);
228
229  g[s_idx] = 0.f;
230  open.push({w * static_cast<float>(heuristic(sx, sy, sz, gx, gy, gz)), 0.f, static_cast<uint32_t>(s_idx)});
231
232  const std::vector<Move>& moves = params_.allow_diagonal ? moves26_ : moves6_;
233  bool found = false, timed_out = false;
234  double best_d2 = std::numeric_limits<double>::infinity();   // closest approach to goal (diagnostics)
235  int best_x = sx, best_y = sy, best_z = sz;
236
L216–236

The open list (priority queue)

std::priority_queue is a max-heap, so the comparator is inverted: the entry with the smallest f comes first. On equal f the larger g wins (a node closer to the goal), which reduces expansions. The start is pushed with g = 0, f = w·h. w = heuristic_weight (1 = optimal A*, > 1 = faster weighted A*).

237  while (!open.empty())
238  {
239    const Entry cur = open.top();
240    open.pop();
241    if (closed[cur.idx]) continue;          // stale duplicate (lazy deletion)
242    closed[cur.idx] = 1;
243
244    if (cur.idx == g_idx) { found = true; break; }
245    ++res.expansions;
246
247    if ((res.expansions & 0x3FF) == 0)      // check limits every 1024 expansions
248    {
249      if (elapsedMs() > params_.max_planning_time_s * 1000.0) { timed_out = true; break; }
250    }
251    if (params_.max_expansions && res.expansions >= params_.max_expansions) { timed_out = true; break; }
252
253    // decode voxel coordinates
254    const int cx = static_cast<int>(cur.idx % info_.size_x);
255    const int cy = static_cast<int>((cur.idx / info_.size_x) % info_.size_y);
256    const int cz = static_cast<int>(cur.idx / (static_cast<size_t>(info_.size_x) * info_.size_y));
257
258    {
259      const double ddx = cx - gx, ddy = cy - gy, ddz = cz - gz;
260      const double d2 = ddx * ddx + ddy * ddy + ddz * ddz;
261      if (d2 < best_d2) { best_d2 = d2; best_x = cx; best_y = cy; best_z = cz; }
262    }
263
L237–263

Main loop: take the best voxel

  • Pop the smallest f. Lazy deletion: a voxel can be in the queue several times (with different f); if it is already closed, skip the stale copy.
  • Goal popped: found (optimal because the heuristic is consistent).
  • Every 1024 expansions check the time limit, and the optional expansion limit.
  • Decode x, y, z from the flat index. Track the voxel closest to the goal (for the error report when no path exists).
264    for (const Move& m : moves)
265    {
266      const int nx = cx + m.dx, ny = cy + m.dy, nz = cz + m.dz;
267      if (isBlocked(nx, ny, nz)) continue;
268      if (!moveIsFree(cx, cy, cz, m.dx, m.dy, m.dz)) continue;
269
270      const size_t nidx = linear(nx, ny, nz);
271      if (closed[nidx]) continue;
272
273      const float ng = cur.g + m.cost;
274      if (ng < g[nidx])
275      {
276        g[nidx] = ng;
277        parent[nidx] = static_cast<int32_t>(cur.idx);
278        const float f = ng + w * static_cast<float>(heuristic(nx, ny, nz, gx, gy, gz));
279        open.push({f, ng, static_cast<uint32_t>(nidx)});
280      }
281    }
282  }
283
L264–283

Relax the neighbours

For each move: skip blocked targets and corner-squeezing diagonals, skip closed voxels. New cost ng = g + move cost; if better than the known g, store it and the parent and push with f = ng + w·h. This is the standard A* "relaxation".

284  if (std::isfinite(best_d2))
285  {
286    res.closest_to_goal = indexToWorld(best_x, best_y, best_z);
287    res.closest_dist_m  = std::sqrt(best_d2) * info_.resolution;
288  }
289  if (!found) return finish(timed_out ? Status::TIMEOUT : Status::NO_PATH);
290
L284–290

No path: diagnostics

Store the closest point reached. Open list empty: NO_PATH (the goal is provably unreachable in this grid); limit hit: TIMEOUT.

291  // ---- 3. reconstruct path -----------------------------------------------------
292  std::vector<Vec3> raw;
293  for (int32_t i = static_cast<int32_t>(g_idx); i != -1; i = parent[i])
294  {
295    const int x = static_cast<int>(i % info_.size_x);
296    const int y = static_cast<int>((i / info_.size_x) % info_.size_y);
297    const int z = static_cast<int>(i / (static_cast<size_t>(info_.size_x) * info_.size_y));
298    raw.push_back(indexToWorld(x, y, z));
299  }
300  std::reverse(raw.begin(), raw.end());
301
302  // Use the exact requested endpoints (they lie inside the free start/goal voxels)
303  // unless we had to snap to another voxel.
304  if (!start_snapped) raw.front() = start;
305  if (!goal_snapped)  raw.back()  = goal;
306  res.raw_waypoints = raw.size();
307  for (size_t i = 1; i < raw.size(); ++i)
308  {
309    const double dx = raw[i].x - raw[i - 1].x, dy = raw[i].y - raw[i - 1].y, dz = raw[i].z - raw[i - 1].z;
310    res.raw_path_length_m += std::sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz);
311  }
312
L291–312

Step 3: rebuild the path

Follow parent from the goal back to the start, converting to voxel centres, then reverse. Replace the first/last centre by the exact requested start/goal (unless snapped). Count points and length before simplification.

313  res.path = params_.simplify_path ? simplify(raw) : raw;
314
315  for (size_t i = 1; i < res.path.size(); ++i)
316  {
317    const double dx = res.path[i].x - res.path[i - 1].x;
318    const double dy = res.path[i].y - res.path[i - 1].y;
319    const double dz = res.path[i].z - res.path[i - 1].z;
320    res.path_length_m += std::sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz);
321  }
322  return finish(Status::SUCCESS);
323}

L313–324

Simplify and measure

Line-of-sight shortcutting (if enabled), final length, SUCCESS. The planner node then checks the simplified path once more against the inflated map before publishing it.

325}  // namespace astar
326
327// ============================================================================
328// NOTES FOR THE DOCUMENTATION
329//
330// Heuristic:  3D octile distance (see heuristic()).  On an empty 26-connected
331//   grid the cheapest route from a to b uses min(d) triple-diagonals, then
332//   (mid-min) double-diagonals, then straight steps - that is exactly the
333//   formula, so obstacles can only make the true cost larger, never smaller
334//   => admissible.  It is also consistent, so a node is never re-opened and
335//   the returned path is optimal on the grid (heuristic_weight = 1).
336//   Euclidean distance would also be admissible but is weaker (smaller h ->
337//   more expansions); Manhattan would be inadmissible with diagonal moves.
338//
339// Why the corner-cutting check: a diagonal step between two voxels that only
340//   touch at an edge/corner would let the path squeeze through a gap the drone
341//   cannot physically pass.  Combined with inflation this keeps the path clear.
342//
343// Complexity: O(E log V) with a binary heap, V = free voxels reachable before
344//   the goal is popped.  Memory: 4 + 4 + 1 + 1 bytes per voxel.
345//
346// Termination: the open list is finite (each voxel is closed at most once),
347//   so a missing path ends with NO_PATH; the time / expansion limits bound
348//   even huge maps (TIMEOUT).
349// ============================================================================
L325–349

Notes for the documentation

The author's own summary: heuristic proof, why the corner check exists, complexity O(E log V) with a binary heap, memory per voxel, and why the search always terminates. Useful exam material.

Validation

Rejects invalid values: voxel size ≤ 0, negative radius, rack list not a multiple of 7, missing racks when required, heuristic weight < 1 (would overestimate and could produce non-optimal and odd paths), negative limits.

139  void loadMap(const std::string& path, const std::string& occupancy_pcd_path,
140               float leaf_size, float safety_radius,

142  {

147

151                min_point.x, max_point.x, min_point.y, max_point.y, min_point.z, max_point.z);
152

154    pcl::VoxelGrid<pcl::PointXYZ> filter;
155    filter.setInputCloud(raw);

157    filter.filter(*downsampled);

181    RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "Saved inflated voxel centers (including racks) to %s",
182                occupancy_pcd_path.c_str());
183    sensor_msgs::msg::PointCloud2 occupancy_message;
184    pcl::toROSMsg(inflated_points, occupancy_message);
185    occupancy_message.header.frame_id = "map";
186    occupancy_publisher_->publish(occupancy_message);

188    const VoxelOccupancyGrid::IndexBounds bounds = grid_->inflatedBounds();
189    if (!bounds.valid) throw std::runtime_error("no occupied voxels in planning map");
190    constexpr int64_t padding = 1;
191    const int64_t sx = bounds.max_x - bounds.min_x + 1 + 2 * padding;
192    const int64_t sy = bounds.max_y - bounds.min_y + 1 + 2 * padding;
193    const int64_t sz = bounds.max_z - bounds.min_z + 1 + 2 * padding;
194    const double cells = static_cast<double>(sx) * sy * sz;
195    if (cells > 4e8) throw std::runtime_error("planning grid exceeds 400 million cells");
196
197    grid_info_.resolution = leaf_size;
198    grid_info_.origin_x = static_cast<double>(bounds.min_x - padding) * leaf_size;
199    grid_info_.origin_y = static_cast<double>(bounds.min_y - padding) * leaf_size;
200    grid_info_.origin_z = static_cast<double>(bounds.min_z - padding) * leaf_size;
201    grid_info_.size_x = static_cast<int>(sx);
202    grid_info_.size_y = static_cast<int>(sy);
203    grid_info_.size_z = static_cast<int>(sz);
204    const int64_t ox = bounds.min_x - padding;
205    const int64_t oy = bounds.min_y - padding;
206    const int64_t oz = bounds.min_z - padding;
207    planner_ = std::make_unique<astar::AStarPlanner>(
208        grid_info_, [this, ox, oy, oz](int x, int y, int z) {
209          return grid_->isOccupiedInflatedIndex(ox + x, oy + y, oz + z);
210        }, astar_params_);
211
212    RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "Voxelized points: %zu; occupied voxels: %zu; inflated: %zu",
213                downsampled->size(), grid_->voxelCount(), grid_->inflatedVoxelCount());
214    RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "3D planning grid: %d x %d x %d", grid_info_.size_x,
215                grid_info_.size_y, grid_info_.size_z);

L188–217

Dense grid for A*

The sparse grid is copied into a dense box covering all inflated voxels plus one voxel of padding, so the outside is never "free". Sizes are checked (max 400 M cells). The lambda converts A*'s local indices to the sparse grid's global indices.

218  bool pathIsCollisionFree(const std::vector<astar::Vec3>& path) const
219  {
220    const double step = 0.25 * grid_->leafSize();
221    for (size_t i = 0; i < path.size(); ++i)
222    {
223      if (grid_->isOccupiedInflated(path[i].x, path[i].y, path[i].z)) return false;
224      if (i == 0) continue;
225      const astar::Vec3& a = path[i - 1];
226      const astar::Vec3& b = path[i];
227      const double dx = b.x - a.x, dy = b.y - a.y, dz = b.z - a.z;
228      const int samples = std::max(1, static_cast<int>(
229          std::ceil(std::sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz) / step)));
230      for (int k = 0; k <= samples; ++k)
231      {
232        const double t = static_cast<double>(k) / samples;
233        if (grid_->isOccupiedInflated(a.x + t * dx, a.y + t * dy, a.z + t * dz)) return false;
234      }
235    }
236    return true;
237  }
238
L218–238

Independent collision check

Before publishing, every point and every segment (sampled every quarter voxel) is checked against the inflated map once more. This is a second, independent safety check after A*.

239  void publishRackBoxes(const std::vector<double>& boxes)
240  {
241    visualization_msgs::msg::Marker marker;
242    marker.header.frame_id = "map";
243    marker.header.stamp = now();
244    marker.ns = "rack_collision_boxes";
245    marker.id = 0;
246    marker.type = visualization_msgs::msg::Marker::LINE_LIST;
247    marker.action = visualization_msgs::msg::Marker::ADD;
248    marker.pose.orientation.w = 1.0;
249    marker.scale.x = 0.045;
250    marker.color.r = 1.0f;
251    marker.color.g = 0.72f;
252    marker.color.b = 0.08f;
253    marker.color.a = 1.0f;
254
255    constexpr int edges[12][2] = {
256      {0, 1}, {0, 2}, {0, 4}, {1, 3}, {1, 5}, {2, 3},
257      {2, 6}, {3, 7}, {4, 5}, {4, 6}, {5, 7}, {6, 7}
258    };
259    for (size_t offset = 0; offset < boxes.size(); offset += 7)
260    {
261      const double cx = boxes[offset], cy = boxes[offset + 1], cz = boxes[offset + 2];
262      const double hx = 0.5 * boxes[offset + 3];
263      const double hy = 0.5 * boxes[offset + 4];
264      const double hz = 0.5 * boxes[offset + 5];
265      const double c = std::cos(boxes[offset + 6]);
266      const double s = std::sin(boxes[offset + 6]);
267      geometry_msgs::msg::Point corners[8];
268      for (int corner = 0; corner < 8; ++corner)
269      {
270        const double lx = (corner & 1) ? hx : -hx;
271        const double ly = (corner & 2) ? hy : -hy;
272        const double lz = (corner & 4) ? hz : -hz;
273        corners[corner].x = cx + c * lx - s * ly;
274        corners[corner].y = cy + s * lx + c * ly;
275        corners[corner].z = cz + lz;
276      }
277      for (const auto& edge : edges)
278      {
279        marker.points.push_back(corners[edge[0]]);
280        marker.points.push_back(corners[edge[1]]);
281      }
282    }
283    rack_marker_publisher_->publish(marker);
284  }

L309–348

Check, then publish

A colliding path is never published (the report says why). Otherwise the route goes out as a PoseArray, an RViz line marker, and the report with [OK] TRASA NÁJDENÁ. The executor needs both the route and the report.

349  //  Human readable report (Slovak) -- published on `planning_status` and logged.
350  // ------------------------------------------------------------------------
351  static std::string fmt(double v, int prec = 2)
352  {
353    std::ostringstream o;
354    o << std::fixed << std::setprecision(prec) << v;
355    return o.str();
356  }
357  static std::string pt(const astar::Vec3& p)
358  {
359    return "(" + fmt(p.x) + ", " + fmt(p.y) + ", " + fmt(p.z) + ") m";
360  }
361  static double dist3(const astar::Vec3& a, const astar::Vec3& b)
362  {
363    const double dx = a.x - b.x, dy = a.y - b.y, dz = a.z - b.z;
364    return std::sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz);
365  }
366
367  /// Nearest point that is free in the INFLATED map, searched within max_m.
368  bool nearestFree(const astar::Vec3& p, double max_m, astar::Vec3& out, double& d) const
369  {
370    const double step = grid_info_.resolution;
371    const int R = static_cast<int>(std::ceil(max_m / step));
372    double best = std::numeric_limits<double>::infinity();
373    for (int dz = -R; dz <= R; ++dz)
374      for (int dy = -R; dy <= R; ++dy)
375        for (int dx = -R; dx <= R; ++dx)
376        {
377          const astar::Vec3 q{p.x + dx * step, p.y + dy * step, p.z + dz * step};
378          if (planner_->isBlockedWorld(q)) continue;
379          const double dd = dist3(p, q);
380          if (dd < best && dd <= max_m) { best = dd; out = q; }
381        }
382    d = best;
383    return std::isfinite(best);
384  }
385
386  /// Explains why an endpoint is not usable (out of bounds / inside obstacle / inside safety margin).
L349–386

Report helpers

Number/point formatting, distance, and nearestFree (closest free voxel within 3 m, used to suggest a fix).

387  void describeBadEndpoint(std::ostringstream& o, const char* name, const astar::Vec3& p,
388                           bool out_of_bounds) const
389  {
390    const auto& gi = grid_info_;
391    if (out_of_bounds)
392    {
393      const double mn[3] = {gi.origin_x, gi.origin_y, gi.origin_z};
394      const double mx[3] = {gi.origin_x + gi.size_x * gi.resolution,
395                            gi.origin_y + gi.size_y * gi.resolution,
396                            gi.origin_z + gi.size_z * gi.resolution};
397      const double v[3] = {p.x, p.y, p.z};
398      const char* ax[3] = {"x", "y", "z"};
399      o << "  " << name << " " << pt(p) << " leží MIMO plánovacej mriežky.\n";
400      for (int k = 0; k < 3; ++k)
401        if (v[k] < mn[k] || v[k] >= mx[k])
402          o << "    - os " << ax[k] << " = " << fmt(v[k]) << " m, povolený rozsah je ["
403            << fmt(mn[k]) << ", " << fmt(mx[k]) << "] m\n";
404      o << "  Tip: skontroluj súradnicový systém (origin mapy vs. pozícia dronu) a jednotky (metre).\n";
405      return;
406    }
407
408    const bool raw = grid_->isOccupied(static_cast<float>(p.x), static_cast<float>(p.y),
409                                       static_cast<float>(p.z));
410    if (raw)
411      o << "  " << name << " " << pt(p) << " leží PRIAMO V PREKÁŽKE (voxel z mapy alebo z vyplneného regálu).\n";
412    else
413      o << "  " << name << " " << pt(p) << " je voľný, ale leží v BEZPEČNOSTNOM PÁSME okolo prekážky "
414        << "(safety_radius = " << fmt(safety_radius_) << " m).\n";
415
416    astar::Vec3 q;
417    double d = 0.0;
418    if (nearestFree(p, 3.0, q, d))
419    {
420      const int cells = static_cast<int>(std::ceil(d / grid_info_.resolution));
421      o << "    - najbližší voľný bod: " << pt(q) << ", vzdialenosť " << fmt(d) << " m (" << cells
422        << " voxelov)\n";
423      o << "  Tip: posuň bod do voľného priestoru, alebo nastav snap_radius_cells >= " << cells
424        << " (teraz " << astar_params_.snap_radius_cells << "), alebo zníž safety_radius.\n";
425    }
426    else
427      o << "    - do 3 m od bodu nie je žiadny voľný voxel -> bod je hlboko v obstacle/racku.\n";
428  }

describeBadEndpoint()

Explains why a start/goal is unusable: outside the map (which axis), inside an obstacle, or only inside the safety zone, with the nearest free point and how to fix it.

430  std::string buildReport(const astar::Result& r, const astar::Vec3& s, const astar::Vec3& g,
431                          bool collision_free) const
432  {
433    const std::string bar(64, '=');
434    const std::string line(64, '-');
435    const double res = grid_info_.resolution;
436    std::ostringstream o;
437    o << bar << "\n";
438    o << " Štart : " << pt(s) << "\n";
439    o << " Cieľ  : " << pt(g) << "\n";
440    o << " Priama vzdialenosť štart-cieľ: " << fmt(dist3(s, g)) << " m"
441      << "  (dz = " << fmt(g.z - s.z) << " m)\n";
442    o << line << "\n";
443
444    const bool ok = r.success() && collision_free;
445    o << " VÝSLEDOK: " << (ok ? "[OK] TRASA NÁJDENÁ" : "[CHYBA] TRASA NEBOLA NÁJDENÁ / NEPLATNÁ") << "\n";
446    o << " Stav plánovača: " << astar::toString(r.status) << "\n";
447    o << line << "\n";
448
449    if (!r.success())
450    {
451      o << " DÔVOD:\n";
452      switch (r.status)
453      {
454        case astar::Status::START_OUT_OF_BOUNDS:
455          describeBadEndpoint(o, "Štart", s, true);
456          break;
457        case astar::Status::GOAL_OUT_OF_BOUNDS:
458          describeBadEndpoint(o, "Cieľ", g, true);
459          break;
460        case astar::Status::START_OCCUPIED:
461          describeBadEndpoint(o, "Štart", s, false);
462          break;
463        case astar::Status::GOAL_OCCUPIED:
464          describeBadEndpoint(o, "Cieľ", g, false);
465          break;
466        case astar::Status::NO_PATH:
467          o << "  Štart aj cieľ sú voľné, ale cieľ je z neho NEDOSIAHNUTEĽNÝ (prehľadaný celý\n"
468            << "  súvislý voľný priestor okolo štartu a cieľ v ňom nie je).\n";
469          o << "    - prehľadaných voxelov: " << r.expansions << " (~"
470            << fmt(r.expansions * res * res * res, 1) << " m3 voľného priestoru)\n";
471          if (std::isfinite(r.closest_dist_m))
472            o << "    - najbližšie k cieľu sa trasa dostala na " << fmt(r.closest_dist_m) << " m, bod "
473              << pt(r.closest_to_goal) << "\n";
474          o << "  Možné príčiny:\n"
475            << "    1) safety_radius (" << fmt(safety_radius_) << " m) uzavrel úzky priechod -> skús menší\n"
476            << "    2) cieľ je v uzavretom priestore (za regálom / v obstacle)\n"
477            << "    3) rack_boxes zablokovali jediný priechod -> skontroluj rozmery a yaw\n";
478          break;
479        case astar::Status::TIMEOUT:
480        {
481          const bool by_exp = astar_params_.max_expansions && r.expansions >= astar_params_.max_expansions;
482          o << "  Prekročený limit " << (by_exp ? "počtu expanzií" : "času plánovania")
483            << " skôr, než sa našla odpoveď (trasa môže existovať).\n";
484          o << "    - limit času: " << fmt(astar_params_.max_planning_time_s, 1) << " s, uplynulo "
485            << fmt(r.planning_time_ms / 1000.0, 2) << " s\n";
486          o << "    - limit expanzií: "
487            << (astar_params_.max_expansions ? std::to_string(astar_params_.max_expansions) : "bez limitu")
488            << ", vykonaných " << r.expansions << "\n";
489          if (std::isfinite(r.closest_dist_m))
490            o << "    - najbližšie k cieľu: " << fmt(r.closest_dist_m) << " m, bod "
491              << pt(r.closest_to_goal) << "\n";
492          o << "  Tip: zvýš max_planning_time_s / max_expansions, alebo heuristic_weight > 1 (rýchlejšie,\n"
493            << "  mierne suboptimálne), alebo zväčši voxel_size.\n";
494          break;
495        }
496        default:
497          o << "  Neznámy dôvod.\n";
498      }
499      o << line << "\n";
500      o << " Čas plánovania: " << fmt(r.planning_time_ms, 1) << " ms, expanzií: " << r.expansions << "\n";
501      o << " Parametre: voxel_size=" << fmt(leaf_size_) << " m, safety_radius=" << fmt(safety_radius_)
502        << " m, heuristic_weight=" << fmt(astar_params_.heuristic_weight, 1) << ", "
503        << (astar_params_.allow_diagonal ? "26" : "6") << "-susedstvo\n";
504      o << bar;
505      return o.str();
506    }

557int main(int argc, char** argv)
558{
559  rclcpp::init(argc, argv);
560  try
561  {
562    auto node = std::make_shared<MapPlannerNode>();
563    // Needed for the two callback groups above to actually run concurrently --
564    // the default single-threaded rclcpp::spin() would serialize them anyway.
565    rclcpp::executors::MultiThreadedExecutor executor;
566    executor.add_node(node);
567    executor.spin();
568  }
569  catch (const std::exception& error)
570  {
571    RCLCPP_FATAL(rclcpp::get_logger("map_planner_node"), "%s", error.what());
572    rclcpp::shutdown();
573    return 1;
574  }
575  rclcpp::shutdown();
576  return 0;
577}
L557–577

main()

Same pattern as the executor: init, create the node, MultiThreadedExecutor, spin; errors are logged as FATAL with exit code 1.

14Sparse voxel grid (voxel_occupancy_grid.h, A1.1)

src/lrs_psenak_valko/include/lrs_psenak_valko/voxel_occupancy_grid.h · 191 lines · 12 explained blocks

Stores only the occupied voxels, in a hash set. "Free" = not in the set. This needs little memory for a big, mostly empty hangar. It fills the racks and inflates the obstacles by the safety radius.

1// voxel_occupancy_grid.h
2//
3// Sparse 3D occupancy grid (hash set of occupied voxels) with
4//   * build()      : point cloud -> occupied voxels
5//   * addBox()     : fill a SOLID box (used for the shelving racks, whose
6//                    point-cloud surfaces leave free-looking gaps between levels)
7//   * inflate()    : grow every occupied voxel into a sphere of a given radius
8//   * queries      : world coordinates OR integer voxel indices
9//   * bounds       : index range of the inflated map (needed by the dense A* grid)
10//
11// Only OCCUPIED voxels are stored.  "Free" simply means "not in the set".
12// Typical call order:  build() -> addBox()* -> inflate().
13
L1–13

Header comment

Lists the operations and their order: build() → addBox()* → inflate().

14#pragma once
15
16#include <algorithm>
17#include <array>

IndexBounds and constructor

IndexBounds: inclusive min/max voxel index range. The constructor stores the voxel size.

39  void build(const pcl::PointCloud<pcl::PointXYZ>& cloud)
40  {
41    occupied_.clear();
42    occupied_.reserve(cloud.size());
43    for (const auto& point : cloud.points) {
44      occupied_.insert(encodeVoxel(toIndex(point.x), toIndex(point.y), toIndex(point.z)));
45    }
46    inflated_ = occupied_;  // until inflate() is called
47  }
48
L39–48

build(): points → voxels

Every point's voxel index is encoded into one 64-bit key and inserted into the set (duplicates vanish automatically). The inflated layer starts as a copy.

49  /// Fill a solid box (centre, full sizes, yaw about +Z in radians) with occupied
50  /// voxels.  Use it for objects whose collision shape is a known solid box.
51  /// Call BEFORE inflate() (inflate() restarts from the raw layer, which now
52  /// contains the box).
53  size_t addBox(float cx, float cy, float cz, float size_x, float size_y, float size_z, float yaw)
54  {
55    const float c = std::cos(yaw), s = std::sin(yaw);
56    const float hx = 0.5f * size_x, hy = 0.5f * size_y, hz = 0.5f * size_z;
57    // Axis-aligned bounding box of the rotated box.
58    const float ex = std::fabs(c) * hx + std::fabs(s) * hy;
59    const float ey = std::fabs(s) * hx + std::fabs(c) * hy;
60
61    size_t added = 0;
62    for (int64_t ix = toIndex(cx - ex); ix <= toIndex(cx + ex); ++ix) {
63      for (int64_t iy = toIndex(cy - ey); iy <= toIndex(cy + ey); ++iy) {
64        for (int64_t iz = toIndex(cz - hz); iz <= toIndex(cz + hz); ++iz) {
65          // Voxel centre relative to the box centre, rotated into the box frame.
66          const float dx = (ix + 0.5f) * leaf_size_ - cx;
67          const float dy = (iy + 0.5f) * leaf_size_ - cy;
68          const float dz = (iz + 0.5f) * leaf_size_ - cz;
69          const float lx = c * dx + s * dy;
70          const float ly = -s * dx + c * dy;
71          if (std::fabs(lx) <= hx && std::fabs(ly) <= hy && std::fabs(dz) <= hz) {
72            if (occupied_.insert(encodeVoxel(ix, iy, iz)).second) ++added;
73          }
74        }
75      }
76    }
77    inflated_ = occupied_;
78    return added;
79  }
80
L49–80

addBox(): fill a rack

For a box rotated by yaw: loop over the voxels of its axis-aligned bounding box, rotate each voxel centre into the box frame and keep it if it is inside the half-sizes. Returns how many new voxels were added. Racks are solid in the map because the point cloud only has their surfaces.

81  /// Grow every occupied voxel into a sphere of radius_m (3D -- also up/down).
82  /// Safe to call repeatedly with different radii (always restarts from occupied_).
83  void inflate(float radius_m)
84  {
85    inflated_ = occupied_;
86    if (radius_m <= 0.0f) return;
87
88    // Work in voxel units and precompute the sphere's offsets ONCE
89    // (the old version recomputed sqrt() for every voxel).  The small epsilon
90    // avoids float rounding dropping the voxel exactly at the radius.
91    const double r_vox = static_cast<double>(radius_m) / static_cast<double>(leaf_size_);
92    const double r2 = r_vox * r_vox + 1e-6;
93    const int R = static_cast<int>(std::floor(std::sqrt(r2)));
94
95    std::vector<std::array<int, 3>> offsets;
96    for (int dx = -R; dx <= R; ++dx)
97      for (int dy = -R; dy <= R; ++dy)
98        for (int dz = -R; dz <= R; ++dz)
99          if (dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz <= r2) offsets.push_back({dx, dy, dz});
100
101    for (const int64_t key : occupied_) {
102      int64_t ix, iy, iz;
103      decodeVoxel(key, ix, iy, iz);
104      for (const auto& o : offsets) {
105        inflated_.insert(encodeVoxel(ix + o[0], iy + o[1], iz + o[2]));
106      }
107    }
108  }
109
L81–109

inflate(): the safety margin

Converts the radius to voxels (0.30 / 0.15 = 2), precomputes once all offsets (dx, dy, dz) inside that sphere, then adds every occupied voxel + every offset to the inflated set. It is spherical and 3D, so it also covers above and below obstacles. After this the drone can be treated as a point.

110  // ---- queries in world coordinates ----------------------------------------
111  bool isOccupied(float x, float y, float z) const
112  {
113    return occupied_.count(encodeVoxel(toIndex(x), toIndex(y), toIndex(z))) > 0;
114  }
115  bool isOccupiedInflated(float x, float y, float z) const
116  {
117    return inflated_.count(encodeVoxel(toIndex(x), toIndex(y), toIndex(z))) > 0;
118  }
119
120  // ---- queries in voxel indices (used to fill the dense grid for A*) --------
121  bool isOccupiedInflatedIndex(int64_t ix, int64_t iy, int64_t iz) const
122  {
123    return inflated_.count(encodeVoxel(ix, iy, iz)) > 0;
124  }

L110–125

126  pcl::PointCloud<pcl::PointXYZ> inflatedPointCloud() const
127  {
128    pcl::PointCloud<pcl::PointXYZ> cloud;
129    cloud.points.reserve(inflated_.size());
130    for (const int64_t key : inflated_) {
131      int64_t ix, iy, iz;
132      decodeVoxel(key, ix, iy, iz);
133      cloud.points.emplace_back((static_cast<float>(ix) + 0.5f) * leaf_size_,
134                                (static_cast<float>(iy) + 0.5f) * leaf_size_,
135                                (static_cast<float>(iz) + 0.5f) * leaf_size_);
136    }
137    cloud.width = static_cast<uint32_t>(cloud.points.size());
138    cloud.height = 1;
139    cloud.is_dense = true;
140    return cloud;
141  }
142
L126–142

inflatedPointCloud()

Turns the inflated voxels back into points (voxel centres), for RViz and the saved PCD.

143  /// Index range covered by the inflated map (O(N), call once).
144  IndexBounds inflatedBounds() const
145  {
146    IndexBounds b;
147    for (const int64_t key : inflated_) {
148      int64_t ix, iy, iz;
149      decodeVoxel(key, ix, iy, iz);
150      if (!b.valid) {
151        b = {ix, iy, iz, ix, iy, iz, true};
152      } else {
153        b.min_x = std::min(b.min_x, ix); b.max_x = std::max(b.max_x, ix);
154        b.min_y = std::min(b.min_y, iy); b.max_y = std::max(b.max_y, iy);
155        b.min_z = std::min(b.min_z, iz); b.max_z = std::max(b.max_z, iz);
156      }
157    }
158    return b;
159  }
160
161  float  leafSize() const { return leaf_size_; }
162  size_t voxelCount() const { return occupied_.size(); }
163  size_t inflatedVoxelCount() const { return inflated_.size(); }

165private:

167  {

L165–170

toIndex()

World coordinate → voxel index: floor(coord / size).

171  // Pack three voxel indices into one hashable key (OFFSET keeps them positive).
172  static constexpr int64_t OFFSET = 1 << 20;
173  static constexpr int64_t MASK21 = (1 << 21) - 1;
174
175  static int64_t encodeVoxel(int64_t ix, int64_t iy, int64_t iz)
176  {
177    ix += OFFSET; iy += OFFSET; iz += OFFSET;
178    return (ix << 42) | (iy << 21) | iz;
179  }
180
181  static void decodeVoxel(int64_t key, int64_t& ix, int64_t& iy, int64_t& iz)
182  {
183    iz = (key & MASK21) - OFFSET;
184    iy = ((key >> 21) & MASK21) - OFFSET;
185    ix = (key >> 42) - OFFSET;
186  }
187
L171–187

Encoding three indices in one integer

Each index gets +220 (so negatives become positive) and 21 bits; x is shifted by 42, y by 21: key = x·2⁴² | y·2²¹ | z. Decoding masks and shifts back. One integer key is fast to hash.

188  float leaf_size_;
189  std::unordered_set<int64_t> occupied_;   // raw obstacle voxels (+ filled boxes)
190  std::unordered_set<int64_t> inflated_;   // occupied_ grown by the safety radius
191};
L188–191

Voxel size, the raw set and the inflated set.

18while (($#)); do
19  case "$1" in

21      if (($# < 2)); then usage >&2; exit 2; fi
22      RADIUS="$2"
23      RADIUS_SET=1
24      shift 2
25      ;;
26    --start)

30      shift 4
31      ;;

54if ((RADIUS_SET)) && ! [[ "$RADIUS" =~ ^[0-9]+([.][0-9]+)?$ ]]; then

Wait and request

Up to 20 s for /map_planner_node, then one ros2 topic pub --once /plan_request with start and goal. The planner keeps running for more requests until Ctrl+C.

A1.2 — indoor mission

16Mission executor node (A1.2 core)

src/lrs_psenak_valko/src/mission_executor_node.cpp · 1339 lines · 103 explained blocks

This is the program that flies the mission. It is one ROS 2 node, a C++ class MissionExecutorNode. It reads the mission file, runs a state machine 20 times a second, talks to ArduPilot through MAVROS (topics in, setpoints and service calls out) and to the A* planner (route requests and answers), and writes the flight log.

How to read it: the constructor sets everything up; the callbacks (on...) only store incoming data; tick() (every 50 ms) contains all decisions. Every state is a case in tick(), and the bigger states have a helper function (takeoffService, navigateTick, alignPathTick, taskTick).

L3–17

C++ standard library headers

  • algorithm: std::min, std::max.
  • chrono: steady_clock, used for every timeout. A steady clock never jumps when the system time is changed.
  • cmath: sqrt, atan2, sin/cos, remainder (angle wrapping), isfinite.
  • cstdint: fixed-size integers (uint64_t...).
  • filesystem: creates the log folder. fstream: writes the CSV files. iomanip: setprecision for the CSV numbers.
  • functional: std::bind for callbacks. future: the service response type (shared_future).
  • memory: shared_ptr. mutex: thread safety. stdexcept: invalid_argument. string, vector.

L18–33

ROS 2 message and service types

Each header is one generated ROS 2 type:

  • PoseArray: plan request (start + goal) and the planned route. PoseStamped: drone pose in, setpoint out. TwistStamped: velocity.
  • mavros_msgs: State (connected / armed / mode), StatusText (FCU text messages), HomePosition, EstimatorStatus; services CommandBool (arm/disarm), CommandTOL (takeoff/land), SetMode, MessageInterval.
  • nav_msgs/Path: the flown track for RViz. NavSatFix: GPS. std_msgs/String: the planner's text report.
  • rclcpp/rclcpp.hpp: the ROS 2 C++ client library itself (Node, timers, publishers...).

Load the mission (required parameter)

declare_parameter<T>(name, default) registers a ROS parameter and returns its value (from the command line -p mission_file:=..., or the default).

  • Empty path: throw, so the program stops with a clear message (caught in main).
  • mission::loadCsv parses the file; a bad line also throws, with its line number.

L45–49

Precision parameters

  • hard_radius 0.15 m: tolerance of a hard waypoint.
  • soft_radius 0.55 m: tolerance of a soft waypoint (pass through).
  • pass_radius 0.45 m: when an intermediate route point counts as passed.
  • settle_speed 0.2 m/s and settle_time 1 s: "stopped" at a hard waypoint means slower than 0.2 m/s for 1 s.

Trailing underscore (hard_radius_) = a member variable of the class (naming convention).

Telemetry and loop-rate parameters

  • pose_stale_timeout_sec 5 s: no pose for 5 s while armed = the link is lost: fail.
  • velocity_stale_timeout_sec 3 s: older velocity is not trusted for "settled".
  • local_position_rate_hz 10: the rate we ask ArduPilot to stream its local position at.
  • setpoint_rate_hz 20: control loop and setpoint rate (the assignment requires 10–20 Hz).

Start-up and ground parameters

  • handshake_timeout_sec 180 s: max time for SET_MODE and for ARM.
  • fcu_ready_timeout_sec 600 s: max time to wait for GPS/EKF in WAIT_FCU (slow PC).
  • gps_stable_sec 5 s: the GPS fix must be stable this long before GUIDED/arm.
  • ground_wait_sec 3 s: pause on the ground during landtakeoff.
  • disarm_grace_sec 5 s: after touchdown, wait this long for LAND mode to disarm itself before sending our own disarm.

Carrot, logging and exit parameters

  • cruise_speed 1 m/s: how fast the carrot setpoint slides along the route.
  • max_lead 1 m: the carrot may be at most this far ahead of the drone.
  • log_dir: folder for flight_log.csv; empty = no logging (the script sets it).
  • exit_on_done: end the program after DONE, so the script can continue.

Retry intervals and per-state timeouts

  • Retry GUIDED / arming every 2 s.
  • service_timeout_sec 5 s: a service without an answer within 5 s = fail.
  • planner_timeout_sec 10 s, navigation_timeout_sec 120 s per leg, takeoff_timeout_sec 60 s, landing_timeout_sec 90 s.
  • takeoff_climb_timeout_sec 12 s: accepted takeoff but no climb: re-send.
  • max_takeoff_attempts 3: read as int64_t (ROS integer type), clamped to ≥ 0 and cast to uint32_t.
73    yaw_tolerance_deg_ = declare_parameter<double>("yaw_tolerance_deg", 5.0);
74    yaw_hold_time_sec_ = declare_parameter<double>("yaw_hold_time_sec", 0.5);
75    yaw_timeout_sec_ = declare_parameter<double>("yaw_timeout_sec", 15.0);
76    turn_alignment_threshold_deg_ = declare_parameter<double>("turn_alignment_threshold_deg", 20.0);
77    map_to_local_yaw_offset_deg_ = declare_parameter<double>("map_to_local_yaw_offset_deg", 90.0);
L73–77

Yaw and frame parameters

  • yaw_tolerance_deg 5°, yaw_hold_time_sec 0.5 s: a heading is "reached" when within 5° for 0.5 s.
  • yaw_timeout_sec 15 s: max time for ALIGN_PATH and TASK.
  • turn_alignment_threshold_deg 20°: at a route corner sharper than this, stop and turn in place.
  • map_to_local_yaw_offset_deg 90°: the rotation between the planner's map frame and MAVROS's local frame.
78    if (hard_radius_ <= 0.0 || soft_radius_ <= hard_radius_ || pass_radius_ <= 0.0 ||
79      settle_speed_ <= 0.0 || settle_time_ <= 0.0 || setpoint_rate_hz_ < 10.0 ||
80      setpoint_rate_hz_ > 20.0 || handshake_timeout_sec_ <= 0.0 ||
81      fcu_ready_timeout_sec_ <= 0.0 || gps_stable_sec_ < 0.0 || ground_wait_sec_ < 0.0 ||
82      cruise_speed_ <= 0.0 || max_lead_ <= pass_radius_ ||
83      pose_stale_timeout_sec_ <= 1.0 || velocity_stale_timeout_sec_ <= 0.5 ||
84      local_position_rate_hz_ <= 0.0 ||
85      mode_retry_interval_sec_ <= 0.0 || arm_retry_interval_sec_ <= 0.0 ||
86      service_timeout_sec_ <= 0.0 || planner_timeout_sec_ <= 0.0 ||
87      navigation_timeout_sec_ <= 0.0 || takeoff_timeout_sec_ <= 0.0 || landing_timeout_sec_ <= 0.0 ||
88      takeoff_climb_timeout_sec_ <= 0.0 ||
89      yaw_tolerance_deg_ <= 0.0 || yaw_tolerance_deg_ >= 45.0 ||
90      yaw_hold_time_sec_ <= 0.0 || yaw_timeout_sec_ <= 0.0 ||
91      turn_alignment_threshold_deg_ <= 0.0 || turn_alignment_threshold_deg_ >= 180.0 ||
92      !std::isfinite(map_to_local_yaw_offset_deg_))
93      throw std::invalid_argument("invalid waypoint precision/controller parameters");
94
L78–94

Validate all parameters at start-up

One big if: any nonsensical value (negative time, soft tolerance smaller than hard, setpoint rate outside 10–20 Hz, max_lead not larger than pass_radius, yaw tolerance ≥ 45°...) throws and the node refuses to start. This is fail fast: bad configuration is caught on the ground, not in the air.

95    state_group_ = create_callback_group(rclcpp::CallbackGroupType::MutuallyExclusive);
96    timer_group_ = create_callback_group(rclcpp::CallbackGroupType::MutuallyExclusive);
97    rclcpp::SubscriptionOptions state_options;
98    state_options.callback_group = state_group_;
L95–98

Two callback groups

A callback group tells the executor which callbacks may run at the same time. Both are MutuallyExclusive: inside one group callbacks run one after another; two different groups may run in parallel on the MultiThreadedExecutor (see main).

  • state_group_: all subscriptions (incoming data).
  • timer_group_: the control timer.

So a burst of incoming messages never delays the 20 Hz control loop. This is the mt_executor_demo pattern the assignment asks for. SubscriptionOptions carries the group into create_subscription.

99    state_subscription_ = create_subscription<mavros_msgs::msg::State>(
100      "/mavros/state", 10,
101      std::bind(&MissionExecutorNode::onState, this, std::placeholders::_1), state_options);
L99–101

Subscribe to /mavros/state

/mavros/state says whether MAVROS is connected to the FCU, whether the drone is armed, and its mode (GUIDED, LAND...). Queue depth 10. std::bind(&Class::onState, this, _1) turns the member function into a callback; _1 is the message.

102    const rclcpp::QoS telemetry_qos = rclcpp::SensorDataQoS();
103    const rclcpp::QoS latched_qos = rclcpp::QoS(1).reliable().transient_local();
104    rclcpp::SubscriptionOptions telemetry_options;
105    telemetry_options.callback_group = state_group_;
L102–105

Quality of Service profiles

QoS decides how messages are delivered. A subscriber only receives messages when its QoS is compatible with the publisher's.

  • SensorDataQoS() = best effort, small queue: old sensor data is useless, losing one sample is fine. MAVROS publishes pose/velocity best-effort; a reliable subscriber would receive nothing. Best effort is compatible with both.
  • latched_qos = reliable + transient local: a late subscriber still gets the last message (used for the home position, sent rarely).
106    pose_subscription_ = create_subscription<geometry_msgs::msg::PoseStamped>(
107      "/mavros/local_position/pose", telemetry_qos,
108      std::bind(&MissionExecutorNode::onPose, this, std::placeholders::_1), telemetry_options);
109    velocity_subscription_ = create_subscription<geometry_msgs::msg::TwistStamped>(
110      "/mavros/local_position/velocity_local", telemetry_qos,
111      std::bind(&MissionExecutorNode::onVelocity, this, std::placeholders::_1), telemetry_options);
L106–111

Pose and velocity

FCU text messages

L137–143

Service clients

A service is a request/response call (unlike a topic, you get an answer):

  • /mavros/set_mode: switch flight mode (GUIDED).
  • /mavros/cmd/arming: arm (true) / disarm (false).
  • /mavros/cmd/takeoff, /mavros/cmd/land: MAV_CMD_NAV_TAKEOFF / NAV_LAND.
  • /mavros/set_message_interval: ask ArduPilot to send a MAVLink message at a given rate.
144    const auto timer_period = std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::nanoseconds>(
145      std::chrono::duration<double>(1.0 / setpoint_rate_hz_));
146    timer_ = create_wall_timer(timer_period, std::bind(&MissionExecutorNode::tick, this), timer_group_);

L144–147

The 20 Hz control timer

Period = 1/20 s = 50 ms, converted to nanoseconds. create_wall_timer calls tick() every period, in timer_group_. Wall timer = real time (not simulation time).
