159private:
160  enum class State
161  {
162    IDLE, WAIT_FCU, SET_MODE, ARM, TAKEOFF, PLAN, WAIT_PLAN, WAIT_PREFETCH,
163    ALIGN_PATH, NAVIGATE, TASK, LAND, WAIT_LANDED, WAIT_DISARMED, GROUND_WAIT, RETAKEOFF, DONE, ERROR
164  };
165
L159–165

The list of states

enum class State = a type-safe list of names (cannot be mixed with integers). Every value is one phase of the mission; see the "big picture" chapter for the diagram. WAIT_PREFETCH = waiting for a route that was requested in advance; GROUND_WAIT and RETAKEOFF exist for landtakeoff.

166  static const char* stateName(State state)
167  {
168    switch (state) {
169      case State::IDLE: return "IDLE";
170      case State::WAIT_FCU: return "WAIT_FCU";
171      case State::SET_MODE: return "SET_MODE";
172      case State::ARM: return "ARM";
173      case State::TAKEOFF: return "TAKEOFF";
174      case State::PLAN: return "PLAN";
175      case State::WAIT_PLAN: return "WAIT_PLAN";
176      case State::WAIT_PREFETCH: return "WAIT_PREFETCH";
177      case State::ALIGN_PATH: return "ALIGN_PATH";
178      case State::NAVIGATE: return "NAVIGATE";
179      case State::TASK: return "TASK";
180      case State::LAND: return "LAND";
181      case State::WAIT_LANDED: return "WAIT_LANDED";
182      case State::WAIT_DISARMED: return "WAIT_DISARMED";
183      case State::GROUND_WAIT: return "GROUND_WAIT";
184      case State::RETAKEOFF: return "RETAKEOFF";
185      case State::DONE: return "DONE";
186      case State::ERROR: return "ERROR";
187    }
188    return "UNKNOWN";
189  }
190
L166–190

State names for the log

Converts a state to text for log lines and the CSV. static = does not need an object. The switch covers every value; the final return "UNKNOWN" silences the compiler warning.

191  void transition(State next)
192  {
193    state_ = next;
194    state_started_at_ = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();
195    last_wait_log_at_ = state_started_at_;
196    if (next == State::TAKEOFF || next == State::RETAKEOFF) {
197      takeoff_target_set_ = false;
198      takeoff_request_sent_ = false;
199      takeoff_command_accepted_ = false;
200      next_takeoff_attempt_at_ = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();
201      takeoff_start_altitude_ = have_pose_ ? pose_.pose.position.z : 0.0;
202    }
203    if (next == State::SET_MODE) {
204      next_mode_attempt_at_ = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();
205      mode_rejection_count_ = 0;
206    }
207    if (next == State::ARM) {
208      next_arm_attempt_at_ = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();
209      arm_rejection_count_ = 0;
210    }
211    if (next == State::NAVIGATE) carrot_ = target_.pose.position;
212    if (next == State::TASK || next == State::NAVIGATE || next == State::ALIGN_PATH) {
213      resetHardArrivalSamples();
214    }
215    RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "Mission state: %s", stateName(state_));
216    service_pending_ = false;
217    ++service_request_id_;
218    stable_since_ = rclcpp::Time(0, 0, get_clock()->get_clock_type());
219  }
220
L191–220

transition(): the only way to change state

Every state change goes through here, so the bookkeeping is always done:

  • Store the new state and the time it started (for state timeouts) and reset the "still waiting" log timer.
  • TAKEOFF/RETAKEOFF: reset takeoff flags and remember the start altitude.
  • SET_MODE / ARM: allow an immediate first attempt, reset rejection counters.
  • NAVIGATE: the carrot starts at the current target.
  • TASK/NAVIGATE/ALIGN: reset "settled" counters.
  • Log Mission state: X (the timestamped log the assignment asks for).
  • service_pending_ = false; ++service_request_id_: any service answer still in flight for the previous state will be ignored (its id no longer matches).
  • Reset stable_since_ (time 0 = "not stable yet").
221  void fail(const std::string& reason)
222  {
223    RCLCPP_ERROR(get_logger(), "Mission failed in %s: %s", stateName(state_), reason.c_str());
224    if (have_pose_) {
225      target_.pose = localToMapPose(pose_.pose);
226      target_yaw_ = yawFromQuaternion(target_.pose.orientation);
227      target_initialized_ = true;
228    }
229    transition(State::ERROR);
230  }
231
L221–231

fail(): go to ERROR safely

Logs the reason with the state it happened in. If a pose is known, the target becomes the current measured position and heading. In ERROR setpoints keep being published, so the drone hovers where it is instead of continuing to an old target. Then ERROR.

232  uint64_t beginServiceCall()
233  {
234    service_pending_ = true;
235    service_started_at_ = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();
236    return ++service_request_id_;
237  }
238
239  bool finishServiceCall(uint64_t request_id)
240  {
241    if (!service_pending_ || request_id != service_request_id_ || state_ == State::ERROR) return false;
242    service_pending_ = false;
243    return true;
244  }
245
L232–245

Service call bookkeeping

beginServiceCall: mark "a call is pending", remember when (for the 5 s timeout), and return a new request id.

finishServiceCall(id) is called in the response callback. It accepts the answer only if this exact call is still the pending one and we are not in ERROR. A late answer to an old request (after a state change) is thrown away. Without this, an old "arm accepted" could act on a new state.

246  double timeoutForState() const
247  {
248    switch (state_) {
249      case State::WAIT_FCU:
250        return fcu_ready_timeout_sec_;
251      case State::SET_MODE:
252      case State::ARM:
253        return handshake_timeout_sec_;
254      case State::WAIT_PLAN:
255      case State::WAIT_PREFETCH:
256        return planner_timeout_sec_;
257      case State::NAVIGATE:
258        return navigation_timeout_sec_;
259      case State::ALIGN_PATH:
260      case State::TASK:
261        return yaw_timeout_sec_;
262      case State::TAKEOFF:
263      case State::RETAKEOFF:
264        return takeoff_timeout_sec_;
265      case State::LAND:
266      case State::WAIT_LANDED:
267      case State::WAIT_DISARMED:
268        return landing_timeout_sec_;
269      default:
270        return 0.0;
271    }
272  }
273
L246–273

Time limit of each state

Returns the maximum time the current state may last (0 = no limit). tick() fails the mission if it is exceeded. Several cases share one return. This is how "a missing response or state transition ends in a logged ERROR" is implemented.

274  void logWaiting()
275  {
276    const auto now_steady = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();
277    if (std::chrono::duration<double>(now_steady - last_wait_log_at_).count() < 5.0) return;
278    RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "Still waiting in state %s", stateName(state_));
279    last_wait_log_at_ = now_steady;
280  }
281
L274–281

Rate-limited 'still waiting' log

Prints Still waiting in state X at most every 5 s, so a waiting state does not flood the terminal at 20 Hz.

282  void onState(const mavros_msgs::msg::State::SharedPtr message)
283  {
284    std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(state_mutex_);
285    vehicle_state_ = *message;
286  }
L282–286

onState(): store the FCU state

std::lock_guard locks the mutex for the rest of the function (unlocks automatically at the closing brace, even on exceptions; this is RAII). It copies the message. All callbacks follow this pattern: lock, store, return. Decisions are made only in tick().

287  void onPose(const geometry_msgs::msg::PoseStamped::SharedPtr message)
288  {
289    std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(state_mutex_);
290    pose_ = *message;
291    have_pose_ = true;
292    pose_received_at_ = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();
293    if (!target_initialized_) {
294      target_.pose = localToMapPose(pose_.pose);
295      target_initialized_ = true;
296      target_yaw_ = yawFromQuaternion(target_.pose.orientation);
297      RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(),
298        "Coordinate frames: MAVROS local=(%.2f, %.2f, %.2f), planner map=(%.2f, %.2f, %.2f), yaw offset=%.1f deg",
299        pose_.pose.position.x, pose_.pose.position.y, pose_.pose.position.z,
300        target_.pose.position.x, target_.pose.position.y, target_.pose.position.z,
301        map_to_local_yaw_offset_deg_);
302    }
303  }
L287–303

onPose(): store the pose

  • Store the pose, mark it received, and remember when (freshness checks).
  • On the first pose: initialise the target to the current position (map frame) and heading, so a target exists before flight. Log the frame conversion; this line proves the 90° rotation (local (-7, 12.99) = map (12.99, 7)).
304  void onVelocity(const geometry_msgs::msg::TwistStamped::SharedPtr message)
305  {
306    std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(state_mutex_);
307    velocity_ = *message;
308    have_velocity_ = true;
309    velocity_received_at_ = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();
310    ++velocity_sample_sequence_;
311  }
L304–311

onVelocity()

Stores the velocity and increments velocity_sample_sequence_. The counter lets the "settled" check count different messages, instead of the same old message seen 20 times by the 20 Hz loop.

312  void onGps(const sensor_msgs::msg::NavSatFix::SharedPtr message)
313  {
314    std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(state_mutex_);
315    const bool fix = message->status.status >= sensor_msgs::msg::NavSatStatus::STATUS_FIX;
316    if (!fix) gps_fix_since_valid_ = false;
317    else if (!gps_fix_since_valid_) {
318      gps_fix_since_ = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();
319      gps_fix_since_valid_ = true;
320    }
321    gps_fix_status_ = message->status.status;
322    gps_received_at_ = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();
323    have_gps_ = true;
324  }
L312–324

onGps(): GPS fix stability

status >= STATUS_FIX means a fix. Losing the fix resets the timer; the first message with a fix starts it (gps_fix_since_). fcuReady() later requires the fix to have lasted gps_stable_sec.

325  void onHome(const mavros_msgs::msg::HomePosition::SharedPtr message)
326  {
327    std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(state_mutex_);
328    have_home_ = std::isfinite(message->geo.latitude) && std::isfinite(message->geo.longitude) &&
329      (message->geo.latitude != 0.0 || message->geo.longitude != 0.0);
330  }
L325–330

onHome()

Home is valid when latitude/longitude are finite and not (0, 0). Stored for diagnostics.

331  void onStatusText(const mavros_msgs::msg::StatusText::SharedPtr message)
332  {
333    std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(state_mutex_);
334    if (message->text.find("is using GPS") != std::string::npos && !ekf_using_gps_) {
335      ekf_using_gps_ = true;
336      RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "FCU: %s -> EKF has a GPS position", message->text.c_str());
337      return;
338    }
339    if (message->severity <= mavros_msgs::msg::StatusText::WARNING) {
340      RCLCPP_WARN(get_logger(), "FCU: %s", message->text.c_str());
341    } else {
342      RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "FCU: %s", message->text.c_str());
343    }
344  }
L331–344

onStatusText(): FCU messages

  • The first "... is using GPS" message means the EKF has an absolute position. That is the best readiness signal, so the flag ekf_using_gps_ is set.
  • Every other text is re-logged: severity ≤ WARNING (0–4: emergency... warning) as WARN, else INFO. This is how FCU: Arm: Gyros inconsistent appears in our log.
345  void onEstimatorStatus(const mavros_msgs::msg::EstimatorStatus::SharedPtr message)
346  {
347    std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(state_mutex_);
348    estimator_status_ = *message;
349    estimator_received_at_ = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();
350    have_estimator_status_ = true;
351  }
L345–351

onEstimatorStatus()

Stores the EKF status flags and their time. Kept for diagnostics; the decisions use GPS + status text instead.

352  void onPath(const geometry_msgs::msg::PoseArray::SharedPtr message)
353  {
354    std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(state_mutex_);
355    if (prefetch_request_pending_) {
356      prefetched_path_ = message->poses;
357      prefetched_path_received_ = true;
358    } else if (state_ == State::WAIT_PLAN) {
359      path_ = message->poses;
360      got_path_ = true;
361    }
362  }
L352–362

onPath(): which route is this?

The planner answers on one topic. The executor must know which request an answer belongs to:

  • if a prefetch (next leg) request is outstanding, the route is the prefetched one;
  • else, if we are in WAIT_PLAN, it is the route for the current leg;
  • otherwise it is ignored.
363  void onPlanningStatus(const std_msgs::msg::String::SharedPtr message)
364  {
365    std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(state_mutex_);
366    const bool success = message->data.find("[OK] TRASA NÁJDENÁ") != std::string::npos;
367    if (prefetch_request_pending_) {
368      prefetched_status_received_ = true;
369      prefetched_plan_ok_ = success;
370      if (!success) prefetched_plan_error_ = message->data;
371    } else if (state_ == State::WAIT_PLAN) {
372      got_status_ = true;
373      plan_ok_ = success;
374      if (!success) plan_error_ = message->data;
375    }
376  }
377
L363–377

onPlanningStatus(): success or not

The planner's report is Slovak text. Success = it contains [OK] TRASA NÁJDENÁ ("route found"). The same prefetch / WAIT_PLAN routing as onPath. On failure the whole report is kept and becomes the error message.

378  static double yawFromQuaternion(const geometry_msgs::msg::Quaternion& q)
379  {
380    return std::atan2(2.0 * (q.w * q.z + q.x * q.y),
381                      1.0 - 2.0 * (q.y * q.y + q.z * q.z));
382  }
383
L378–383

Quaternion → yaw

ROS orientations are quaternions (x, y, z, w). The yaw (rotation about z) is atan2(2(wz + xy), 1 − 2(y² + z²)), the standard ZYX Euler formula. Result in (−π, π].

384  static double unwrapNear(double reference, double angle)
385  {
386    return reference + std::remainder(angle - reference, 2.0 * M_PI);
387  }
388
L384–388

Angle unwrapping

Returns the angle equivalent to angle (same direction, ± k·360°) that is closest to reference. remainder(a, 2π) maps a difference into [−π, π]. Example: reference 170°, angle −170°: result 190°, so the drone turns 20°, not 340°. This is the "unwrap to avoid full-turn spins" from the assignment.

389  static geometry_msgs::msg::Quaternion quaternionFromYaw(double yaw)
390  {
391    geometry_msgs::msg::Quaternion q;
392    q.z = std::sin(yaw * 0.5);
393    q.w = std::cos(yaw * 0.5);
394    return q;
395  }
396
L389–396

Yaw → quaternion

A pure rotation about z by angle ψ is the quaternion (0, 0, sin ψ/2, cos ψ/2). x and y stay 0, so roll and pitch are not commanded (ArduPilot controls them itself).

397  geometry_msgs::msg::Pose localToMapPose(const geometry_msgs::msg::Pose& local_pose) const
398  {
399    const double offset = map_to_local_yaw_offset_deg_ * M_PI / 180.0;
400    const double c = std::cos(offset), s = std::sin(offset);
401    geometry_msgs::msg::Pose map_pose = local_pose;
402    map_pose.position.x = c * local_pose.position.x + s * local_pose.position.y;
403    map_pose.position.y = -s * local_pose.position.x + c * local_pose.position.y;
404    map_pose.orientation = quaternionFromYaw(yawFromQuaternion(local_pose.orientation) - offset);
405    return map_pose;
406  }
407
L397–407

MAVROS local → map

Rotates a pose by −offset (offset = 90°) about z: x_m = c·x_l + s·y_l, y_m = −s·x_l + c·y_l, yaw − offset. z is unchanged. With 90°: x_m = y_l (north), y_m = −x_l (west). Every pose that comes from MAVROS passes through here, so the rest of the code works only in the map frame (the frame of the planner and mission file).

408  geometry_msgs::msg::Pose mapToLocalPose(const geometry_msgs::msg::Pose& map_pose) const
409  {
410    const double offset = map_to_local_yaw_offset_deg_ * M_PI / 180.0;
411    const double c = std::cos(offset), s = std::sin(offset);
412    geometry_msgs::msg::Pose local_pose = map_pose;
413    local_pose.position.x = c * map_pose.position.x - s * map_pose.position.y;
414    local_pose.position.y = s * map_pose.position.x + c * map_pose.position.y;
415    local_pose.orientation = quaternionFromYaw(yawFromQuaternion(map_pose.orientation) + offset);
416    return local_pose;
417  }
418
L408–418

map → MAVROS local

The inverse rotation (+offset). Used once: on the way out, in publishSetpoint().

419  double currentMapYaw() const
420  {
421    return yawFromQuaternion(pose_.pose.orientation) - map_to_local_yaw_offset_deg_ * M_PI / 180.0;
422  }
423
L419–423

Current heading in the map frame

Measured yaw from MAVROS minus the 90° offset.

424  double distanceTo(const mission::Waypoint& point) const
425  {
426    const geometry_msgs::msg::Pose map_pose = localToMapPose(pose_.pose);
427    const double dx = map_pose.position.x - point.x;
428    const double dy = map_pose.position.y - point.y;
429    const double dz = map_pose.position.z - point.z;
430    return std::sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz);
431  }
432
L424–432

3D distance to a waypoint

Converts the current pose to map and returns the Euclidean distance √(dx²+dy²+dz²) to the waypoint. Used for the hard/soft arrival check.

433  double speed() const
434  {
435    const auto& v = velocity_.twist.linear;
436    return std::sqrt(v.x * v.x + v.y * v.y + v.z * v.z);
437  }
438
L433–438

Speed

Magnitude of the velocity vector (m/s).

439  bool velocityFresh() const
440  {
441    return have_velocity_ && std::chrono::duration<double>(
442      std::chrono::steady_clock::now() - velocity_received_at_).count() <= velocity_stale_timeout_sec_;
443  }
444
445  bool poseFresh() const
446  {
447    return have_pose_ && std::chrono::duration<double>(
448      std::chrono::steady_clock::now() - pose_received_at_).count() <= pose_stale_timeout_sec_;
449  }

L439–451

Freshness checks

Data counts as fresh if it was received and is younger than its stale timeout. Old data must never confirm "settled" or "landed".

452  // Arming readiness: the EKF announced it uses GPS, or (when the executor was started after that
453  // message) the GPS fix has been stable for gps_stable_sec.  Without this, ArduCopter refuses
454  // GUIDED ("requires position") and arming, and the mission would burn its handshake timeout.
455  bool fcuReady(std::string* why) const
456  {
457    if (!vehicle_state_.connected) { *why = "FCU not connected"; return false; }
458    if (!poseFresh()) { *why = "no fresh local position"; return false; }
459    if (ekf_using_gps_) return true;
460    const bool gps_fresh = have_gps_ && std::chrono::duration<double>(
461      std::chrono::steady_clock::now() - gps_received_at_).count() <= 2.0;
462    if (!gps_fresh || !gps_fix_since_valid_) { *why = "no GPS fix yet"; return false; }
463    const double stable = std::chrono::duration<double>(
464      std::chrono::steady_clock::now() - gps_fix_since_).count();
465    if (stable < gps_stable_sec_) { *why = "GPS fix not stable yet"; return false; }
466    return true;
467  }
468
L452–468

fcuReady(): may we try GUIDED and arming?

Checked in WAIT_FCU, in this order. The first failing check is reported in why (the log shows the reason every 5 s):

  1. MAVROS connected to the FCU;
  2. a fresh local pose;
  3. EKF said "is using GPS": ready; or else
  4. a fresh GPS message with a fix, stable for gps_stable_sec.

The fallback (4) exists because the "using GPS" text is sent only once: an executor started later would never see it. In the tests the GPS fix came first; arming then failed for ~25 s with "Gyros inconsistent" and was retried until it succeeded.

469  void resetHardArrivalSamples()
470  {
471    stable_velocity_samples_ = 0;
472    last_stable_velocity_sequence_ = 0;
473    stable_since_ = rclcpp::Time(0, 0, get_clock()->get_clock_type());
474  }
475
L469–475

Reset the 'settled' detector

Zero stable samples, and time 0 means "not stable". Called whenever the drone is not (or no longer) within tolerance and slow.

476  // ArduCopter's GUIDED mode drops a running takeoff as soon as it receives a position setpoint
477  // (ModeGuided::set_destination switches the submode to Pos), and the Pos controller does nothing
478  // while the vehicle is still landed: rotors idle, no climb, then DISARM_DELAY disarms it.
479  // So no setpoints are streamed while arming/taking off/landing, only during flight (and in ERROR,
480  // where the target is the current pose and acts as a hold).
481  bool setpointsAllowed() const
482  {
483    switch (state_) {
484      case State::PLAN:
485      case State::WAIT_PLAN:
486      case State::WAIT_PREFETCH:
487      case State::ALIGN_PATH:
488      case State::NAVIGATE:
489      case State::TASK:
490      case State::ERROR:
491        return true;
492      default:
493        return false;
494    }
495  }
496
L476–496

setpointsAllowed(): the takeoff bug fix

Important for the exam. Setpoints are published only in flight states (PLAN … TASK) and in ERROR (hold). Not during SET_MODE/ARM/TAKEOFF/LAND.

Why: in GUIDED mode, a position target cancels ArduCopter's takeoff (the guided sub-mode switches from Takeoff to Position). Position control does not spin the motors up while landed, so the copter sits on the ground and auto-disarms after DISARM_DELAY. That was exactly the original symptom "arms, then disarms, never takes off".

497  void publishSetpoint()
498  {
499    if (!have_pose_ || !target_initialized_ || !setpointsAllowed()) return;
500    target_.header.stamp = now();
501    target_.header.frame_id = "map";
502    geometry_msgs::msg::PoseStamped local_target = target_;
503    local_target.pose = mapToLocalPose(target_.pose);
504    local_target.pose.orientation = quaternionFromYaw(target_yaw_ + map_to_local_yaw_offset_deg_ * M_PI / 180.0);
505    setpoint_publisher_->publish(local_target);
506  }
507
L497–507

publishSetpoint(): send the target to MAVROS

  • Nothing to send without a pose or target, or when setpoints are not allowed.
  • Stamp with the current time, frame "map" (MAVROS ignores frame_id for this topic and treats it as its local frame).
  • Convert the target position map → local; build the orientation from target_yaw_ + 90°.
  • Publish. Called at 20 Hz at the start of every tick().
508  void setTarget(const geometry_msgs::msg::Pose& pose, double yaw)
509  {
510    target_.pose = pose;
511    target_yaw_ = unwrapNear(target_yaw_, yaw);
512  }
513
L508–513

setTarget()

Sets the target pose and the yaw, unwrapped near the previous yaw, so the commanded yaw never jumps by 360°.

514  void requestPlan()
515  {
516    if (!have_pose_) return;
517    const auto& goal = mission_[waypoint_index_];
518    geometry_msgs::msg::PoseArray request;
519    request.header.frame_id = "map";
520    request.header.stamp = now();
521    geometry_msgs::msg::Pose start = localToMapPose(pose_.pose);
522    geometry_msgs::msg::Pose finish;
523    finish.position.x = goal.x;
524    finish.position.y = goal.y;
525    finish.position.z = goal.z;
526    finish.orientation.w = 1.0;
527    request.poses = {start, finish};
528    got_path_ = false;
529    got_status_ = false;
530    plan_ok_ = false;
531    plan_error_.clear();
532    prefetch_request_pending_ = false;
533    transition(State::WAIT_PLAN);
534    plan_publisher_->publish(request);
535  }
536
L514–536

requestPlan(): ask A* for the current leg

  • Start = current pose (map frame); goal = the current mission waypoint (orientation.w = 1 = no rotation, a valid quaternion).
  • Clear the old answer flags, mark "not a prefetch".
  • Transition first, publish second: we must already be in WAIT_PLAN when the answer arrives, or onPath would ignore it.
537  void requestPrefetchPlan()
538  {
539    if (waypoint_index_ + 1 >= mission_.size()) return;
540    const auto& start_waypoint = mission_[waypoint_index_];
541    const auto& goal = mission_[waypoint_index_ + 1];
542    geometry_msgs::msg::PoseArray request;
543    request.header.frame_id = "map";
544    request.header.stamp = now();
545    geometry_msgs::msg::Pose start;
546    start.position.x = start_waypoint.x;
547    start.position.y = start_waypoint.y;
548    start.position.z = start_waypoint.z;
549    start.orientation.w = 1.0;
550    geometry_msgs::msg::Pose finish;
551    finish.position.x = goal.x;
552    finish.position.y = goal.y;
553    finish.position.z = goal.z;
554    finish.orientation.w = 1.0;
555    request.poses = {start, finish};
556
557    prefetched_waypoint_index_ = waypoint_index_ + 1;
558    prefetched_path_.clear();
559    prefetched_path_received_ = false;
560    prefetched_status_received_ = false;
561    prefetched_plan_ok_ = false;
562    prefetched_plan_error_.clear();
563    prefetch_request_pending_ = true;
564    plan_publisher_->publish(request);
565  }
566
L537–566

requestPrefetchPlan(): plan the next leg in advance

While the drone flies to waypoint i, ask for the route i → i+1, starting at the waypoint position (where the drone will be). When the drone arrives, the route is usually ready and there is no hovering while A* runs. Returns immediately at the last waypoint. Remembers which waypoint the prefetch is for.

567  bool activatePrefetchedPath()
568  {
569    if (!prefetched_status_received_) return false;
570    if (prefetched_waypoint_index_ != waypoint_index_) {
571      fail("prefetched route does not match the current mission waypoint");
572      return false;
573    }
574    if (!prefetched_plan_ok_) {
575      fail("planner failed for next waypoint: " + prefetched_plan_error_);
576      return false;
577    }
578    if (!prefetched_path_received_) return false;
579    if (prefetched_path_.empty()) {
580      fail("planner returned an empty prefetched path");
581      return false;
582    }
583    const auto& goal = mission_[waypoint_index_];
584    const auto& endpoint = prefetched_path_.back().position;
585    const double dx = endpoint.x - goal.x;
586    const double dy = endpoint.y - goal.y;
587    const double dz = endpoint.z - goal.z;
588    const double endpoint_error = std::sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz);
589    const double acceptance = goal.precision == mission::Precision::HARD
590      ? hard_radius_ : soft_radius_;
591    if (endpoint_error > acceptance) {
592      fail("prefetched route endpoint is " + std::to_string(endpoint_error) +
593        " m from the mission goal, outside its " + std::string(mission::toString(goal.precision)) +
594        " tolerance");
595      return false;
596    }
597    path_ = std::move(prefetched_path_);
598    prefetch_request_pending_ = false;
599    beginPathNavigation();
600    return true;
601  }
602
L567–602

activatePrefetchedPath(): accept the prepared route

Called in WAIT_PREFETCH. Returns false = "still waiting".

  • No status yet: wait.
  • Prefetch is for another waypoint: fail (should never happen; a safety check).
  • Planner failed: fail with its report. Route empty: fail.
  • Endpoint check: if the planner moved ("snapped") the goal further than the waypoint's tolerance (0.15 hard / 0.55 soft), fail. Flying there would not reach the mission point.
  • OK: take the route (std::move, no copy) and start navigating.
603  void beginPathNavigation()
604  {
605    logPlannedPath();
606    if (path_.size() > 1) {
607      path_index_ = 1;
608      setTarget(localToMapPose(pose_.pose), segmentYaw(path_index_));
609      transition(State::ALIGN_PATH);
610    } else {
611      path_index_ = 0;
612      setTarget(path_.back(), target_yaw_);
613      transition(State::NAVIGATE);
614    }
615    requestPrefetchPlan();
616  }
617
L603–617

beginPathNavigation(): start flying a route

  • Log the route to planned_paths.csv.
  • Route with ≥ 2 points: target = hold the current position, yaw = direction of the first segment, then ALIGN_PATH (turn first, then fly).
  • One point: fly straight to it.
  • Immediately prefetch the next leg.
618  // ------------------------------------------------------------------ flight logging
619  // <log_dir>/flight_log.csv: one row per control tick (20 Hz), map frame, read by plot_a1_2.py.
620  // <log_dir>/planned_paths.csv: every planner route that was flown, one row per path point.
621  void openLogs()
622  {
623    if (log_dir_.empty()) return;
624    std::error_code error;
625    std::filesystem::create_directories(log_dir_, error);
626    flight_log_.open(log_dir_ + "/flight_log.csv");
627    path_log_.open(log_dir_ + "/planned_paths.csv");
628    if (!flight_log_ || !path_log_) {
629      RCLCPP_WARN(get_logger(), "Cannot write logs to %s; flight logging disabled", log_dir_.c_str());
630      flight_log_.close();
631      path_log_.close();
632      return;
633    }
634    flight_log_ << "t_s,state,waypoint,armed,mode,cmd_valid,cmd_x,cmd_y,cmd_z,cmd_yaw_deg,"
635                   "x,y,z,yaw_deg,speed\n";
636    path_log_ << "leg,waypoint,point,x,y,z\n";
637    log_started_at_ = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();
638    RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "Logging flight data to %s", log_dir_.c_str());
639  }
640
L618–640

openLogs(): create the CSV files

Creates the folder (create_directories, error code instead of exception), opens both files, writes the CSV headers. If they cannot be opened, logging is disabled with a warning; the flight itself must not fail because of logging. log_started_at_ is time 0 of the log.

641  void logSample()
642  {
643    const auto steady_now = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();
644    if (have_pose_ && std::chrono::duration<double>(steady_now - last_flown_publish_at_).count() >= 0.2 &&
645      vehicle_state_.armed)
646    {
647      last_flown_publish_at_ = steady_now;
648      geometry_msgs::msg::PoseStamped sample;
649      sample.header.frame_id = "map";
650      sample.header.stamp = now();
651      sample.pose = localToMapPose(pose_.pose);
652      flown_path_.header = sample.header;
653      if (flown_path_.poses.size() < 20000) flown_path_.poses.push_back(sample);
654      flown_path_publisher_->publish(flown_path_);
655    }
L641–655

logSample(): the flown track for RViz

Every 0.2 s while armed, append the current map-frame pose to a nav_msgs/Path and publish it (RViz shows it as a line). Capped at 20 000 poses so memory cannot grow without limit.

656    if (!flight_log_ || !have_pose_) return;
657    const geometry_msgs::msg::Pose map_pose = localToMapPose(pose_.pose);
658    const bool cmd_valid = target_initialized_ && setpointsAllowed();
659    const auto deg = [](double rad) { return std::remainder(rad, 2.0 * M_PI) * 180.0 / M_PI; };
660    flight_log_ << std::fixed << std::setprecision(3)
661      << std::chrono::duration<double>(steady_now - log_started_at_).count() << ','
662      << stateName(state_) << ',' << waypoint_index_ + 1 << ','
663      << (vehicle_state_.armed ? 1 : 0) << ',' << vehicle_state_.mode << ','
664      << (cmd_valid ? 1 : 0) << ','
665      << target_.pose.position.x << ',' << target_.pose.position.y << ',' << target_.pose.position.z << ','
666      << deg(target_yaw_) << ','
667      << map_pose.position.x << ',' << map_pose.position.y << ',' << map_pose.position.z << ','
668      << deg(currentMapYaw()) << ',' << (have_velocity_ ? speed() : 0.0) << '\n';
669    if (std::chrono::duration<double>(steady_now - last_log_flush_at_).count() >= 1.0) {
670      flight_log_.flush();
671      last_log_flush_at_ = steady_now;
672    }
673  }
674
L656–674

One CSV row per tick

Writes t, state, waypoint, armed, mode, cmd_valid, cmd x/y/z/yaw, x/y/z/yaw, speed. cmd_valid = whether a setpoint was really being sent; this is why the plots have gaps during takeoff/landing. deg() wraps to ±180° and converts to degrees. The file is flushed every second, so a crash loses at most 1 s.

675  void logPlannedPath()
676  {
677    ++leg_counter_;
678    if (!path_log_) return;
679    path_log_ << std::fixed << std::setprecision(3);
680    for (size_t i = 0; i < path_.size(); ++i) {
681      const auto& p = path_[i].position;
682      path_log_ << leg_counter_ << ',' << waypoint_index_ + 1 << ',' << i << ','
683        << p.x << ',' << p.y << ',' << p.z << '\n';
684    }
685    path_log_.flush();
686  }
687
L675–687

logPlannedPath()

Writes every point of the route being started (leg number, waypoint, point index, x, y, z) and flushes immediately. Read by the plot and 3D scripts.

688  void tick()
689  {
690    std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(state_mutex_);
691    publishSetpoint();
692    logSample();
693    if (state_ == State::DONE && exit_on_done_ && !shutdown_requested_ &&
694      std::chrono::duration<double>(std::chrono::steady_clock::now() - state_started_at_).count() > 1.0)
695    {
696      shutdown_requested_ = true;
697      if (flight_log_) flight_log_.flush();
698      if (path_log_) path_log_.flush();
699      RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "Mission DONE; executor exits (exit_on_done=true)");
700      rclcpp::shutdown();
701      return;
702    }
L688–702

tick(): start of the 20 Hz loop

  • Lock the mutex for the whole tick: callbacks cannot change data in the middle of a decision.
  • Publish the setpoint and log a sample, in every state, before any early return (so a hold keeps being sent even in ERROR).
  • One second after DONE: flush the logs and call rclcpp::shutdown(). The executor's spin() returns and the program exits; the run script then continues with plotting.
703    if (state_ == State::DONE || state_ == State::ERROR) return;
704    const auto steady_now = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();
705    if (service_pending_ &&
706      std::chrono::duration<double>(steady_now - service_started_at_).count() > service_timeout_sec_)
707    {
708      fail("MAVROS service response timed out in " + std::string(stateName(state_)));
709      return;
710    }
L703–710

Global checks 1: finished states, service timeout

DONE and ERROR make no further decisions. A service call pending longer than service_timeout_sec means MAVROS/the FCU did not answer: fail.

711    const double state_timeout = timeoutForState();
712    if (state_timeout > 0.0 &&
713      std::chrono::duration<double>(steady_now - state_started_at_).count() > state_timeout)
714    {
715      if (state_ == State::ARM) {
716        fail("arming timed out after " + std::to_string(state_timeout) + " s, rejected attempts=" +
717          std::to_string(arm_rejection_count_) + "; inspect SITL pre-arm messages");
718      } else {
719        fail("state timed out after " + std::to_string(state_timeout) + " s");
720      }
721      return;
722    }
L711–722

Global checks 2: state timeout

If the current state has lasted longer than its limit, fail. For ARM the message includes how many attempts were rejected and points to the PreArm messages.

723    if (vehicle_state_.armed && have_pose_ &&

725    {

L723–728

Global checks 3: lost telemetry

Armed, but no pose for pose_stale_timeout_sec: the link is lost (in our tests: Gazebo paused, so SITL froze). Fail with a hint. Only while armed, because short gaps while disarmed (pre-arm) are harmless.

729    const bool requires_guided_flight = state_ == State::TAKEOFF || state_ == State::RETAKEOFF ||
730      state_ == State::PLAN || state_ == State::WAIT_PLAN ||
731      state_ == State::WAIT_PREFETCH || state_ == State::ALIGN_PATH || state_ == State::NAVIGATE ||
732      state_ == State::TASK;

734      fail("MAVROS FCU connection was lost during the mission");
735      return;

L729–736

Global checks 4: FCU link in flight

The list of states in which the copter must be connected, armed and (mostly) in GUIDED. If the MAVROS link drops in any of them: fail.

737    if (requires_guided_flight && !vehicle_state_.armed) {
738      // ArduCopter auto-disarms (DISARM_DELAY) when it is armed but never leaves the ground,
739      // e.g. when the EKF was not ready at arming time.  Retry mode/arm/takeoff a few times.
740      const bool on_ground = !have_pose_ || pose_.pose.position.z < takeoff_start_altitude_ + 0.3;
741      if ((state_ == State::TAKEOFF || state_ == State::RETAKEOFF) && on_ground &&
742        takeoff_disarm_retries_ < max_takeoff_attempts_)
743      {
744        ++takeoff_disarm_retries_;
745        RCLCPP_WARN(get_logger(),
746          "Vehicle disarmed before leaving the ground (attempt %u/%u); retrying GUIDED, arm and takeoff",
747          takeoff_disarm_retries_, max_takeoff_attempts_);
748        transition(State::SET_MODE);
749        return;
750      }
751      fail("vehicle disarmed unexpectedly during the mission");
752      return;
753    }
L737–753

Global checks 5: unexpected disarm

In a flight state but not armed:

  • During TAKEOFF/RETAKEOFF, still on the ground (z < start + 0.3 m), and retries left: ArduCopter auto-disarmed before lifting off. Retry the whole GUIDED → arm → takeoff sequence (max 3×).
  • Anything else (e.g. disarmed in the air): fail.
754    if ((state_ == State::TAKEOFF || state_ == State::RETAKEOFF || state_ == State::ALIGN_PATH ||
755      state_ == State::NAVIGATE || state_ == State::TASK) &&
756      vehicle_state_.mode != "GUIDED")
757    {
758      fail("vehicle left GUIDED mode during waypoint execution");
759      return;
760    }
761    if (state_ != State::IDLE && state_ != State::WAIT_FCU && !have_pose_) return;
762
L754–762

Global checks 6: still in GUIDED?

If the mode changed away from GUIDED during takeoff/navigation (e.g. RC override, failsafe RTL/LAND), stop: we must not fight the pilot or a failsafe. Then: past WAIT_FCU, nothing can be decided without a pose, so wait.

L763–766

The state switch: IDLE

Each case below is one state. IDLE only moves on to WAIT_FCU (it exists so the log shows the start).

767      case State::WAIT_FCU: {
768        std::string why;
769        if (fcuReady(&why)) {
770          if (!position_rate_requested_ && message_interval_client_->service_is_ready()) {
771            position_rate_requested_ = true;
772            auto request = std::make_shared<mavros_msgs::srv::MessageInterval::Request>();
773            request->message_id = 32;
774            request->message_rate = static_cast<float>(local_position_rate_hz_);

777              const auto response = future.get();

779                RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "Requested LOCAL_POSITION_NED at %.1f Hz",
780                  local_position_rate_hz_);
781              }
782            });
783          }
784          RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "FCU ready (%s)",
785            ekf_using_gps_ ? "EKF is using GPS" : "GPS fix stable");

787        } else if (std::chrono::duration<double>(steady_now - last_wait_log_at_).count() >= 5.0) {
788          RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "Waiting for FCU readiness: %s", why.c_str());
789          last_wait_log_at_ = steady_now;

L767–792

WAIT_FCU

  • If fcuReady(): once, ask ArduPilot to send LOCAL_POSITION_NED (MAVLink message id 32) at 10 Hz, so pose updates are frequent enough for settling. The response callback just logs.
  • Log why it is ready, then SET_MODE.
  • Else log the reason every 5 s.
  • Braces { } around the case: needed because a variable (why) is declared inside it.
793      case State::SET_MODE:
794        if (vehicle_state_.mode == "GUIDED") {
795          transition(State::ARM);
796          break;
797        }
798        if (!service_pending_ && mode_client_->service_is_ready() &&
799          std::chrono::steady_clock::now() >= next_mode_attempt_at_) {
800          const uint64_t request_id = beginServiceCall();
801          auto request = std::make_shared<mavros_msgs::srv::SetMode::Request>();
802          request->custom_mode = "GUIDED";
803          mode_client_->async_send_request(request, [this, request_id](
804              std::shared_future<mavros_msgs::srv::SetMode::Response::SharedPtr> future) {
805            std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(state_mutex_);
806            if (!finishServiceCall(request_id)) return;
807            const auto response = future.get();
808            if (!response || !response->mode_sent) {
809              ++mode_rejection_count_;
810              next_mode_attempt_at_ = std::chrono::steady_clock::now() +
811                std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::steady_clock::duration>(
812                  std::chrono::duration<double>(mode_retry_interval_sec_));
813              RCLCPP_WARN(get_logger(),
814                "Set GUIDED mode attempt %u rejected; retrying in %.1f s",
815                mode_rejection_count_, mode_retry_interval_sec_);
816            } else {
817              // mode_sent only means MAVROS sent it; the FCU may still refuse (e.g. no position
818              // yet), so give /mavros/state time to report GUIDED before resending.
819              next_mode_attempt_at_ = std::chrono::steady_clock::now() +
820                std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::steady_clock::duration>(
821                  std::chrono::duration<double>(mode_retry_interval_sec_));
822              RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "GUIDED mode request sent to FCU");
823            }
824          });
825        } else logWaiting();
826        break;
L793–826

SET_MODE: request GUIDED

  • Already GUIDED (reported by /mavros/state): go to ARM.
  • Otherwise, if no call is pending, the service exists and the retry time has come: call set_mode(custom_mode="GUIDED") asynchronously.
  • The lambda [this, request_id](future){...} runs when the answer arrives; it locks the mutex and checks the request id.
  • mode_sent == false: rejected by MAVROS, retry in 2 s. mode_sent == true only means "sent": the FCU may still refuse (e.g. no position yet), so wait 2 s for /mavros/state before sending again. Earlier the code re-sent every 50 ms.

831        }

852              RCLCPP_WARN(get_logger(),

857          });

L827–859

ARM: arm the motors

  • Armed: go to TAKEOFF, or RETAKEOFF during a landtakeoff.
  • No fresh pose: wait.
  • Call cmd/arming(value=true). If refused (success == false), count it, log the MAVLink result code (4 = FAILED), retry in 2 s. The reason itself arrives as an FCU text (e.g. "Arm: Gyros inconsistent") and is logged by onStatusText.

TAKEOFF and PLAN

866      case State::WAIT_PLAN:
867        if (got_status_ && !plan_ok_) { fail("planner failed: " + plan_error_); break; }
868        if (got_status_ && got_path_) {
869          if (path_.empty()) { fail("planner returned an empty path"); break; }

WAIT_PLAN: check the answer

  • Status says failure: fail with the planner's report.
  • Status and route both arrived: reject an empty route; reject a route whose end is further from the goal than the waypoint tolerance (goal snapped too far); otherwise start the route.

LAND: command landing

Call cmd/land once (yaw = current target yaw). ArduCopter switches to LAND mode and descends by itself. Accepted: WAIT_LANDED; rejected: fail. No setpoints are sent now.

WAIT_LANDED: detect touchdown

On the ground = already disarmed, or z ≤ 0.15 m with speed ≤ 0.15 m/s from fresh data. Then set the earliest time for our own disarm (now + disarm_grace_sec) and go to WAIT_DISARMED.

926      case State::WAIT_DISARMED:
927        // ArduCopter's LAND mode disarms by itself once its own land detector agrees (about a
928        // second after touchdown).  Before that, a disarm command is refused, so it is only sent
929        // as a fallback after disarm_grace_sec, and a refusal is retried rather than fatal.
930        if (vehicle_state_.armed) {

L926–941

WAIT_DISARMED: the landing bug fix

Important for the exam. ArduCopter's LAND mode disarms by itself once its own land detector agrees, about a second after touchdown. A disarm command before that is refused. The old code treated that refusal as fatal, and the mission died at the landtakeoff waypoint. Now:

  • still armed: after the grace time, send a disarm (refusal = retry in 2 s);
  • disarmed: landtakeoff: GROUND_WAIT; final land: log the task as complete, then DONE.

947        }
948        break;
L942–948

GROUND_WAIT

Stay on the ground for ground_wait_sec ("land, wait on the ground" from the assignment), then SET_MODE. The copter is in LAND mode and must be put back into GUIDED before it can be armed and take off again.

RETAKEOFF, DONE, ERROR

RETAKEOFF: take off to this waypoint's altitude; when done, clear the landtakeoff flag, log the task, and continue with the next waypoint. DONE/ERROR: nothing (handled at the top of tick()).

962  bool takeoffService(double altitude)
963  {
964    if (!takeoff_target_set_ && have_pose_) {
965      geometry_msgs::msg::Pose takeoff_target = localToMapPose(pose_.pose);
966      takeoff_target.position.z = altitude;
967      setTarget(takeoff_target, target_yaw_);
968      takeoff_target_set_ = true;
969      RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(),
970        "Takeoff setpoint: holding map x=%.2f y=%.2f and commanding z=%.2f m",
971        takeoff_target.position.x, takeoff_target.position.y, altitude);
972    }
973    if (!takeoff_target_set_) {
974      logWaiting();
975      return true;
976    }

takeoffService() part 1: takeoff target

Returns true = "still taking off", false = done. First call: remember a target above the current x/y at the requested altitude (used as the hold target after takeoff; it is not sent during takeoff).

L978–1011

takeoffService() part 2: NAV_TAKEOFF

  • Send cmd/takeoff(altitude) once (unless a call is pending or a retry time is set). ArduPilot interprets the altitude relative to home (the floor).
  • Rejected: allow a retry in 2 s. Accepted: remember when (for the "no climb" check).
  • Not accepted yet: return "still taking off".
1012    const double confirmed_altitude = std::max(takeoff_start_altitude_ + 0.5, altitude - hard_radius_);
1013    if (!vehicle_state_.armed || pose_.pose.position.z < confirmed_altitude) {
1014      const auto steady_now = std::chrono::steady_clock::now();
1015      if (vehicle_state_.armed && pose_.pose.position.z < takeoff_start_altitude_ + 0.3 &&
1016        std::chrono::duration<double>(steady_now - takeoff_accepted_at_).count() >
1017          takeoff_climb_timeout_sec_)
1018      {
1019        if (++takeoff_resends_ > max_takeoff_attempts_) {
1020          fail("vehicle accepted NAV_TAKEOFF but did not climb; check EKF/GPS and SITL messages");
1021          return true;
1022        }
1023        RCLCPP_WARN(get_logger(), "No climb %.0f s after NAV_TAKEOFF; re-sending (attempt %u/%u)",
1024          takeoff_climb_timeout_sec_, takeoff_resends_, max_takeoff_attempts_);
1025        takeoff_command_accepted_ = false;
1026        takeoff_request_sent_ = false;
1027        next_takeoff_attempt_at_ = steady_now;
1028        return true;
1029      }
1030      if (std::chrono::duration<double>(steady_now - last_wait_log_at_).count() >= 5.0) {
1031        RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(),
1032          "Takeoff progress: armed=%s local_z=%.2f m target_z=%.2f m; waiting for climb",
1033          vehicle_state_.armed ? "true" : "false", pose_.pose.position.z, altitude);

1035      }
1036      return true;
1037    }

takeoffService() part 3: wait for the climb

Takeoff counts as complete at z ≥ max(start + 0.5 m, target − 0.15 m). While below:

  • armed, still on the ground, and more than takeoff_climb_timeout_sec since the accepted command: re-send the takeoff (max 3×, then fail);
  • log progress every 5 s.

L1045–1053

navigateTick() part 1: carrot target

While there are route points left: the current route point is the "vertex". Advance the carrot towards it; the target is the carrot position, with yaw = direction of the current segment. The drone always faces the direction of travel.

1054      const geometry_msgs::msg::Pose map_pose = localToMapPose(pose_.pose);
1055      const double dx = map_pose.position.x - pose.position.x;
1056      const double dy = map_pose.position.y - pose.position.y;
1057      const double dz = map_pose.position.z - pose.position.z;
1058      if (carrot_at_vertex && std::sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz) <= pass_radius_) {
1059        const size_t reached_index = path_index_;
1060        ++path_index_;
1061        if (path_index_ < path_.size()) {
1062          const double incoming_yaw = segmentYaw(reached_index);
1063          const double outgoing_yaw = segmentYaw(path_index_);
1064          const double turn = std::abs(std::remainder(outgoing_yaw - incoming_yaw, 2.0 * M_PI));
1065          if (turn >= turn_alignment_threshold_deg_ * M_PI / 180.0) {
1066            setTarget(path_[reached_index], outgoing_yaw);
1067            transition(State::ALIGN_PATH);
1068          }
1069        }
1070      }
1071      return;
1072    }

L1054–1073

navigateTick() part 2: passing a route point

A route point is passed when the carrot has reached it and the drone is within pass_radius (0.45 m). Intermediate points are pass-through, no stopping. Then the next point. If the new segment turns more than 20° from the old one, hold at the corner and turn in ALIGN_PATH first; gentle turns are flown without stopping.

1074    setTarget(path_.back(), target_yaw_);
1075    const double radius = goal.precision == mission::Precision::HARD ? hard_radius_ : soft_radius_;
1076    if (distanceTo(goal) > radius) { resetHardArrivalSamples(); return; }
1077    if (goal.precision == mission::Precision::SOFT) {
1078      RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "Reached soft waypoint %zu: error=%.3f m; continuing without settling",
1079        waypoint_index_ + 1, distanceTo(goal));
1080      transition(State::TASK);
1081      return;
1082    }
L1074–1082

navigateTick() part 3: arriving at the waypoint

All route points passed; the target is the final point (the waypoint). Outside the tolerance radius: keep flying, reset "settled". Soft waypoint inside 0.55 m: done immediately, no stop (go to TASK).

1083    if (!velocityFresh()) {
1084      resetHardArrivalSamples();
1085      return;
1086    }
1087    if (speed() > settle_speed_) { resetHardArrivalSamples(); return; }
1088    if (velocity_sample_sequence_ != last_stable_velocity_sequence_) {
1089      if (stable_velocity_samples_ == 0) stable_since_ = now();
1090      ++stable_velocity_samples_;

L1083–1099

navigateTick() part 4: settling at a hard waypoint

Hard waypoint inside 0.15 m: additionally the drone must be slow (≤ 0.2 m/s) on fresh data. Each new velocity message that is slow increments the counter (the first one starts the clock). At least 2 messages and ≥ settle_time (1 s) of continuous slowness: "arrived and stable", go to TASK. Any fast or stale sample resets it.

1100  // Carrot following: the setpoint slides along the planned segment at cruise_speed and never runs
1101  // more than max_lead ahead of the drone.  Jumping the setpoint straight to the segment end lets
1102  // ArduCopter mix its horizontal and vertical speed limits, so it leaves the straight line (e.g.
1103  // descends early) and can clip an obstacle the A* route went over.  Returns true at the vertex.
1104  bool advanceCarrot(const geometry_msgs::msg::Point& vertex)
1105  {
1106    const auto drone = localToMapPose(pose_.pose).position;
1107    const auto dist = [](const geometry_msgs::msg::Point& a, const geometry_msgs::msg::Point& b) {
1108      return std::sqrt((a.x - b.x) * (a.x - b.x) + (a.y - b.y) * (a.y - b.y) + (a.z - b.z) * (a.z - b.z));
1109    };
1110    const double remaining = dist(carrot_, vertex);
1111    if (remaining < 1e-3) { carrot_ = vertex; return true; }
1112    if (dist(drone, carrot_) < max_lead_) {
1113      const double step = std::min(cruise_speed_ / setpoint_rate_hz_, remaining);
1114      carrot_.x += (vertex.x - carrot_.x) / remaining * step;
1115      carrot_.y += (vertex.y - carrot_.y) / remaining * step;
1116      carrot_.z += (vertex.z - carrot_.z) / remaining * step;
1117    }
1118    return dist(carrot_, vertex) < 1e-3;
1119  }
1120
L1100–1120

advanceCarrot(): follow the straight line

Important for the exam. The carrot is a point on the segment that moves towards the vertex by cruise_speed / 20 per tick (e.g. 1 m/s → 5 cm per tick), but only while the drone is closer than max_lead (1 m) to it. The carrot waits for a slow drone.

Why: if the setpoint jumps straight to the segment end, ArduCopter flies there with its own horizontal and vertical speed limits and leaves the straight line (0.56 m measured, e.g. descending early). The A* route is only guaranteed collision-free on the line, which matters most over a rack. Returns true when the carrot is at the vertex.

1121  void alignPathTick()
1122  {
1123    const double heading_error = std::abs(std::remainder(
1124      target_yaw_ - currentMapYaw(), 2.0 * M_PI));
1125    if (heading_error > yaw_tolerance_deg_ * M_PI / 180.0) {
1126      stable_since_ = rclcpp::Time(0, 0, get_clock()->get_clock_type());
1127      return;
1128    }
1129    if (stable_since_.nanoseconds() == 0) stable_since_ = now();
1130    if ((now() - stable_since_).seconds() >= yaw_hold_time_sec_) {
1131      RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "Aligned with path heading %.1f deg", target_yaw_ * 180.0 / M_PI);
1132      transition(State::NAVIGATE);
1133    }
1134  }
1135
L1121–1135

alignPathTick(): turn before flying

Hovering at the target, wait until the heading error (wrapped to ±180°) is ≤ 5° continuously for 0.5 s, then NAVIGATE. Any larger error resets the hold time.

1136  double segmentYaw(size_t path_index) const
1137  {
1138    if (path_index == 0 || path_index >= path_.size()) return target_yaw_;
1139    const size_t from = path_index - 1;
1140    const size_t to = path_index;
1141    const auto& a = path_[from].position;
1142    const auto& b = path_[to].position;
1143    return std::atan2(b.y - a.y, b.x - a.x);
1144  }
1145
L1136–1145

segmentYaw(): direction of travel

Heading of the segment ending at point path_index: atan2(Δy, Δx) in the map frame. With no valid segment, keep the current yaw. This is the "atan2 of the segment" from the assignment.

1147  {

1149    switch (task.type) {
1150      case mission::TaskType::NONE:
1151        logTaskComplete(task.type);
1152        advanceWaypoint();
1153        return;
1154      case mission::TaskType::TAKEOFF:
1155        logTaskComplete(task.type);
1156        advanceWaypoint();
1157        return;
L1146–1157

taskTick(): tasks without action

- and takeoff need no action at the waypoint (the takeoff itself already happened in TAKEOFF): log completion and continue.

1158      case mission::TaskType::YAW: {
1159        const double commanded = task.yaw_degrees * M_PI / 180.0;
1160        target_yaw_ = unwrapNear(target_yaw_, commanded);
1161        const double heading_error = std::abs(std::remainder(
1162          currentMapYaw() - commanded, 2.0 * M_PI));
1163        if (heading_error > yaw_tolerance_deg_ * M_PI / 180.0) {
1164          stable_since_ = rclcpp::Time(0, 0, get_clock()->get_clock_type());
1165          return;
1166        }
1167        if (stable_since_.nanoseconds() == 0) stable_since_ = now();
1168        if ((now() - stable_since_).seconds() >= yaw_hold_time_sec_) {
1169          RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "Yaw task %.1f deg reached and held for %.2f s",
1170            task.yaw_degrees, yaw_hold_time_sec_);
1171          logTaskComplete(task.type);
1172          advanceWaypoint();
1173        }
1174        return;
1175      }
L1158–1175

taskTick(): yaw task

Convert degrees to radians, set the commanded yaw (unwrapped near the current target, so the shorter turn is taken). Compare with the measured map yaw; within 5° for 0.5 s: done. Absolute map-frame heading, CCW positive, 0° = map +x.

1176      case mission::TaskType::LAND:
1177        landtakeoff_pending_ = false;

1179        transition(State::LAND);
1180        return;
1181      case mission::TaskType::LAND_TAKEOFF:
1182        landtakeoff_pending_ = true;

taskTick(): land and landtakeoff

Both go to LAND. landtakeoff_pending_ decides what happens after the disarm: false = DONE (mission end), true = GROUND_WAIT → re-arm → RETAKEOFF.

logTaskComplete()

The log line Completed task 'X' at waypoint N: proof that every task ran, in order.

1195  void advanceWaypoint()
1196  {
1197    ++waypoint_index_;
1198    if (waypoint_index_ >= mission_.size()) {
1199      transition(State::DONE);
1200      RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "Mission complete");
1201      return;
1202    }
1203    if (prefetch_request_pending_) transition(State::WAIT_PREFETCH);
1204    else transition(State::PLAN);
1205  }
1206
L1195–1206

advanceWaypoint(): next waypoint

Increment the index. Past the last one: DONE. Otherwise, if the next route was prefetched, WAIT_PREFETCH (usually immediate); else PLAN (request it now).

1207  void disarmOnGround()
1208  {
1209    if (!service_pending_ && arm_client_->service_is_ready()) {
1210      const uint64_t request_id = beginServiceCall();
1211      auto request = std::make_shared<mavros_msgs::srv::CommandBool::Request>();
1212      request->value = false;
1213      arm_client_->async_send_request(request, [this, request_id](
1214            std::shared_future<mavros_msgs::srv::CommandBool::Response::SharedPtr> future) {
1215        std::lock_guard<std::mutex> lock(state_mutex_);
1216        if (!finishServiceCall(request_id)) return;
1217        const auto response = future.get();
1218        next_disarm_attempt_at_ = std::chrono::steady_clock::now() +
1219          std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::steady_clock::duration>(
1220            std::chrono::duration<double>(arm_retry_interval_sec_));
1221        if (!response || !response->success) {
1222          RCLCPP_WARN(get_logger(), "Disarm refused by FCU (not landed yet?); retrying in %.1f s",
1223            arm_retry_interval_sec_);
1224        }
1225      });
1226    } else logWaiting();
1227  }
1228
L1207–1228

disarmOnGround(): fallback disarm

Sends cmd/arming(false). Whatever the answer, the next attempt is allowed 2 s later; a refusal ("not landed yet") is only a warning. WAIT_DISARMED sees the real disarm in /mavros/state.

Shared pointers to the callback groups, subscriptions, publishers, service clients and the timer. They must be stored: if a handle is destroyed, the subscription/timer stops.

1324int main(int argc, char** argv)
1325{
1326  rclcpp::init(argc, argv);
1327  try {
1328    auto node = std::make_shared<MissionExecutorNode>();
1329    rclcpp::executors::MultiThreadedExecutor executor;
1330    executor.add_node(node);
1331    executor.spin();
1332  } catch (const std::exception& error) {
1333    RCLCPP_FATAL(rclcpp::get_logger("mission_executor_node"), "%s", error.what());
1334    rclcpp::shutdown();
1335    return 1;
1336  }
1337  rclcpp::shutdown();
1338  return 0;
1339}
L1324–1339

main()

  • rclcpp::init: start ROS 2 (parses --ros-args).
  • Create the node (the constructor may throw: bad parameter, bad mission file).
  • MultiThreadedExecutor: runs callbacks on several threads, so the two callback groups really run in parallel. spin() blocks until shutdown (Ctrl+C or our rclcpp::shutdown() after DONE).
  • An exception: log FATAL and exit with code 1, which the run script reports.

Task and Waypoint

Anonymous namespace

namespace { } without a name = the helpers inside are visible only in this file (internal linkage), like static functions in C.

13std::string trim(const std::string& value)
14{
15  const size_t first = value.find_first_not_of(" \t\r\n");
16  if (first == std::string::npos) return {};
17  const size_t last = value.find_last_not_of(" \t\r\n");
18  return value.substr(first, last - first + 1);
19}
20
L13–20

trim()

Removes leading/trailing spaces, tabs and \r\n (a file saved on Windows ends lines with \r). An all-space string becomes empty.

21double parseNumber(const std::string& text, size_t line, const char* field)
22{
23  size_t parsed = 0;
24  double value = 0.0;
25  try {
26    value = std::stod(trim(text), &parsed);
27  } catch (const std::exception&) {
28    throw std::runtime_error("mission line " + std::to_string(line) + ": invalid " + field);
29  }
30  if (parsed != trim(text).size() || !std::isfinite(value))
31    throw std::runtime_error("mission line " + std::to_string(line) + ": invalid " + field);
32  return value;
33}
34
35}  // namespace
36
L21–36

parseNumber(): strict numbers

std::stod converts text to a double and reports how many characters it used (parsed). The value is rejected if conversion fails, if not the whole field was a number (e.g. 1.5m), or if it is not finite (nan, inf). The error names the line and the field.

37Mission loadCsv(const std::string& path)

40  if (!input) throw std::runtime_error("cannot open mission file: " + path);
41
42  Mission mission;
43  std::string row;
44  size_t line_number = 0;

L37–45

loadCsv(): open the file

Opens the file (error if missing) and prepares the result, the current line and the line counter.

46  while (std::getline(input, row)) {
47    ++line_number;
48    row = trim(row);
49    if (row.empty() || row.front() == '#') continue;
50
51    std::vector<std::string> fields;
52    std::stringstream row_stream(row);
53    std::string field;
54    while (std::getline(row_stream, field, ',')) fields.push_back(trim(field));
55    if (fields.size() != 5)
56      throw std::runtime_error("mission line " + std::to_string(line_number) +
57                               ": expected x,y,z,precision,task");
58
59    if (!header_seen && fields[0] == "x" && fields[1] == "y" && fields[2] == "z") {
60      header_seen = true;
61      continue;
62    }
63    header_seen = true;
64
L46–64

Read line by line, split by commas

  • Count lines (for error messages), trim, skip empty lines and # comments.
  • Split on , and trim every field. Exactly 5 fields are required.
  • The first data-like line that starts with x,y,z is a header: skip it once.
65    Waypoint waypoint;
66    waypoint.x = parseNumber(fields[0], line_number, "x");
67    waypoint.y = parseNumber(fields[1], line_number, "y");
68    waypoint.z = parseNumber(fields[2], line_number, "z");
69    if (fields[3] == "soft") waypoint.precision = Precision::SOFT;
70    else if (fields[3] == "hard") waypoint.precision = Precision::HARD;
71    else throw std::runtime_error("mission line " + std::to_string(line_number) +
72                                  ": precision must be soft or hard");
73
74    const std::string& task = fields[4];
75    if (task.empty() || task == "-") waypoint.task.type = TaskType::NONE;
76    else if (task == "takeoff") waypoint.task.type = TaskType::TAKEOFF;
77    else if (task == "land") waypoint.task.type = TaskType::LAND;
78    else if (task == "landtakeoff") waypoint.task.type = TaskType::LAND_TAKEOFF;
79    else if (task.rfind("yaw", 0) == 0) {
80      waypoint.task.type = TaskType::YAW;
81      waypoint.task.yaw_degrees = parseNumber(task.substr(3), line_number, "yaw angle");
82    } else {
83      throw std::runtime_error("mission line " + std::to_string(line_number) +
84                               ": unsupported task '" + task + "'");
85    }
86    mission.push_back(waypoint);
87  }
88
L65–88

Fields → Waypoint

  • x, y, z through parseNumber.
  • precision must be exactly soft or hard.
  • task: - or empty = none; takeoff, land, landtakeoff; anything starting with yaw is a yaw task, the rest of the text (substr(3)) is the angle, so yaw-45 → −45°. Anything else throws.
89  if (mission.empty()) throw std::runtime_error("mission file contains no waypoints: " + path);
90  return mission;
91}
92
L89–92

No waypoints = error

A file with only comments is an error. Otherwise the list is returned (moved, not copied).

93const char* toString(Precision precision)

96}

98const char* toString(TaskType task)
99{
100  switch (task) {
101    case TaskType::NONE: return "-";
102    case TaskType::TAKEOFF: return "takeoff";
103    case TaskType::LAND: return "land";
104    case TaskType::LAND_TAKEOFF: return "landtakeoff";
105    case TaskType::YAW: return "yaw";

19One-command runner (run_a1_2.sh)

run_a1_2.sh · 468 lines · 24 explained blocks

A Bash script that does everything an operator would otherwise do in five terminals: build, start Gazebo → SITL → MAVROS with readiness checks between them, start the planner and the executor, record a rosbag, and plot at the end. It is not part of the "robot"; it is the test harness around it.

1#!/usr/bin/env bash
2# A1.2 helper: builds the package, (optionally) starts Gazebo -> SITL -> MAVROS in their own
3# terminals with readiness gates between them, then starts the map planner and the mission executor.
4#
5# Usage: ./run_a1_2.sh [MISSION.csv]
6# Environment (all optional):
7#   A1_2_URK_DIR           repo with scripts/run_gazebo.sh, run_sitl.sh, run_mavros.sh   [$HOME/LRS-URK]
8#   A1_2_START_SIM         1 = start missing Gazebo/SITL/MAVROS here, 0 = you started them [1]
9#   A1_2_STOP_SIM          1 = kill Gazebo/SITL/MAVROS when this script exits             [0]
10#   A1_2_TERMINAL          auto|gnome-terminal|xterm|x-terminal-emulator                  [auto]
11#   A1_2_MIN_RTF           Gazebo real-time factor required before SITL starts            [0.8]
12#   A1_2_RTF_TIMEOUT       seconds to wait for that real-time factor, then continue        [90]
13#   A1_2_SITL_BOOT_TIMEOUT seconds for SITL to build/boot and show its first heartbeat     [300]
14#   A1_2_READY_TIMEOUT     seconds for planner + MAVROS connection                         [180]
15#   A1_2_POLL_INTERVAL     seconds between readiness polls                                 [5]
16#   A1_2_SKIP_BUILD        1 = skip colcon build                                           [0]
17#   A1_2_TIME_SCALE        multiply the executor's wall-clock timeouts (default: 1/RTF when RTF < MIN_RTF)
18#   A1_2_LOG_DIR           where simulator logs go                                         [<ws>/log/a1_2_sim]
19#   A1_2_RUNS_DIR          one folder per mission run: logs, rosbag, plots                 [<ws>/log/a1_2_runs]
20#   A1_2_RECORD            1 = record a rosbag of the flight into the run folder           [1]
21#   A1_2_PLOT              1 = run plot_a1_2.py on the run folder when the mission ends    [1]
22#   A1_2_SHOW_PLOTS        1 = also open the plot windows (needs a display)                [1]
23#   A1_2_SHOW_3D           1 = also open the 3D voxel view of the route (view_3d_a1_2.py)  [1]
24#   A1_2_RVIZ              1 = open RViz with the map, racks, planned and flown path       [0]
L1–24

Shebang and usage header

The first line tells Linux to run the file with bash (found via env on the PATH). The comment block is the manual: usage and every A1_2_* option with its default in brackets.

25set -euo pipefail
26
L25–26

Strict mode

  • -e: stop at the first command that fails.
  • -u: using an unset variable is an error (catches typos).
  • -o pipefail: a pipeline fails if any part fails, not just the last.

L27–53

Paths and options

  • ROOT_DIR: the absolute folder of the script, so it works from any current directory.
  • MISSION_FILE="${1:-default}": first argument, or the example mission.
  • ${A1_2_X:-default}: take the environment variable if set, else the default. Every option follows this pattern.
  • The PIDs start empty; cleanup only stops what was started.
54# BEGIN_FUNCS
55log()  { printf '[%s] %s\n' "$(date +%H:%M:%S)" "$*"; }
56warn() { log "WARNING: $*" >&2; }
57is_uint() { [[ "$1" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]]; }
58is_number() { [[ "$1" =~ ^[0-9]+([.][0-9]+)?$ ]]; }
59float_ge() { awk -v a="$1" -v b="$2" 'BEGIN { exit !(a + 0 >= b + 0) }'; }

L54–60

Small helper functions

  • log/warn: timestamped messages (warn to stderr).
  • is_uint/is_number: regex checks for option values.
  • float_ge: Bash only does integer maths, so awk compares decimals.
61# First /world/<name>/stats topic published by a running Gazebo (empty if none).
62gz_stats_topic() {
63  timeout 6s gz topic -l 2>/dev/null | grep -m1 -E '^/world/[^/]+/stats$' || true
64}
65# One field (e.g. real_time_factor, paused) from the world statistics message.
66gz_stat() {
67  timeout 8s gz topic -e -t "$1" -n 1 2>/dev/null | awk -v key="$2:" '$1 == key { print $2; exit }' || true
68}
69sitl_running()   { pgrep -f 'bin/arducopter' >/dev/null 2>&1; }
70mavros_running() { ros2 service list 2>/dev/null | grep -qx '/mavros/set_mode'; }
71
L61–71

Talking to Gazebo and checking processes

  • gz_stats_topic: lists Gazebo topics and picks /world/<name>/stats. Empty = Gazebo is not running. timeout stops a hanging gz call.
  • gz_stat: reads one stats message and extracts one field (real_time_factor, paused) with awk.
  • sitl_running: is an arducopter process alive? mavros_running: does the /mavros/set_mode service exist?
72wait_for_log() { # file pattern timeout_s
73  local deadline=$((SECONDS + $3))
74  while ((SECONDS < deadline)); do
75    if grep -aqE -- "$2" "$1" 2>/dev/null; then return 0; fi
76    sleep 2
77  done
78  return 1
79}
80
L72–80

wait_for_log()

Polls a file every 2 s until a regex appears or the timeout passes (SECONDS = Bash's built-in seconds-since-start counter).

81# Factor by which wall-clock timeouts must grow when Gazebo runs slower than real time.
82time_scale() { # rtf
83  awk -v r="$1" -v m="$MIN_RTF" 'BEGIN { if (r + 0 <= 0 || r + 0 >= m + 0) { print 1; exit }
84    s = int(1 / r); if (s < 1 / r) s++; if (s > 10) s = 10; print s }'
85}
86scaled() { awk -v v="$1" -v s="$2" 'BEGIN { printf "%.1f", v * s }'; }
87# END_FUNCS
88
L81–88

Time scaling

If Gazebo runs at real-time factor r < 0.8, the executor's wall-clock timeouts are multiplied by ⌈1/r⌉ (max 10). Example: r = 0.28 → ×4. scaled v s prints v·s with one decimal.

L96–125

Check arguments and options

  • At most one argument; --help prints usage.
  • A relative mission path is made absolute (relative to where you ran the script); it must exist.
  • Timeouts must be positive integers; MIN_RTF a number; on/off options 0 or 1. ${!name} = indirect expansion (the value of the variable whose name is in name).
  • When starting the simulator, the three LRS-URK scripts must exist and be executable.
  • Exit code 2 = usage error.

135# Build first, before the simulator exists: compiling during sim start-up slows Gazebo down, and a
136# slow Gazebo is what makes ArduPilot report "Accels/Gyros inconsistent".

L135–149

Build the package

colcon build --packages-select lrs_psenak_valko compiles only our package. It runs before the simulator: compiling uses all CPU cores and would slow Gazebo, which causes "Accels/Gyros inconsistent" pre-arm failures. With A1_2_SKIP_BUILD=1 it only checks that the executable exists. Then source our workspace (install/setup.bash) so ros2 run lrs_psenak_valko ... works.

150stop_group() { # pid [signal]: stop a process started with setsid (and its children); escalates
151  local pid="$1" sig="${2:-TERM}" s i
152  [[ -n "$pid" ]] || return 0
153  for s in "$sig" TERM KILL; do
154    kill -0 "$pid" 2>/dev/null || break
155    kill "-$s" -- "-$pid" 2>/dev/null || kill "-$s" "$pid" 2>/dev/null || true
156    for ((i = 0; i < 20; i++)); do kill -0 "$pid" 2>/dev/null || break; sleep 0.5; done
157  done
158  wait "$pid" 2>/dev/null || true
159}
160
L150–160

stop_group(): reliable stopping

Processes are started with setsid, so each one leads its own process group; kill -SIG -- -PID signals the whole group (ros2 run plus the real node). It escalates: the given signal, then TERM, then KILL, waiting up to 10 s each. It was added because ros2 bag record once ignored SIGINT and hung the cleanup.

161cleanup() {
162  trap - EXIT INT TERM
163  stop_group "$EXECUTOR_PID"
164  stop_group "$BAG_PID" INT          # SIGINT lets ros2 bag close the file properly
165  stop_group "$PLANNER_PID"
166  stop_group "$RVIZ_PID"
167  if [[ -n "$RUN_DIR" && "$PLOT" == 1 && -s "$RUN_DIR/flight_log.csv" ]]; then
168    log "Plotting $RUN_DIR"
169    python3 "$ROOT_DIR/plot_a1_2.py" "$RUN_DIR" --no-show 2>&1 | grep -v -e 'Axes3D' -e 'warnings.warn' ||
170      warn "plot_a1_2.py failed; run it by hand: python3 plot_a1_2.py $RUN_DIR"
171    if [[ "$SHOW_PLOTS" == 1 && -n "${DISPLAY:-}${WAYLAND_DISPLAY:-}" ]]; then
172      # Detached, so the windows stay open after this script (and the simulator) are gone.
173      log "Opening plot windows (close them when done)"
174      setsid python3 "$ROOT_DIR/plot_a1_2.py" "$RUN_DIR" > "$RUN_DIR/plot_windows.log" 2>&1 < /dev/null &
175      disown || true
176    fi
177    if [[ "$SHOW_3D" == 1 && -n "${DISPLAY:-}${WAYLAND_DISPLAY:-}" ]]; then
178      log "Opening 3D route view (keys: space light, +/- wall height, i occupancy, q quit)"
179      setsid python3 "$ROOT_DIR/view_3d_a1_2.py" "$RUN_DIR" > "$RUN_DIR/view_3d.log" 2>&1 < /dev/null &
180      disown || true
181    fi
182  fi
183  if [[ -n "$RUN_DIR" ]]; then log "Run folder: $RUN_DIR"; fi
184  if [[ "$STOP_SIM" == 1 ]]; then
185    log "Stopping MAVROS, SITL and Gazebo (A1_2_STOP_SIM=1)"
186    pkill -f 'mavros_node' 2>/dev/null || true
187    pkill -f 'sim_vehicle.py' 2>/dev/null || true
188    pkill -f 'mavproxy' 2>/dev/null || true
189    pkill -f 'bin/arducopter' 2>/dev/null || true
190    pkill -f 'gz sim' 2>/dev/null || true
191  fi
192}
193trap cleanup EXIT INT TERM

L161–194

cleanup(): runs on exit, Ctrl+C or error

  • trap -: disarm the trap so cleanup cannot run twice.
  • Stop executor, then rosbag with SIGINT (that lets it close the bag file properly), planner, RViz.
  • If a flight log exists: run the plots (PNG + terminal summary), then open the plot windows and the 3D view detached (setsid ... & disown), so they stay open after the script ends.
  • With A1_2_STOP_SIM=1: kill MAVROS, SITL, MAVProxy and Gazebo by name.

trap cleanup EXIT INT TERM registers it for normal exit, Ctrl+C and kill.

195# ---------------------------------------------------------------- simulator start-up
196pick_terminal() {
197  local t="$TERMINAL"
198  if [[ "$t" == auto ]]; then
199    if [[ -z "${DISPLAY:-}${WAYLAND_DISPLAY:-}" ]]; then t=none
200    elif command -v gnome-terminal >/dev/null 2>&1; then t=gnome-terminal
201    elif command -v xterm >/dev/null 2>&1; then t=xterm
202    elif command -v x-terminal-emulator >/dev/null 2>&1; then t=x-terminal-emulator
203    else t=none; fi
204  fi
205  printf '%s' "$t"
206}
207
L195–207

pick_terminal()

Chooses how to open new windows: none without a display; otherwise gnome-terminal, xterm or x-terminal-emulator, whichever exists (command -v).

208# launch_component NAME SCRIPT: runs $URK_DIR/scripts/SCRIPT in its own terminal window and copies
209# its output to $LOG_DIR/NAME.log (through `script`, so the program still sees a real terminal).
210launch_component() {
211  local name="$1" script_name="$2" launcher="$LOG_DIR/launch_$1.sh" logfile="$LOG_DIR/$1.log" term run
212  term="$(pick_terminal)"
213  if [[ "$term" == none ]]; then
214    printf 'No terminal emulator/display found to open %s in.\n' "$name" >&2
215    printf 'Start Gazebo, SITL and MAVROS yourself and run with A1_2_START_SIM=0.\n' >&2
216    exit 1
217  fi
218  mkdir -p "$LOG_DIR"
219  : > "$logfile"
220  run="scripts/$script_name"
221  if command -v script >/dev/null 2>&1; then run="script -qefc 'scripts/$script_name' '$logfile'"; fi
222  cat > "$launcher" <<LAUNCHER
223#!/usr/bin/env bash
224cd "$URK_DIR" || exit 1
225$run
226rc=\$?
227echo
228echo "[$name exited with status \$rc] - press Enter to close this window"
229read -r _
230LAUNCHER
231  chmod +x "$launcher"
232  log "Starting $name in a new terminal (log: $logfile)"
233  # bash -i so ~/.bashrc runs (ROS, ArduPilot venv, ...), exactly like a terminal you open yourself.
234  case "$term" in
235    gnome-terminal)      gnome-terminal --title="$name" -- bash -ic "$launcher" >/dev/null 2>&1 & ;;
236    xterm)               xterm -T "$name" -e bash -ic "$launcher" >/dev/null 2>&1 & ;;
237    x-terminal-emulator) x-terminal-emulator -T "$name" -e bash -ic "$launcher" >/dev/null 2>&1 & ;;
238    *) printf 'Unknown A1_2_TERMINAL=%s\n' "$term" >&2; exit 2 ;;
239  esac
240  disown || true
241}
242
L208–242

launch_component(): one simulator window

  • Writes a small launcher script into log/a1_2_sim/ that runs the LRS-URK script through script -qefc. script records the output to a log and keeps a real terminal, which MAVProxy needs.
  • At the end the window waits for Enter, so you can read errors.
  • Opens it with bash -ic: an interactive shell reads ~/.bashrc (ROS and ArduPilot environment), exactly like a terminal you open yourself.
243wait_gazebo_realtime() {
244  local topic="$1" deadline=$((SECONDS + RTF_TIMEOUT)) good=0 slow=0 rtf paused warned=0
245  log "Waiting for Gazebo to run at >= $MIN_RTF x real time (timeout ${RTF_TIMEOUT}s)"
246  while ((SECONDS < deadline)); do
247    paused="$(gz_stat "$topic" paused)"
248    rtf="$(gz_stat "$topic" real_time_factor)"
249    if [[ "$paused" == true ]]; then
250      if ((!warned)); then warn "Gazebo is PAUSED - press Play in the Gazebo window"; warned=1; fi
251      good=0
252    elif [[ -n "$rtf" ]] && float_ge "$rtf" "$MIN_RTF"; then
253      slow=0
254      good=$((good + 1))
255      log "Gazebo real-time factor $rtf (ok $good/3)"
256      if ((good >= 3)); then return 0; fi
257    else
258      good=0
259      log "Gazebo real-time factor ${rtf:-unknown}; waiting"
260      # A PC that stays far below the target will not get there by waiting longer.
261      if [[ -n "$rtf" ]] && ! float_ge "$rtf" "$(awk -v m="$MIN_RTF" 'BEGIN { print m / 2 }')"; then
262        slow=$((slow + 1))
263        if ((slow >= 5)); then
264          warn "Gazebo stays at ~${rtf} x real time (this PC is slower than that); not waiting longer."
265          return 0
266        fi
267      fi
268    fi
269    sleep 2
270  done
271  warn "Gazebo did not reach real-time factor $MIN_RTF in ${RTF_TIMEOUT}s (last: ${rtf:-unknown}). Continuing, but expect 'Accels/Gyros inconsistent'. Close other heavy programs, or run Gazebo headless."
272}
273
L243–273

wait_gazebo_realtime()

Every 2 s reads paused and real_time_factor:

  • paused: warn once ("press Play").
  • RTF ≥ 0.8 three times in a row: good, continue.
  • RTF below half the target five times: this PC will not get faster by waiting; continue after ~15 s instead of the full timeout (this used to waste 90 s every run).
  • Timeout: warn and continue anyway.
274if [[ "$START_SIM" == 1 ]]; then
275  mkdir -p "$LOG_DIR"

291      exit 1
292    fi
293  fi
294  wait_gazebo_realtime "$GZ_TOPIC"

297  if sitl_running; then
298    log "SITL already running - reusing it"
299  else
300    launch_component sitl run_sitl.sh
301    SITL_LOG="$LOG_DIR/sitl.log"
302    log "Waiting for SITL to boot (first build can take minutes; timeout ${SITL_BOOT_TIMEOUT}s)"
303    # MAVProxy's --console sends FCU messages (PreArm, EKF) to its own window, not to stdout, so
304    # only MAVProxy's own lines can be matched here.  GPS/EKF readiness is checked by the executor.
305    SITL_BOOT_RE='Detected vehicle|Received [0-9]+ parameters|online system|ArduPilot Ready'
306    deadline=$((SECONDS + SITL_BOOT_TIMEOUT))
307    proc_seen_at=0
308    BOOT_OK=0
309    while ((SECONDS < deadline)); do
310      if grep -aqE -- "$SITL_BOOT_RE" "$SITL_LOG" 2>/dev/null; then BOOT_OK=1; break; fi
311      if sitl_running; then
312        if ((proc_seen_at == 0)); then proc_seen_at=$SECONDS; fi
313        if ((SECONDS - proc_seen_at >= 60)); then break; fi   # process is up but the log shows nothing
314      fi
315      sleep 2
316    done
317    if ((BOOT_OK)); then
318      log "SITL is up"
319    elif sitl_running; then
320      warn "SITL is running but $SITL_LOG has no boot message yet; continuing."
321    else
322      printf 'SITL did not start within %s s; see %s\n' "$SITL_BOOT_TIMEOUT" "$SITL_LOG" >&2
323      exit 1
324    fi
325  fi

MAVProxy's --console shows the FCU's PreArm/EKF messages only in its GUI window, so they cannot be read from the log. The old script waited for them and always ran into a 240 s timeout. If the process runs but prints nothing for 60 s, continue anyway.

Start the planner
