342printf 'Waiting for map planner and MAVROS (timeout: %s s)...\n' "$READY_TIMEOUT"
343READY=0
344READY_DEADLINE=$((SECONDS + READY_TIMEOUT))
345LAST_WAIT_REPORT=$SECONDS
346while ((SECONDS < READY_DEADLINE)); do
347  if ! kill -0 "$PLANNER_PID" 2>/dev/null; then
348    wait "$PLANNER_PID"
349    printf 'Map planner exited before becoming ready.\n' >&2
350    exit 1
351  fi
352
353  PLANNER_READY=0
354  if ros2 node list 2>/dev/null | grep -qx '/map_planner_node'; then
355    PLANNER_READY=1
356  fi
357
358  MAVROS_READY=1
359  SERVICES_READY=1
360  MISSING_SERVICES=()
361  SERVICE_LIST="$(ros2 service list 2>/dev/null || true)"
362  for service in /mavros/set_mode /mavros/cmd/arming /mavros/cmd/takeoff /mavros/cmd/land; do
363    if ! grep -qx "$service" <<< "$SERVICE_LIST"; then
364      MAVROS_READY=0
365      SERVICES_READY=0
366      MISSING_SERVICES+=("$service")
367    fi
368  done
369
370  FCU_CONNECTED=0
371  POSE_READY=0
372  if ((SERVICES_READY)); then
373    FCU_STATE="$(timeout 7s ros2 topic echo --once /mavros/state 2>/dev/null || true)"
374    if [[ "$FCU_STATE" != *"connected: true"* ]]; then
375      MAVROS_READY=0
376    else
377      FCU_CONNECTED=1
378      POSE_SAMPLE="$(timeout 7s ros2 topic echo --once /mavros/local_position/pose 2>/dev/null || true)"
379      if [[ "$POSE_SAMPLE" != *"position:"* ]]; then
380        MAVROS_READY=0
381      else
382        POSE_READY=1
383      fi
384    fi
385  else
386    MAVROS_READY=0
387  fi
388
389  if ((PLANNER_READY && MAVROS_READY)); then
390    READY=1
391    break
392  fi
393  if ((SECONDS - LAST_WAIT_REPORT >= 10)); then
394    printf 'Still waiting: planner=%s, mavros_services=%s, fcu_connected=%s, local_pose=%s' \
395      "$PLANNER_READY" "$SERVICES_READY" "$FCU_CONNECTED" "$POSE_READY"
396    if ((${#MISSING_SERVICES[@]})); then
397      printf ', missing_services=%s' "${MISSING_SERVICES[*]}"
398    fi
399    printf '\n'
400    LAST_WAIT_REPORT=$SECONDS
401  fi
402  sleep "$POLL_INTERVAL"
403done
404
405if ((!READY)); then
406  printf 'Planner/MAVROS did not become ready within %s s. Check Gazebo, SITL, MAVROS and the planner terminals.\n' \
407    "$READY_TIMEOUT" >&2
408  exit 1
409fi
410printf 'Planner ready; MAVROS connected with services and local pose available.\n'
411
L342–411

Readiness loop

Every POLL_INTERVAL seconds, until READY_TIMEOUT:

  • the planner process must still live (else exit with its error);
  • /map_planner_node exists in ros2 node list;
  • the four MAVROS services exist;
  • /mavros/state says connected: true;
  • a /mavros/local_position/pose message arrives.

Progress is printed every 10 s. Not ready in time: exit with an explanation.

412# GPS fix / EKF readiness is NOT waited for here: the executor waits in WAIT_FCU until the EKF
413# reports "is using GPS" (or the GPS fix is stable), and logs every FCU PreArm message.
414
415# The executor's timeouts are wall-clock. If Gazebo runs at e.g. 0.18x real time, a 60 s takeoff
416# timeout is only ~11 s of simulated flight, so scale them.
417RTF_NOW=""
418GZ_TOPIC="$(gz_stats_topic)"
419if [[ -n "$GZ_TOPIC" ]]; then RTF_NOW="$(gz_stat "$GZ_TOPIC" real_time_factor)"; fi
420TIME_SCALE="${A1_2_TIME_SCALE:-$(time_scale "$RTF_NOW")}"
421if ! is_number "$TIME_SCALE"; then printf 'A1_2_TIME_SCALE must be a number.\n' >&2; exit 2; fi
422EXEC_ARGS=(-p "mission_file:=$MISSION_FILE")
423EXEC_ARGS+=(-p "handshake_timeout_sec:=$(scaled 180 "$TIME_SCALE")")
424if float_ge "$TIME_SCALE" 1.5; then
425  warn "Gazebo real-time factor is ${RTF_NOW:-unknown}: scaling executor timeouts x$TIME_SCALE. Fix the simulation speed if you can."
426  EXEC_ARGS+=(
427    -p "takeoff_timeout_sec:=$(scaled 60 "$TIME_SCALE")"
428    -p "takeoff_climb_timeout_sec:=$(scaled 12 "$TIME_SCALE")"
429    -p "landing_timeout_sec:=$(scaled 90 "$TIME_SCALE")"
430    -p "navigation_timeout_sec:=$(scaled 120 "$TIME_SCALE")"
431    -p "yaw_timeout_sec:=$(scaled 15 "$TIME_SCALE")"
432    -p "yaw_hold_time_sec:=$(scaled 0.5 "$TIME_SCALE")"
433    -p "settle_time:=$(scaled 1.0 "$TIME_SCALE")"
434    -p "disarm_grace_sec:=$(scaled 5.0 "$TIME_SCALE")"
435    -p "ground_wait_sec:=$(scaled 3.0 "$TIME_SCALE")"
436    -p "cruise_speed:=$(awk -v s="$TIME_SCALE" 'BEGIN { printf "%.3f", 1.0 / s }')"
437  )
438fi
439
L412–439

Time scaling of the executor

GPS/EKF readiness is left to the executor (it has the FCU messages). The script measures the current RTF and computes the scale (or uses A1_2_TIME_SCALE). The executor arguments are collected in a Bash array (EXEC_ARGS), so values with spaces stay intact. If the scale is ≥ 1.5, every wall-clock timeout is multiplied, and cruise_speed is divided, so the carrot moves at ~1 m/s of simulated time.

L440–459

Run folder, rosbag, RViz

  • Folder log/a1_2_runs/<date>_<time>_<mission>, with a copy of the mission; its path goes to the executor as log_dir.
  • ros2 bag record -o with an explicit topic list (state, pose, velocity, setpoints, FCU texts, planner requests/answers, markers). /mission/flown_path was removed from the list: it re-sends the whole track 5× per second and made the bag 38 MB.
  • Optional RViz with our layout.
460printf 'Starting mission: %s\n' "$MISSION_FILE"

462EXECUTOR_PID=$!

467EXECUTOR_PID=""
468if ((EXEC_RC == 0)); then log "Mission executor finished"; else warn "Mission executor exited with status $EXEC_RC"; fi
L460–468

Start the executor and wait

Runs the executor with all collected arguments and waits for it. wait PID || EXEC_RC=$? stores the exit code without triggering set -e. Exit 0 = mission DONE (the executor exits by itself). Then the script ends, which runs cleanup (plots).

20Flight plots and checks (plot_a1_2.py)

plot_a1_2.py · 445 lines · 32 explained blocks

Reads one run folder and produces topview.png and overview.png, plus a terminal summary: arrival error per waypoint, minimum clearance to obstacles, maximum deviation from the planned route. This is how "no collisions" and "hard waypoints reached" are measured, not just watched in Gazebo.

1#!/usr/bin/env python3
2"""
3plot_a1_2.py -- plots of one A1.2 indoor mission run.
4
5Reads the files the mission executor writes into a run folder (log/a1_2_runs/<time>/):
6  flight_log.csv     20 Hz: state, commanded and measured pose (map frame)
7  planned_paths.csv  every A* route that was flown
8  mission.csv        the mission file of the run (copied by run_a1_2.sh)
9plus the hangar map (map.pcd) and the rack boxes / safety radius from config/planner.yaml.
10
11Figures (saved into the run folder):
12  topview.png    floor plan: obstacles, racks (+ safety radius), planned routes, flown track,
13                 heading arrows, mission waypoints with their tasks
14  overview.png   altitude, yaw, distance to target and speed, clearance to obstacles,
15                 arrival error per waypoint, deviation from the planned route
16
17Clearance = distance from the drone centre to the nearest map point (floor excluded) or rack
18surface. The drone is a collision risk when clearance < drone radius (prop tips, ~0.33 m).
19
20Usage:
21    python3 plot_a1_2.py                    # newest run in log/a1_2_runs
22    python3 plot_a1_2.py log/a1_2_runs/20261018_101500
23    python3 plot_a1_2.py RUN_DIR --no-show  # only save the PNGs
24"""
25

54def read_csv(path):
55    with open(path, newline="") as f:
56        rows = [r for r in csv.DictReader(f) if r.get("t_s") or r.get("leg")]
57    return rows
58
59
60def load_flight(path):
61    rows = read_csv(path)
62    if not rows:
63        sys.exit(f"{path} is empty")
64    num = ["t_s", "waypoint", "armed", "cmd_valid", "cmd_x", "cmd_y", "cmd_z", "cmd_yaw_deg",
65           "x", "y", "z", "yaw_deg", "speed"]
66    data = {k: np.array([float(r[k]) if r[k] not in ("", "nan") else np.nan for r in rows])
67            for k in num}
68    data["state"] = np.array([r["state"] for r in rows])
69    data["mode"] = np.array([r["mode"] for r in rows])
70    data["waypoint"] = data["waypoint"].astype(int)
71    return data

L54–73

read_csv() and load_flight()

csv.DictReader gives one dict per row (column name → text). Rows without a time are dropped. load_flight turns every numeric column into a numpy array (empty/"nan" → NaN) and keeps state and mode as text arrays.

74def load_paths(path):
75    legs = {}
76    if not os.path.exists(path):
77        return legs
78    for r in read_csv(path):
79        legs.setdefault(int(r["leg"]), {"waypoint": int(r["waypoint"]), "pts": []})
80        legs[int(r["leg"])]["pts"].append((float(r["x"]), float(r["y"]), float(r["z"])))
81    for leg in legs.values():
82        leg["pts"] = np.array(leg["pts"])
83    return legs

86def load_mission(path):
87    wps = []
88    with open(path) as f:
89        for line in f:
90            line = line.strip()
91            if not line or line.startswith("#"):
92                continue
93            fields = [c.strip() for c in line.split(",")]
94            if fields[0] == "x":
95                continue
96            wps.append({"x": float(fields[0]), "y": float(fields[1]), "z": float(fields[2]),
97                        "precision": fields[3], "task": fields[4]})
98    return wps

101def load_planner_yaml(path):
102    """Tiny parser for the two values we need (no PyYAML dependency)."""
103    text = open(path).read()
104    safety = float(re.search(r"safety_radius:\s*([-\d.]+)", text).group(1))
105    nums = [float(v) for v in re.findall(r"[-\d.]+", re.search(r"rack_boxes:\s*\[(.*?)\]", text, re.S).group(1))]
106    boxes = [nums[i:i + 7] for i in range(0, len(nums) - len(nums) % 7, 7)]
107    return safety, boxes
108

L101–109

load_planner_yaml()

Reads only safety_radius and rack_boxes with regular expressions, so PyYAML is not needed. The rack list is split into groups of 7.

110def load_pcd_ascii(path):
111    with open(path) as f:
112        header = 0
113        for line in f:
114            header += 1
115            if line.startswith("DATA"):
116                if "ascii" not in line:
117                    sys.exit(f"{path}: only ASCII PCD is supported")
118                break
119    return np.loadtxt(path, skiprows=header, dtype=np.float32, usecols=(0, 1, 2))

L110–121

load_pcd_ascii()

A PCD file is a text header then the points. Counts header lines up to DATA, then np.loadtxt reads x y z (620 k rows, a few seconds).

122def box_surface_points(box, step=0.1):
123    """Points on the surface of a (yawed) box [cx, cy, cz, sx, sy, sz, yaw]."""
124    cx, cy, cz, sx, sy, sz, yaw = box
125    ax = [np.arange(-s / 2, s / 2 + 1e-9, step) for s in (sx, sy, sz)]
126    pts = []
127    for dim in range(3):
128        for side in (-1, 1):
129            grids = np.meshgrid(*[ax[d] if d != dim else np.array([side * (sx, sy, sz)[d] / 2])
130                                  for d in range(3)], indexing="ij")
131            pts.append(np.stack([g.ravel() for g in grids], axis=1))
132    p = np.concatenate(pts)
133    c, s = math.cos(yaw), math.sin(yaw)
134    return np.stack([cx + c * p[:, 0] - s * p[:, 1], cy + s * p[:, 0] + c * p[:, 1], cz + p[:, 2]], axis=1)
135
136
L122–136

box_surface_points()

Samples the six faces of a rack box on a 10 cm grid (rotated by its yaw). The racks are then obstacles even where the point cloud has holes.

137def box_corners_xy(box):
138    cx, cy, _, sx, sy, _, yaw = box
139    c, s = math.cos(yaw), math.sin(yaw)
140    pts = [(-sx / 2, -sy / 2), (sx / 2, -sy / 2), (sx / 2, sy / 2), (-sx / 2, sy / 2), (-sx / 2, -sy / 2)]
141    return np.array([(cx + c * x - s * y, cy + s * x + c * y) for x, y in pts])
142

The rotated rectangle of a box for the top view (5 points, closed outline).

145def airborne_mask(data):
146    return (data["armed"] > 0.5) & (data["z"] > 0.3)

L145–148

airborne_mask()

True where the drone is armed and higher than 0.3 m. Only airborne samples count for collision risk.

149def clearance(data, obstacles):
150    """Distance drone centre -> nearest obstacle point (every sample; floor is not an obstacle)."""
151    from scipy.spatial import cKDTree
152    tree = cKDTree(obstacles)
153    pos = np.stack([data["x"], data["y"], data["z"]], axis=1)
154    dist, _ = tree.query(pos)
155    return dist
156
157
L149–157

clearance(): distance to the nearest obstacle

Builds a k-d tree (scipy.spatial.cKDTree) over all obstacle points. A query then finds the nearest point in O(log n) instead of comparing with 600 k points. One query per logged pose gives the distance from the drone centre to the nearest wall, pillar, truss or rack. The floor is excluded by the caller; altitude is plotted separately.

158def ground_spans(data):
159    """(t_start, t_end) of every period on the ground after the first takeoff (landtakeoff, end)."""
160    air = airborne_mask(data)
161    if not air.any():
162        return []
163    t = data["t_s"]
164    first = np.argmax(air)
165    spans, start = [], None
166    for k in range(first, len(t)):
167        if not air[k] and start is None:
168            start = k
169        elif air[k] and start is not None:
170            spans.append((t[start], t[k]))
171            start = None
172    if start is not None:
173        spans.append((t[start], t[-1]))
174    return [sp for sp in spans if sp[1] - sp[0] > 1.0]
175
176
L158–176

ground_spans()

Finds the time intervals spent on the ground after the first takeoff (landtakeoff, final landing), longer than 1 s. They are labelled in every panel.

180        text = "landed\n(end)" if t1 >= last_t - 0.1 else "on ground\n(landtakeoff)"
181        ax.text((t0 + t1) / 2, 0.97, text, transform=ax.get_xaxis_transform(), ha="center", va="top",
182                fontsize=7, color="0.3", bbox=dict(boxstyle="round,pad=0.2", fc="white", ec="0.7", alpha=0.85))

L177–184

label_ground()

Writes "on ground (landtakeoff)" or "landed (end)" at the top of each such interval (get_xaxis_transform(): x in data units, y as a fraction of the axis height).

185def arrivals(data, mission):
186    """Position error when the executor declared each waypoint reached (entry into TASK)."""
187    out = []
188    for i, wp in enumerate(mission, start=1):
189        idx = np.where((data["waypoint"] == i) & (data["state"] == "TASK"))[0]
190        if len(idx) == 0:
191            out.append(None)
192            continue
193        k = idx[0]
194        err = math.dist((data["x"][k], data["y"][k], data["z"][k]), (wp["x"], wp["y"], wp["z"]))
195        out.append({"t": data["t_s"][k], "err": err})
196    return out
197
198
L185–198

arrivals(): accuracy at each waypoint

For every waypoint, the first log row where the executor was in TASK for it is the moment it declared "reached". The 3D distance from the logged position to the mission point is the arrival error (plotted against the tolerance). None = never reached.

199def route_deviation(data, legs):
200    """3D distance from the drone to the planned route of the leg it is flying (NAVIGATE/ALIGN)."""
201    by_wp = {}
202    for leg in legs.values():
203        by_wp[leg["waypoint"]] = leg["pts"]           # the last route planned for that waypoint
204    dev = np.full(len(data["t_s"]), np.nan)
205    flying = np.isin(data["state"], ["NAVIGATE", "ALIGN_PATH"])
206    for k in np.where(flying)[0]:
207        pts = by_wp.get(int(data["waypoint"][k]))
208        if pts is None or len(pts) < 2:
209            continue
210        p = np.array([data["x"][k], data["y"][k], data["z"][k]])
211        a, b = pts[:-1], pts[1:]
212        ab = b - a
213        u = np.clip(np.einsum("ij,ij->i", p - a, ab) / np.maximum(np.einsum("ij,ij->i", ab, ab), 1e-9), 0, 1)
214        dev[k] = np.min(np.linalg.norm(a + ab * u[:, None] - p, axis=1))
215    return dev
216
217
L199–217

route_deviation(): distance to the planned line

For every sample in NAVIGATE/ALIGN_PATH: the distance from the drone to the nearest point of the planned route of that waypoint. Vectorised point-to-segment distance: project onto each segment (u clamped to [0, 1]), take the minimum. Measured 0.56 m before carrot following, ≤ 0.15 m along the climbs with it.

218def break_wraps(deg):
219    """NaN between samples that jump across +-180 deg, so lines do not draw vertical spikes."""
220    out = deg.copy()
221    jump = np.abs(np.diff(out)) > 180
222    out[1:][jump] = np.nan
223    return out
224
225
L218–225

break_wraps()

Yaw jumps from +180° to −180° when it crosses the back. A line plot would draw a vertical stroke there; inserting NaN breaks the line instead.

226def shade_states(ax, data):
227    t, st = data["t_s"], data["state"]
228    start = 0
229    for k in range(1, len(st) + 1):
230        if k == len(st) or st[k] != st[start]:
231            color = STATE_COLORS.get(st[start])
232            if color:
233                ax.axvspan(t[start], t[min(k, len(t) - 1)], color=color, alpha=0.35, lw=0)
234            start = k
235

shade_states()

Colours the background by state (takeoff blue, align orange, task green, landing red), merging consecutive rows with the same state into one span.

238def plot_topview(ax, data, legs, mission, boxes, safety, obstacles_xy):
239    ax.scatter(obstacles_xy[:, 0], obstacles_xy[:, 1], s=0.3, c="0.55", label="map (0.3-4 m)")
240    for b in boxes:
241        xy = box_corners_xy(b)
242        ax.fill(xy[:, 0], xy[:, 1], color="tab:red", alpha=0.25)
243        ax.plot(xy[:, 0], xy[:, 1], color="tab:red", lw=1.2)
244        grown = box_corners_xy([b[0], b[1], b[2], b[3] + 2 * safety, b[4] + 2 * safety, b[5], b[6]])
245        ax.plot(grown[:, 0], grown[:, 1], color="tab:red", lw=0.8, ls="--")
246    ax.plot([], [], color="tab:red", ls="--", lw=0.8, label=f"rack + safety radius {safety:.2f} m")
247    for n, leg in enumerate(legs.values()):
248        p = leg["pts"]
249        ax.plot(p[:, 0], p[:, 1], "--", color="tab:purple", lw=1.2, marker=".", ms=5,
250                label="planned A* route" if n == 0 else None)
251    air = data["armed"] > 0.5
252    ax.plot(np.where(air, data["x"], np.nan), np.where(air, data["y"], np.nan), color="tab:blue",
253            lw=1.6, label="flown track")
254    # heading arrows every ~3 s while airborne
255    step = max(1, int(round(3.0 / max(np.median(np.diff(data["t_s"])), 1e-3))))
256    k = np.where(air & (data["z"] > 0.3))[0][::step]
257    yaw = np.radians(data["yaw_deg"][k])
258    ax.quiver(data["x"][k], data["y"][k], np.cos(yaw), np.sin(yaw), color="tab:blue", scale=25,
259              width=0.003, alpha=0.8)
260    ax.plot(data["x"][0], data["y"][0], "*", color="tab:green", ms=16, label="start")
261    for i, wp in enumerate(mission, start=1):
262        marker = "s" if wp["precision"] == "hard" else "o"
263        ax.plot(wp["x"], wp["y"], marker, ms=10, mfc="gold", mec="k")
264        task = "" if wp["task"] == "-" else f" {wp['task']}"
265        # stack labels of waypoints that share (almost) the same x, y
266        same = sum(1 for p in mission[:i - 1] if math.hypot(p["x"] - wp["x"], p["y"] - wp["y"]) < 0.6)
267        ax.annotate(f"{i}{task}\nz={wp['z']:.2f}", (wp["x"], wp["y"]), xytext=(6, 6 - 26 * same),
268                    textcoords="offset points", fontsize=8,
269                    bbox=dict(boxstyle="round,pad=0.2", fc="white", alpha=0.8))
270    ax.plot([], [], "s", mfc="gold", mec="k", label="hard waypoint")
271    ax.plot([], [], "o", mfc="gold", mec="k", label="soft waypoint")
272    ax.set_aspect("equal")
273    ax.set_xlabel("x [m] (map)")
274    ax.set_ylabel("y [m] (map)")
275    ax.set_title("Top view: planned vs flown (arrows = drone heading)")
276    ax.grid(True, alpha=0.4)
277    ax.legend(loc="upper right", fontsize=7, framealpha=0.9)
278
279
L238–279

plot_topview()

  • Grey dots: map points between 0.3 and 4 m height (walls, pillars).
  • Racks filled red; dashed outline = rack grown by the safety radius.
  • Purple dashed: every planned A* route; blue: flown track while armed.
  • Arrows every ~3 s: drone heading, showing that it faces the direction of travel.
  • Waypoints: square = hard, circle = soft, with task and z; labels of waypoints at the same place are stacked.
280def main():
281    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Plot an A1.2 mission run.")
282    ap.add_argument("run_dir", nargs="?", help="run folder (default: newest in log/a1_2_runs)")

Entry point

213D route viewer (view_3d_a1_2.py)

view_3d_a1_2.py · 453 lines · 22 explained blocks

An interactive 3D window built with VTK (the visualisation library behind ParaView, also used by RViz-like tools). It shows the hangar as voxels and the planned route as a glowing green tube with a travelling light. It is a visual aid; the numbers come from plot_a1_2.py.

1#!/usr/bin/env python3
2"""
3view_3d_a1_2.py -- interactive 3D voxel view of one A1.2 mission run (VTK).
4
5Shows the hangar as voxels (walls, pillars, racks), the planned A* route as a glowing green
6tube with a translucent safety-radius corridor around it, a green light that travels along the
7route (and lights up the voxels it passes), the flown track and the mission waypoints.
8
9Usage:
10    python3 view_3d_a1_2.py                       # newest run in log/a1_2_runs
11    python3 view_3d_a1_2.py log/a1_2_runs/<run>
12    python3 view_3d_a1_2.py RUN --screenshot x.png  # render one image and exit
13
14Mouse: left drag rotate, right drag / wheel zoom, middle drag pan.
15Keys:  space play/pause the light   c roof on/off   +/- hide walls above a lower/higher height
16       i planner occupancy (inflated) on/off   f flown track on/off   r reset camera   q quit
17"""
18

L19–36

Imports, shared loaders, constants

Imports the loaders of plot_a1_2.py as P, so both tools read the logs in exactly the same way (the script folder is put on sys.path first). Constants: voxel size 0.15 m (same as the planner), roof height 5 m, speed of the travelling light, the green colour.

37def load_pcd_binary_xyz(path):
38    with open(path, "rb") as f:
39        header = {}
40        while True:
41            line = f.readline().decode("ascii", "ignore").strip()
42            key, _, value = line.partition(" ")
43            header[key] = value
44            if key == "DATA":
45                break
46        if header["DATA"] != "binary" or header.get("FIELDS") != "x y z":
47            return None
48        n = int(header["POINTS"])
49        return np.frombuffer(f.read(n * 12), dtype=np.float32).reshape(n, 3)
50
51
L37–51

load_pcd_binary_xyz()

Reads the planner's inflated occupancy file: parse the text header up to DATA binary, then the rest is POINTS × 3 32-bit floats, read in one go with np.frombuffer.

52def voxelize(points, size=VOXEL):
53    idx = np.unique(np.floor(points / size).astype(np.int32), axis=0)
54    return (idx + 0.5) * size
55
56
L52–56

voxelize()

floor(p / 0.15) = integer voxel index of each point; np.unique keeps one per voxel; (index + 0.5) · size = voxel centre. This reproduces the planner's voxel map.

57def box_solid_points(box, step=VOXEL):
58    cx, cy, cz, sx, sy, sz, yaw = box
59    g = np.stack(np.meshgrid(*[np.arange(-s / 2 + step / 2, s / 2, step) for s in (sx, sy, sz)],
60                             indexing="ij"), axis=-1).reshape(-1, 3)
61    c, s = math.cos(yaw), math.sin(yaw)
62    return np.stack([cx + c * g[:, 0] - s * g[:, 1], cy + s * g[:, 0] + c * g[:, 1], cz + g[:, 2]], axis=1)
63
64
L57–64

box_solid_points()

A 3D grid of points filling a rotated rack box: solid, like the planner's addBox().

65def route_polyline(data, legs, mission):
66    """Planned route in flight order, including the vertical takeoff, landtakeoff and landing."""
67    pts = []
68    air = P.airborne_mask(data)
69    if air.any():
70        k = np.argmax(air)
71        pts.append((data["x"][k], data["y"][k], 0.0))
72    for leg in legs.values():
73        wp = mission[leg["waypoint"] - 1] if 0 < leg["waypoint"] <= len(mission) else None
74        pts.extend(map(tuple, leg["pts"]))
75        if wp and wp["task"] in ("landtakeoff", "land"):
76            pts.append((wp["x"], wp["y"], 0.0))
77            if wp["task"] == "landtakeoff":
78                pts.append((wp["x"], wp["y"], wp["z"]))
79    clean = [pts[0]] if pts else []
80    for p in pts[1:]:
81        if math.dist(p, clean[-1]) > 1e-3:
82            clean.append(p)
83    return np.array(clean)
84
85
L65–85

route_polyline(): the whole flight as one line

Joins the planned legs in order and adds the vertical parts that are not A* routes: from the floor at takeoff, down and up at a landtakeoff, down at the final land. Duplicate consecutive points are removed (they would give zero-length segments).

L123–128

Renderer

A renderer draws a scene of actors into a window. Gradient dark background.

129    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ voxels
130    def voxel_poly(centers, colors_rgb):
131        poly = vtk.vtkPolyData()
132        pts = vtk.vtkPoints()
133        pts.SetData(numpy_to_vtk(np.ascontiguousarray(centers, dtype=np.float32).reshape(-1, 3), deep=True))
134        poly.SetPoints(pts)
135        col = numpy_to_vtk(np.ascontiguousarray(colors_rgb, dtype=np.uint8).reshape(-1, 3), deep=True)
136        col.SetName("rgb")
137        poly.GetPointData().SetScalars(col)
138        return poly
139
140    def voxel_actor(centers, colors_rgb, size=VOXEL * 0.92, opacity=1.0):
141        poly = voxel_poly(centers, colors_rgb)
142        cube = vtk.vtkCubeSource()
143        cube.SetXLength(size)
144        cube.SetYLength(size)
145        cube.SetZLength(size)
146        mapper = vtk.vtkGlyph3DMapper()
147        mapper.SetInputData(poly)
148        mapper.SetSourceConnection(cube.GetOutputPort())
149        mapper.ScalingOff()
150        mapper.SetScalarModeToUsePointData()
151        actor = vtk.vtkActor()
152        actor.SetMapper(mapper)
153        actor.GetProperty().SetOpacity(opacity)
154        actor.GetProperty().SetAmbient(0.25)
155        actor.GetProperty().SetDiffuse(0.8)
156        return actor
157
L129–157

Voxels with GPU instancing

voxel_poly: the centres become a VTK point set with an RGB colour per point (numpy_to_vtk copies numpy arrays into VTK).

voxel_actor: vtkGlyph3DMapper draws one small cube at every point on the GPU (instancing). That is how 60 000+ voxels stay interactive. The cubes are 92 % of a voxel, so gaps show the grid.

158    def height_colors(c, base, top):
159        t = np.clip((c[:, 2] - 0.0) / 5.0, 0, 1)[:, None]
160        return (np.array(base) * (1 - t) + np.array(top) * t)
161
162    # Walls/pillars/roof are one actor; voxels above the "cut" height are hidden so you can look in.
163    wall_colors = height_colors(walls, (70, 85, 110), (175, 185, 205))
164    wall_actor = voxel_actor(walls, wall_colors)
165    view = {"cut": args.cut if args.cut is not None else CEILING_Z}
166
167    def apply_cut():
168        keep = walls[:, 2] <= view["cut"]
169        wall_actor.GetMapper().SetInputData(voxel_poly(walls[keep], wall_colors[keep]))
170
171    apply_cut()
172    rack_actor = voxel_actor(racks, height_colors(racks, (150, 70, 40), (215, 120, 70)))
173    for a in (wall_actor, rack_actor):
174        renderer.AddActor(a)
175
L158–175

Colours and wall height cut

Colour blends from dark (floor) to light (5 m), so height is readable. Walls are one actor whose input is swapped by apply_cut(): only voxels below the cut height are shown. Keys +/-/c change it. Racks are a separate, orange actor.

176    floor = vtk.vtkPlaneSource()
177    floor.SetOrigin(lo[0], lo[1], 0)
178    floor.SetPoint1(hi[0], lo[1], 0)
179    floor.SetPoint2(lo[0], hi[1], 0)
180    floor.SetResolution(int(hi[0] - lo[0]), int(hi[1] - lo[1]))
181    fm = vtk.vtkPolyDataMapper()
182    fm.SetInputConnection(floor.GetOutputPort())
183    floor_actor = vtk.vtkActor()
184    floor_actor.SetMapper(fm)
185    floor_actor.GetProperty().SetColor(0.20, 0.22, 0.26)
186    floor_actor.GetProperty().SetEdgeColor(0.30, 0.33, 0.38)
187    floor_actor.GetProperty().EdgeVisibilityOn()
188    renderer.AddActor(floor_actor)

Floor grid

A flat plane at z = 0 with a 1 m grid drawn as edges, instead of thousands of floor voxels.

L190–197

Planner occupancy (key i)

The inflated occupancy from the planner (the space A* considers blocked), as small faint red cubes, hidden by default.

198    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ lines
199    def tube_actor(points, radius, color, opacity=1.0, glow=False):
200        pts = vtk.vtkPoints()
201        for p in points:
202            pts.InsertNextPoint(*p)
203        line = vtk.vtkPolyLine()
204        line.GetPointIds().SetNumberOfIds(len(points))
205        for i in range(len(points)):
206            line.GetPointIds().SetId(i, i)
207        cells = vtk.vtkCellArray()
208        cells.InsertNextCell(line)
209        poly = vtk.vtkPolyData()
210        poly.SetPoints(pts)
211        poly.SetLines(cells)
212        tube = vtk.vtkTubeFilter()
213        tube.SetInputData(poly)
214        tube.SetRadius(radius)
215        tube.SetNumberOfSides(16)
216        tube.CappingOn()
217        m = vtk.vtkPolyDataMapper()
218        m.SetInputConnection(tube.GetOutputPort())
219        a = vtk.vtkActor()
220        a.SetMapper(m)
221        prop = a.GetProperty()
222        prop.SetColor(*color)
223        prop.SetOpacity(opacity)
224        if glow:
225            prop.SetAmbient(1.0)
226            prop.SetDiffuse(0.0)
227            prop.SetSpecular(0.0)
228        return a

Tubes

tube_actor: builds a polyline from points and wraps it in a vtkTubeFilter (a 3D tube of given radius). glow=True: ambient light only, so it looks self-lit.

L285–324

The travelling green light

A bright core sphere, a translucent halo and a positional VTK light (green, attenuated with distance) all at the same point. The light really illuminates the voxels near it, which makes the route easy to follow in 3D. A headlight lights the rest.

325    if len(route) >= 2:
326        seg = np.linalg.norm(np.diff(route, axis=0), axis=1)
327        cum = np.concatenate([[0], np.cumsum(seg)])
328    else:
329        cum = np.array([0.0])
330    state = {"s": 0.0, "playing": True}
331
332    def place_light():
333        if len(route) < 2:
334            return
335        s = state["s"] % cum[-1]
336        k = min(np.searchsorted(cum, s, side="right") - 1, len(seg) - 1)
337        u = (s - cum[k]) / max(seg[k], 1e-9)
338        p = route[k] + (route[k + 1] - route[k]) * u
339        orb.SetPosition(*p)
340        halo.SetPosition(*p)
341        green_light.SetPosition(*p)
342        green_light.SetFocalPoint(p[0], p[1], p[2] - 1.0)
343
344    place_light()
345    if len(route) < 2:                       # nothing to travel along (e.g. --routes-only)
346        orb.VisibilityOff()
347        halo.VisibilityOff()
348        green_light.SetIntensity(0.0)
349
L325–349

Moving the light along the route

Cumulative segment lengths cum turn "distance travelled s" into a segment index (searchsorted) and a fraction u; the position is interpolated. s wraps around (% total), so the light loops.

Screenshot mode

406    interactor = vtk.vtkRenderWindowInteractor()
407    interactor.SetRenderWindow(window)
408    style = vtk.vtkInteractorStyleTrackballCamera()
409    interactor.SetInteractorStyle(style)
410
411    # An observer on the style's CharEvent replaces VTK's built-in keys (w wireframe, f fly-to, ...).
412    def on_key(_obj, _event):
413        key = interactor.GetKeySym().lower()
414        if key == "space":
415            state["playing"] = not state["playing"]
416        elif key == "c":
417            view["cut"] = 99.0 if view["cut"] < 50 else CEILING_Z
418            apply_cut()
419        elif key in ("plus", "equal", "kp_add"):
420            view["cut"] = min(view["cut"] + 0.25, 99.0) if view["cut"] < 50 else CEILING_Z
421            apply_cut()
422        elif key in ("minus", "kp_subtract"):
423            view["cut"] = max((view["cut"] if view["cut"] < 50 else hi[2]) - 0.25, 0.25)
424            apply_cut()
425        elif key == "i" and occ_actor is not None:
426            occ_actor.SetVisibility(not occ_actor.GetVisibility())
427        elif key == "f" and flown_actor is not None:
428            flown_actor.SetVisibility(not flown_actor.GetVisibility())
429        elif key == "r":
430            reset_camera()
431        elif key in ("q", "escape"):
432            window.Finalize()
433            interactor.TerminateApp()
434            return
435        update_info()
436        window.Render()
437
438    def on_timer(_obj, _event):
439        if state["playing"] and total > 0:
440            state["s"] += LIGHT_SPEED * 0.04
441            place_light()
442            window.Render()
443
444    style.AddObserver("CharEvent", on_key)
445    interactor.Initialize()
446    interactor.AddObserver("TimerEvent", on_timer)
447    interactor.CreateRepeatingTimer(40)
448    window.Render()
449    interactor.Start()
450
451
L406–451

Interaction: mouse, keys, timer

  • Trackball camera style: drag to rotate, wheel to zoom, middle button to pan.
  • An observer on the style's CharEvent replaces VTK's built-in keys (otherwise w = wireframe, f = fly-to...) with ours.
  • A repeating 40 ms timer moves the light by speed × 0.04 s and re-renders.
  • Start() runs the event loop until the window is closed or q.

A1.3 — outdoor GPS mission (branch A1_3)

22Outdoor mission node (A1.3)

src/lrs_psenak_valko/src/outdoor_mission_node.cpp (branch A1_3) · 1117 lines · 54 explained blocks

The A1.3 program (branch A1_3). One ROS 2 node flies the outdoor mission: wait for a good GPS fix and capture the origin, take off to 5 m, fly a 5 × 5 m square with the nose locked on a point of interest (POI), fly a figure-8 as a continuous trajectory with the nose along the tangent, return and land.

Differences from A1.2 worth remembering: single-threaded executor (no mutex), /mavros/setpoint_raw/local with position + velocity + yaw, a 30 Hz timer, three ways out of a mission (ABORTING, STOPPED, FAULT), and all geometry maths in outdoor_math.h.

1// outdoor_mission_node.cpp -- A1.3 outdoor GPS mission.
2//
3//   WAIT_GPS -> SET_MODE -> ARM -> TAKEOFF -> TO_START -> SQUARE -> TO_CENTER
4//            -> FIGURE8 -> RETURN -> LAND -> DONE
5//   any flight state --(abort service / geofence / tracking error)--> ABORTING -> ABORTED
6//   any flight state --(RC mode change / MAVROS drop-out)-----------> STOPPED (no setpoints!)
7//
8// Everything is RELATIVE to the launch point captured before arming (mean of the stable
9// GPS fixes paired with the local pose at that instant) -- no absolute coordinates.
10//
11// Frames / angles (see outdoor_math.h):  local ENU, yaw 0 = East, CCW positive.
12// Altitude: we COMMAND local z = z_launch + flight_altitude and CHECK both
13// (local z - z_launch) and /mavros/global_position/rel_alt.
14//
15// POI: static ground point, given EITHER as lat/lon (poi_lat_deg + poi_lon_deg, converted
16// geodetic -> local ENU after the origin is captured) OR as an ENU offset from the launch
17// point (poi_east_m, poi_north_m).  Only the HORIZONTAL bearing is controlled (yaw); the
18// camera on this airframe is fixed and looks forward, so the required downward look angle
19// (poi_pitch_deg) is only logged, not commanded.
20//
21// Heading policy:
22//   * transit legs (TO_START, TO_CENTER, RETURN): nose along the direction of travel
23//     until close to the destination, then the final turn (POI bearing / figure-8 tangent);
24//   * SQUARE: nose on the POI (continuously recomputed, with a small look-ahead);
25//   * FIGURE8: nose on the path tangent (unwrapped).
26//
27// Single-threaded executor on purpose: all callbacks and the control timer run in
28// one thread, so there are no data races and no mutexes to explain.
29
L1–29

Header: the whole design in one comment

  • The state sequence, and the two ways of leaving a mission: ABORTING (we switch the vehicle to a safe mode) and STOPPED (the pilot or the link took over, so we only stop sending).
  • Everything is relative to the launch point (mean of stable GPS fixes): no hard-coded lat/lon.
  • Frames: local ENU, yaw 0 = East, CCW+. Altitude is commanded as local z = launch z + 5 m and checked against rel_alt.
  • POI as lat/lon or as an offset; only yaw is controlled (fixed camera), the downward look angle is logged.
  • Heading policy per phase, and why one thread: no data races, no mutex.

67class OutdoorMissionNode final : public rclcpp::Node
68{
69public:
70  OutdoorMissionNode() : Node("outdoor_mission_node")
71  {
72    declareParameters();
73    validateParameters();
74    checkMissionEnvelope();
75    createInterfaces();
76    openLog();
77
78    RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(),
79      "Parameters: altitude=%.2f m, square=%.2f m, figure-8 A=%.2f m omega=%.3f rad/s (lap %.1f s, "
80      "peak speed %.2f m/s), yaw look-ahead=%.2f s",
81      p_.flight_altitude, p_.square_side, p_.amplitude, p_.omega, 2.0 * outdoor::kPi / p_.omega,
82      outdoor::figure8PeakSpeed(p_.amplitude, p_.omega), p_.yaw_lead_s);
83    if (poiFromGeodetic())
84      RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "POI given as lat/lon: %.7f, %.7f (converted after the origin is captured)",
85        p_.poi_lat, p_.poi_lon);
86    else
87      RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "POI given as offset from launch: (E %.2f, N %.2f) m", p_.poi_east, p_.poi_north);
88    RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "Setpoints: position%s + yaw; POI yaw from %s position",
89      p_.velocity_feedforward ? " + velocity feed-forward" : " only", p_.poi_yaw_from_measured ? "MEASURED" : "commanded");
90    transition(State::WAIT_GPS);
91  }

123  struct Params
124  {
125    // mission geometry (fixed by the assignment; exposed so they are documented, not buried)
126    double flight_altitude = 5.0, square_side = 5.0, amplitude = 3.0;
127    double omega = 0.30, laps = 1.0, ramp_s = 3.0;
128    double poi_east = 0.0, poi_north = 0.0;          // POI offset from the LAUNCH point [m]
129    double poi_lat = std::numeric_limits<double>::quiet_NaN();   // POI as lat/lon (overrides the offset)
130    double poi_lon = std::numeric_limits<double>::quiet_NaN();
131    bool close_square = true;
132    // motion
133    double control_rate_hz = 30.0, max_speed = 1.5, square_speed = 0.5;
134    double waypoint_tol = 0.3, max_lead = 1.0, yaw_rate_limit_deg = 45.0;
135    double max_accel = 0.5;                // carrot acceleration/braking limit (no velocity steps at corners)
136    double yaw_lead_s = 0.5;              // look-ahead that compensates the yaw controller lag
137    double transit_turn_radius = 0.7;      // closer than this to the goal: final turn instead of travel bearing
138    double figure8_close_timeout = 10.0;   // max wait for the drone to reach the end of the lap
139    bool velocity_feedforward = true;      // send velocity together with position (removes the ~1-2 s lag)
140    bool poi_yaw_from_measured = true;     // bearing to POI from the MEASURED position (not the carrot)
141    // takeoff
142    double takeoff_tol = 0.2, stabilize_time = 2.0, stabilize_speed = 0.15;
143    // GPS gate
144    int min_satellites = 8;
145    double max_h_std = 2.0, fix_stable_s = 3.0;
146    // safety
147    double max_horizontal = 4.0, max_altitude = 5.5, max_tracking_error = 2.0;
148    std::string abort_mode = "LAND";
149    // timeouts
150    double handshake_timeout = 60.0, takeoff_timeout = 40.0, segment_timeout = 90.0;
151    double landing_timeout = 90.0, service_timeout = 5.0, telemetry_timeout = 1.0;
152    // output
153    std::string log_csv;
154    double status_period = 1.0;
155  };
156
157  // ----------------------------------------------------------- parameters
L123–157

All parameters in one struct

  • Geometry fixed by the assignment (5 m altitude, 5 m square, A = 3 m), but exposed as parameters so they are documented.
  • Figure-8: ω = 0.30 rad/s, 1 lap, 3 s ramp.
  • Motion: 30 Hz control, 1.5 m/s max, 0.5 m/s on the square, 0.5 m/s² carrot acceleration, yaw look-ahead 0.5 s, velocity feed-forward on.
  • GPS gate: ≥ 8 satellites, σ ≤ 2 m, stable 3 s. Geofence 4 m / 5.5 m, tracking error 2 m. Timeouts.

212  void validateParameters() const
213  {
214    auto bad = [](const std::string& m) { throw std::invalid_argument("invalid parameter: " + m); };
215    if (p_.flight_altitude <= 0.5) bad("flight_altitude_m must be > 0.5");
216    if (p_.square_side <= 0.0 || p_.amplitude <= 0.0) bad("square_side_m and figure8_amplitude_m must be > 0");
217    if (p_.omega <= 0.0 || p_.laps <= 0.0 || p_.ramp_s < 0.0) bad("figure8_omega/laps must be > 0, ramp >= 0");
218    if (p_.laps * 2.0 * outdoor::kPi / p_.omega < p_.ramp_s) bad("figure8_ramp_s longer than the lap itself");
219    if (p_.max_accel <= 0.0) bad("max_accel_mps2 must be > 0");
220    if (p_.control_rate_hz < 10.0 || p_.control_rate_hz > 50.0) bad("control_rate_hz must be in [10, 50]");
221    if (p_.max_speed <= 0.0 || p_.square_speed <= 0.0 || p_.square_speed > p_.max_speed)
222      bad("0 < square_speed_mps <= max_speed_mps");
223    if (p_.waypoint_tol <= 0.0 || p_.max_lead <= p_.waypoint_tol) bad("need 0 < waypoint_tolerance < max_lead");
224    if (p_.yaw_rate_limit_deg <= 0.0) bad("yaw_rate_limit_deg_s must be > 0");
225    if (p_.yaw_lead_s < 0.0 || p_.yaw_lead_s > 2.0) bad("yaw_lead_s must be in [0, 2]");
226    if (p_.transit_turn_radius <= 0.0 || p_.figure8_close_timeout < 0.0) bad("transit_turn_radius_m / figure8_close_timeout_s");
227    if (p_.takeoff_tol <= 0.0 || p_.stabilize_time <= 0.0 || p_.stabilize_speed <= 0.0) bad("takeoff/stabilize values");
228    if (p_.min_satellites < 0 || p_.max_h_std <= 0.0 || p_.fix_stable_s < 0.0) bad("GPS gate values");
229    if (p_.max_horizontal <= 0.0 || p_.max_altitude <= 0.0 || p_.max_tracking_error <= 0.0) bad("safety limits");
230    if (p_.abort_mode != "LAND" && p_.abort_mode != "RTL" && p_.abort_mode != "LOITER" && p_.abort_mode != "BRAKE")
231      bad("abort_mode must be one of LAND, RTL, LOITER, BRAKE");
232    if (std::isfinite(p_.poi_lat) != std::isfinite(p_.poi_lon)) bad("poi_lat_deg and poi_lon_deg must be given together");
233    if (poiFromGeodetic() && (std::fabs(p_.poi_lat) > 90.0 || std::fabs(p_.poi_lon) > 180.0)) bad("poi lat/lon out of range");
234    const double peak = outdoor::figure8PeakSpeed(p_.amplitude, p_.omega);
235    if (peak > p_.max_speed)
236      bad("figure8_omega_rad_s=" + std::to_string(p_.omega) + " gives peak speed " + std::to_string(peak) +
237          " m/s > max_speed_mps; maximum omega is " +
238          std::to_string(outdoor::figure8MaxOmega(p_.amplitude, p_.max_speed)));
239  }
240
L212–240

validateParameters(): refuse bad numbers

Throws for impossible values. Most important: the figure-8 peak speed A·ω·√2 must not exceed max_speed; the error message even says the largest allowed ω.

241  /// Pre-flight geofence arithmetic in the constructor (POI as ENU offset).  When the POI is
242  /// given as lat/lon its position is only known after the origin is captured, so the same
243  /// check is repeated in captureOrigin().
244  void checkMissionEnvelope() const
245  {
246    if (p_.flight_altitude > p_.max_altitude)
247      throw std::invalid_argument("flight_altitude_m exceeds max_altitude_m");
248    if (poiFromGeodetic()) {
249      RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "Geofence check postponed until the POI is converted to local coordinates");
250      return;
251    }
252    const double r = outdoor::missionMaxRadius({p_.poi_east, p_.poi_north}, p_.square_side, p_.amplitude);
253    RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(),
254      "Mission envelope: max horizontal distance from launch = %.2f m (limit %.2f m), "
255      "max altitude = %.2f m (limit %.2f m)", r, p_.max_horizontal, p_.flight_altitude, p_.max_altitude);
256    if (r > p_.max_horizontal)
257      throw std::invalid_argument("mission reaches " + std::to_string(r) + " m from launch, limit is " +
258                                  std::to_string(p_.max_horizontal) + " m (move the POI or raise the limit)");
259  }
260
261  // ----------------------------------------------------------- interfaces
L241–261

checkMissionEnvelope(): geofence before flight

Computes the largest distance from launch of anything the mission visits (corners and the whole figure-8). Over the limit: the node does not start. With a lat/lon POI the check is repeated after the origin is known.

L262–283

Subscriptions

All telemetry with SensorDataQoS (best effort, compatible with MAVROS). Callbacks only store the message and the time (single thread, so no mutex). The GPS callback also counts messages (fix_seq_) so the origin averaging adds each fix only once.

325  void transition(State next)
326  {
327    RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "State: %s -> %s", stateName(state_), stateName(next));
328    state_ = next;
329    state_started_ = SteadyClock::now();
330    last_wait_log_ = state_started_;
331    stable_since_valid_ = false;
332    carrot_speed_ = 0.0;            // every phase starts (and the previous one ended) at rest
333    if (next == State::FIGURE8) { f8_started_ = state_started_; f8_done_ = false; }
334    std_msgs::msg::String msg;
335    msg.data = stateName(next);
336    state_pub_->publish(msg);
337  }

L325–338

transition()

Logs State: A -> B, stores time, resets the "stable" timer and the carrot speed (every phase starts at rest), starts the figure-8 clock, publishes the state.

339  static bool isStreaming(State s)
340  {
341    return s == State::TAKEOFF || s == State::TO_START || s == State::SQUARE || s == State::TO_CENTER ||
342           s == State::FIGURE8 || s == State::RETURN;
343  }
344  static bool isTerminal(State s)
345  {
346    return s == State::DONE || s == State::ABORTED || s == State::STOPPED || s == State::FAULT;
347  }
348  bool airborne() const { return isStreaming(state_) || state_ == State::LAND; }
349
350  double secondsIn(State) const
351  {
352    return std::chrono::duration<double>(SteadyClock::now() - state_started_).count();
353  }

L339–354

Phase helpers

isStreaming: states in which setpoints are sent (TAKEOFF is listed but excluded when publishing). isTerminal: nothing more to do. airborne: flying, so an abort must put the vehicle into a safe mode.

355  void fault(const std::string& why)
356  {
357    RCLCPP_ERROR(get_logger(), "FAULT in %s: %s", stateName(state_), why.c_str());
358    transition(State::FAULT);
359  }
360
361  /// Leave the mission and put the vehicle in a safe mode (airborne), or just stop (on ground).
362  void beginAbort(const std::string& why)
363  {
364    RCLCPP_ERROR(get_logger(), "ABORT in %s: %s -> switching to %s", stateName(state_), why.c_str(),
365      p_.abort_mode.c_str());
366    if (airborne()) transition(State::ABORTING);
367    else transition(State::FAULT);
368  }
369
370  /// Do NOT fight the pilot: stop publishing setpoints, change nothing else.
371  void stopSilently(const std::string& why)
372  {
373    RCLCPP_ERROR(get_logger(), "STOPPED in %s: %s. No more setpoints will be sent.", stateName(state_), why.c_str());
374    transition(State::STOPPED);
375  }
376
L355–376

Three ways out

  • fault: error on the ground: just stop.
  • beginAbort: airborne: switch to abort_mode (LAND by default).
  • stopSilently: the pilot or link took over: send nothing any more, change nothing ("do not fight the pilot").

378  {

381    last_wait_log_ = t;

Rate-limited waiting log

At most every 5 s.

385  // ---------------------------------------------------- service helpers
386  uint64_t beginService()
387  {
388    service_pending_ = true;
389    service_started_ = SteadyClock::now();
390    return ++service_id_;
391  }
392  bool endService(uint64_t id)
393  {
394    if (!service_pending_ || id != service_id_) return false;
395    service_pending_ = false;
396    return true;
397  }
398  bool canCallService()
399  {
400    const auto t = SteadyClock::now();
401    if (service_pending_) return false;
402    if (std::chrono::duration<double>(t - last_service_try_).count() < 1.0) return false;
403    last_service_try_ = t;
404    return true;
405  }
406
L385–406

Service helpers

Same idea as A1.2: one call pending at a time, an id per call, plus canCallService that allows at most one attempt per second.

407  void trySetMode(const std::string& mode)
408  {
409    if (!mode_client_->service_is_ready() || !canCallService()) return;
410    const uint64_t id = beginService();
411    auto req = std::make_shared<mavros_msgs::srv::SetMode::Request>();
412    req->custom_mode = mode;
413    mode_client_->async_send_request(req,
414      [this, id, mode](rclcpp::Client<mavros_msgs::srv::SetMode>::SharedFuture f) {
415        if (!endService(id)) return;
416        const auto r = f.get();
417        if (!r || !r->mode_sent) RCLCPP_ERROR(get_logger(), "MAVROS rejected mode %s, will retry", mode.c_str());
418      });
419  }
420
421  void tryArm()
422  {
423    if (!arm_client_->service_is_ready() || !canCallService()) return;
424    const uint64_t id = beginService();
425    auto req = std::make_shared<mavros_msgs::srv::CommandBool::Request>();
426    req->value = true;
427    arm_client_->async_send_request(req,
428      [this, id](rclcpp::Client<mavros_msgs::srv::CommandBool>::SharedFuture f) {
429        if (!endService(id)) return;
430        const auto r = f.get();
431        if (!r || !r->success) RCLCPP_ERROR(get_logger(), "arming rejected (pre-arm checks?), will retry");
432      });
433  }
434
L407–434

trySetMode() / tryArm()

Asynchronous calls; refusals are logged and simply retried on a later tick.

435  void tryTakeoff()
436  {
437    if (takeoff_requested_ || !takeoff_client_->service_is_ready() || !canCallService()) return;
438    const uint64_t id = beginService();
439    takeoff_requested_ = true;
440    auto req = std::make_shared<mavros_msgs::srv::CommandTOL::Request>();
441    req->altitude = static_cast<float>(p_.flight_altitude);   // metres ABOVE the launch point
442    // lat/lon are float32 (~0.4 m resolution at 48 deg) and Copter ignores them for a guided
443    // takeoff, so they are left at 0.  The MAVLink yaw field is NED/compass degrees, not our ENU
444    // yaw, so "no yaw requested" (NaN) is sent instead of a value in the wrong convention.
445    req->yaw = std::nanf("");
446    takeoff_client_->async_send_request(req,
447      [this, id](rclcpp::Client<mavros_msgs::srv::CommandTOL>::SharedFuture f) {
448        if (!endService(id)) return;
449        const auto r = f.get();
450        if (!r || !r->success) {
451          RCLCPP_ERROR(get_logger(), "takeoff command rejected, will retry");
452          takeoff_requested_ = false;
453        } else {
454          takeoff_acknowledged_ = true;
455          RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "MAVROS accepted takeoff to %.2f m above launch", p_.flight_altitude);
456        }
457      });
458  }
459
460  // ----------------------------------------------------- telemetry views
L435–460

tryTakeoff()

Altitude = 5 m above launch. Lat/lon left 0 (float32 would lose ~0.4 m; Copter ignores them in GUIDED). Yaw = NaN, because the MAVLink yaw field is a compass angle (NED), not our ENU yaw: sending our value would be in the wrong convention.

461  double poseYaw() const
462  {
463    const auto& q = pose_.pose.orientation;
464    return std::atan2(2.0 * (q.w * q.z + q.x * q.y), 1.0 - 2.0 * (q.y * q.y + q.z * q.z));
465  }
466  Vec3 posePos() const { return {pose_.pose.position.x, pose_.pose.position.y, pose_.pose.position.z}; }
467  double speed() const
468  {
469    const auto& v = vel_.twist.linear;
470    return std::sqrt(v.x * v.x + v.y * v.y + v.z * v.z);
471  }
472  bool fresh(SteadyClock::time_point t, double max_age_s) const
473  {
474    return std::chrono::duration<double>(SteadyClock::now() - t).count() <= max_age_s;
475  }

477  /// Downward look angle (deg below the horizon) from the drone to the POI on the ground.
478  /// Only logged: the camera is fixed and the drone is not pitched to look down.
479  double poiPitchDeg() const
480  {
481    const Vec3 p = posePos();
482    return std::atan2(p.z - launch_.z, outdoor::dist2({p.x, p.y}, poi_)) * outdoor::kRad2Deg;
483  }

L477–484

poiPitchDeg()

Angle below the horizon from the drone to the POI: atan2(height, horizontal distance). Only logged (54–64° in flight), because the camera is fixed.

485  /// "Usable fix": status >= FIX, finite lat/lon, enough satellites, small enough covariance.
486  bool fixGood(std::string* why = nullptr) const
487  {
488    auto no = [&](const std::string& s) { if (why) *why = s; return false; };
489    if (!have_fix_ || !fresh(fix_time_, 2.0)) return no("no fresh NavSatFix");
490    if (fix_.status.status < sensor_msgs::msg::NavSatStatus::STATUS_FIX)
491      return no("fix status " + std::to_string(fix_.status.status) + " (NO_FIX)");
492    if (!std::isfinite(fix_.latitude) || !std::isfinite(fix_.longitude)) return no("lat/lon not finite");
493    if (p_.min_satellites > 0) {
494      if (!have_sats_) return no("no satellite count received");
495      if (static_cast<int>(satellites_) < p_.min_satellites)
496        return no("only " + std::to_string(satellites_) + " satellites (need " + std::to_string(p_.min_satellites) + ")");
497    }
498    if (fix_.position_covariance_type != sensor_msgs::msg::NavSatFix::COVARIANCE_TYPE_UNKNOWN) {
499      const double h_std = std::sqrt(std::max(fix_.position_covariance[0], fix_.position_covariance[4]));
500      if (h_std > p_.max_h_std) return no("horizontal std " + std::to_string(h_std) + " m too large");
501    }
502    return true;
503  }
504
L485–504

fixGood(): is the GPS fix usable?

Fresh message, status ≥ FIX, finite lat/lon, enough satellites, and horizontal σ (from the covariance) ≤ 2 m. The reason for "no" is returned for the log.

505  // ----------------------------------------------- origin (fix averaging)
506  void resetFixAveraging()
507  {
508    stable_since_valid_ = false;
509    fix_n_ = 0;
510    sum_lat_ = sum_lon_ = sum_alt_ = 0.0;
511  }
512
513  /// Adds every NEW fix once (the control tick runs faster than the GPS).
514  void accumulateFix()
515  {
516    if (fix_seq_ == last_acc_seq_) return;
517    last_acc_seq_ = fix_seq_;
518    sum_lat_ += fix_.latitude;
519    sum_lon_ += fix_.longitude;
520    sum_alt_ += fix_.altitude;
521    ++fix_n_;
522  }
523
L505–523

Fix averaging

Sums of lat/lon/alt and a count; each new fix (sequence number) is added once, because the 30 Hz tick runs faster than the GPS.

524  // ---------------------------------------------------------- the origin
525  /// Returns false (state already FAULT) when the mission would leave the geofence.
526  bool captureOrigin()
527  {
528    // The vehicle stands still on the ground, so the MEAN of the stable fixes is a better origin
529    // than a single noisy sample (a single fix would put its whole error into every waypoint).
530    const double lat0 = sum_lat_ / fix_n_, lon0 = sum_lon_ / fix_n_, alt0 = sum_alt_ / fix_n_;
531    ltp_ = outdoor::LocalTangentPlane(lat0, lon0, alt0);
532    launch_ = posePos();                 // local pose at the same instant as the fix
533    cmd_yaw_ = poseYaw();
534    origin_valid_ = true;
535
536    if (poiFromGeodetic()) {
537      const Vec3 q = toLocal({p_.poi_lat, p_.poi_lon, alt0});     // geodetic -> local ENU (planning)
538      poi_ = {q.x, q.y};
539    } else {
540      poi_ = {launch_.x + p_.poi_east, launch_.y + p_.poi_north};
541    }
542    corners_ = outdoor::squareCorners(poi_, p_.square_side);
543    z_fly_ = launch_.z + p_.flight_altitude;
544
545    const double r = outdoor::missionMaxRadius({poi_.x - launch_.x, poi_.y - launch_.y}, p_.square_side, p_.amplitude);
546    RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "Mission envelope: max horizontal distance from launch = %.2f m (limit %.2f m)",
547      r, p_.max_horizontal);
548    if (r > p_.max_horizontal) {
549      fault("mission reaches " + std::to_string(r) + " m from launch, limit is " +
550            std::to_string(p_.max_horizontal) + " m");
551      return false;
552    }
553
554    sq_.clear();
555    for (const Vec2& c : corners_) sq_.push_back(c);
556    if (p_.close_square) sq_.push_back(corners_[0]);
557
558    RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(),
559      "ORIGIN (launch point): lat=%.7f lon=%.7f alt(AMSL)=%.2f m (mean of %d fixes) | local ENU=(%.2f, %.2f, %.2f) | yaw=%.1f deg",
560      lat0, lon0, alt0, fix_n_, launch_.x, launch_.y, launch_.z, poseYaw() * outdoor::kRad2Deg);
561    if (have_home_) {
562      const outdoor::Enu h = ltp_.toEnu({home_.geo.latitude, home_.geo.longitude, home_.geo.altitude});
563      RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "MAVROS home: lat=%.7f lon=%.7f alt=%.2f (differs from origin by %.2f m horizontally)",
564        home_.geo.latitude, home_.geo.longitude, home_.geo.altitude, std::hypot(h.e, h.n));
565    }
566    reportGeodeticPlan();
567    publishIntendedPath();
568    return true;
569  }
570
L524–570

captureOrigin(): define the mission

  • Origin = mean of the stable fixes; the local tangent plane is built there; the local pose at that moment is the launch point.
  • POI from lat/lon (geodetic → local) or from the offset. Corners around the POI, flight z = launch z + 5 m.
  • Geofence check again (now with the real POI).
  • The square list (closed: back to W1), log the origin, compare with MAVROS home, print the plan in lat/lon, publish the intended path.
571  Vec3 toLocal(const outdoor::Geodetic& g) const
572  {
573    const outdoor::Enu e = ltp_.toEnu(g);
574    return {launch_.x + e.e, launch_.y + e.n, launch_.z + e.u};
575  }
576  outdoor::Geodetic toGeodetic(const Vec3& local) const
577  {
578    return ltp_.toGeodetic({local.x - launch_.x, local.y - launch_.y, local.z - launch_.z});
579  }

L571–580

toLocal() / toGeodetic()

Tangent-plane ENU offsets plus the launch point = local coordinates, and back. Both directions are required by the assignment.

581  /// Both conversion directions exercised at startup: plan in local -> report as lat/lon -> back.
582  void reportGeodeticPlan()
583  {
584    auto line = [&](const char* name, const Vec2& p) {
585      const outdoor::Geodetic g = toGeodetic({p.x, p.y, z_fly_});
586      const Vec3 back = toLocal(g);
587      RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "  %-3s local=(%.2f, %.2f)  lat=%.7f lon=%.7f  round-trip error=%.2e m",
588        name, p.x, p.y, g.lat_deg, g.lon_deg, std::hypot(back.x - p.x, back.y - p.y));
589    };
590    RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "Planned geometry (flat-Earth tangent plane at the launch point):");
591    line("POI", poi_);
592    line("W1", corners_[0]); line("W2", corners_[1]); line("W3", corners_[2]); line("W4", corners_[3]);
593  }

L581–594

reportGeodeticPlan()

For the POI and every corner: local → lat/lon → local again, printing the round-trip error (~1e-9 m). Proof that both conversions work.

595  void publishIntendedPath()
596  {
597    nav_msgs::msg::Path path;
598    path.header.frame_id = "map";
599    path.header.stamp = now();
600    auto add = [&](double x, double y, double z) {
601      geometry_msgs::msg::PoseStamped ps;
602      ps.header = path.header;
603      ps.pose.position.x = x; ps.pose.position.y = y; ps.pose.position.z = z;
604      ps.pose.orientation.w = 1.0;
605      path.poses.push_back(ps);
606    };
607    for (const Vec2& c : sq_) add(c.x, c.y, z_fly_);
608    for (int i = 0; i <= 200; ++i) {
609      const auto s = outdoor::figure8(p_.amplitude, 2.0 * outdoor::kPi * i / 200.0);
610      add(poi_.x + s.x, poi_.y + s.y, z_fly_);
611    }
612    intended_path_pub_->publish(path);
613  }

L595–614

publishIntendedPath()

The square and 200 points of the figure-8 as a Path for RViz.

615  // ----------------------------------------------------------- motion
616  /// Moves the "carrot" (the position we command) towards `goal` at `speed`, but never lets it
617  /// run more than max_lead ahead of the real drone.  Returns true when the carrot is at the
618  /// goal AND the drone is within waypoint_tol of it.
619  ///
620  /// The carrot's speed follows a trapezoidal profile: it rises by at most max_accel*dt per tick
621  /// and is capped by sqrt(2*max_accel*d), the speed from which it can still stop at the goal.
622  /// Without this the velocity setpoint jumps 0 -> 0.5 m/s at the start of every side and
623  /// 0.5 -> 0 at its end (a 90 deg velocity step at each corner); ArduPilot answers such a step
624  /// with a sharp tilt, which is the "nose dip" visible at the corners of the square.
625  bool flyCarrotTo(const Vec3& goal, double speed, double dt)
626  {
627    const Vec3 pos = posePos();
628    cmd_vel_ = {0.0, 0.0, 0.0};
629    if (outdoor::dist3(pos, carrot_) >= p_.max_lead) carrot_speed_ = 0.0;   // wait for the drone
630    else {
631      const double d = outdoor::dist3(carrot_, goal);
632      if (d > 1e-9) {
633        carrot_speed_ = std::min({speed, carrot_speed_ + p_.max_accel * dt, std::sqrt(2.0 * p_.max_accel * d)});
634        const double step = std::min(carrot_speed_ * dt, d);
635        const double ux = (goal.x - carrot_.x) / d, uy = (goal.y - carrot_.y) / d, uz = (goal.z - carrot_.z) / d;
636        carrot_.x += ux * step;
637        carrot_.y += uy * step;
638        carrot_.z += uz * step;
639        cmd_vel_ = {ux * step / dt, uy * step / dt, uz * step / dt};   // the carrot's own velocity
640      }
641    }
642    cmd_ = carrot_;
643    return outdoor::dist3(carrot_, goal) < 1e-6 && outdoor::dist3(pos, goal) <= p_.waypoint_tol;
644  }
645
L615–645

flyCarrotTo(): smooth carrot

A carrot like in A1.2, plus a trapezoidal speed profile: speed rises by at most max_accel·dt per tick and is capped by √(2·a·d) (the speed from which it can still stop at the goal). The carrot waits while the drone is more than max_lead behind. Its own velocity is sent as feed-forward. Without the profile the velocity jumped 0 ↔ 0.5 m/s at each corner and the drone dipped its nose.

646  void slewYawTo(double target, double dt)
647  {
648    cmd_yaw_ = outdoor::slewAngle(cmd_yaw_, target, p_.yaw_rate_limit_deg * outdoor::kDeg2Rad * dt);
649  }
650
L646–650

slewYawTo()

Turns the commanded yaw towards a target by at most 45°/s (shortest way, kept continuous).

651  /// Transit leg: nose along the direction of travel (rule 4 of the assignment) until the drone
652  /// is within transit_turn_radius of `goal`; from there `final_yaw` is used (the heading the
653  /// next phase needs).  Very close to the goal the bearing is undefined, so the last yaw is held.
654  void updateYawForTransit(const Vec2& goal, double final_yaw, double dt)
655  {
656    const Vec3 p = posePos();
657    const Vec2 here{p.x, p.y};
658    const double d = outdoor::dist2(here, goal);
659    if (d > p_.transit_turn_radius) slewYawTo(outdoor::bearingYaw(here, goal), dt);
660    else slewYawTo(final_yaw, dt);
661  }
662
L651–662

Yaw on transit legs

Nose along the direction to the goal; within transit_turn_radius (0.7 m) of the goal turn to the heading the next phase needs.

663  /// Yaw continuously aimed at the POI, recomputed every tick.
664  /// The bearing is taken from the MEASURED position (poi_yaw_from_measured): the carrot runs
665  /// ahead of the drone by the tracking lag, so a bearing from the carrot points the nose at a
666  /// place the drone is not at.  On top of that the position is extrapolated by yaw_lead_s *
667  /// measured velocity: the yaw controller itself lags the setpoint by roughly that time, and the
668  /// bearing changes fastest in the middle of each side (~speed / distance).  Right at the POI
669  /// the bearing is undefined, so the last yaw is held there.
670  void updateYawToPoi(double dt)
671  {
672    const Vec3 p = posePos();
673    Vec2 from = p_.poi_yaw_from_measured ? Vec2{p.x, p.y} : Vec2{carrot_.x, carrot_.y};
674    if (p_.yaw_lead_s > 0.0 && have_vel_ && fresh(vel_time_, 0.5)) {
675      from.x += vel_.twist.linear.x * p_.yaw_lead_s;     // velocity_local is ENU
676      from.y += vel_.twist.linear.y * p_.yaw_lead_s;
677    }
678    if (outdoor::dist2(from, poi_) > 0.5) slewYawTo(outdoor::bearingYaw(from, poi_), dt);
679  }
680
L663–680

updateYawToPoi(): nose on the POI

Every tick: bearing from the measured position (the carrot is ahead of the drone), shifted by velocity × 0.5 s to compensate the yaw controller lag. Near the POI (< 0.5 m) the bearing is undefined, so the yaw is held. Result: max 3.3° error on the square.

681  void publishSetpoint()
682  {
683    using PT = mavros_msgs::msg::PositionTarget;
684    PT m;
685    m.header.stamp = now();
686    m.header.frame_id = "map";
687    m.coordinate_frame = PT::FRAME_LOCAL_NED;                  // MAVROS converts ENU->NED
688    uint16_t mask = PT::IGNORE_AFX | PT::IGNORE_AFY | PT::IGNORE_AFZ | PT::FORCE | PT::IGNORE_YAW_RATE;
689    m.position.x = cmd_.x; m.position.y = cmd_.y; m.position.z = cmd_.z;
690    if (p_.velocity_feedforward) {
691      // position + velocity: ArduPilot tracks the position and uses the velocity as feed-forward,
692      // so the drone no longer lags behind the setpoint by ~speed / P-gain.
693      m.velocity.x = cmd_vel_.x; m.velocity.y = cmd_vel_.y; m.velocity.z = cmd_vel_.z;
694    } else {
695      mask |= PT::IGNORE_VX | PT::IGNORE_VY | PT::IGNORE_VZ;
696    }
697    m.type_mask = mask;
698    m.yaw = static_cast<float>(cmd_yaw_);                      // ENU yaw, kept continuous
699    setpoint_pub_->publish(m);
700  }
701
702  // ------------------------------------------------------ main control tick
L681–702

publishSetpoint(): PositionTarget

FRAME_LOCAL_NED but MAVROS converts from ENU. The type_mask ignores acceleration and yaw rate (and velocity when feed-forward is off); position, velocity and yaw are used. type_mask must not ignore yaw, or the heading is not controlled.

703  void tick()
704  {
705    const auto t_now = SteadyClock::now();
706    const double dt = std::clamp(std::chrono::duration<double>(t_now - last_tick_).count(), 0.001, 0.2);
707    last_tick_ = t_now;
708
709    printStatus();
710    if (isTerminal(state_)) return;                       // nothing is published any more
711
712    // ---- service timeout (a lost reply must not wedge the node) ----
713    if (service_pending_ &&
714        std::chrono::duration<double>(t_now - service_started_).count() > p_.service_timeout) {
715      RCLCPP_WARN(get_logger(), "MAVROS service reply timed out, will retry");
716      service_pending_ = false;
717    }
718
719    if (!safetyChecks()) return;
720
721    switch (state_) {
722      case State::WAIT_GPS: tickWaitGps(); break;
723      case State::SET_MODE:
724        if (vehicle_state_.mode == "GUIDED") transition(State::ARM);
725        else { trySetMode("GUIDED"); logWaiting("GUIDED mode"); }
726        break;
727      case State::ARM:
728        if (vehicle_state_.armed) transition(State::TAKEOFF);
729        else { tryArm(); logWaiting("arming"); }
730        break;
731      case State::TAKEOFF: tickTakeoff(dt); break;
732      case State::TO_START: tickToStart(dt); break;
733      case State::SQUARE: tickSquare(dt); break;
734      case State::TO_CENTER: tickToCenter(dt); break;
735      case State::FIGURE8: tickFigure8(dt); break;
736      case State::RETURN: tickReturn(dt); break;
737      case State::LAND: tickLand(); break;
738      case State::ABORTING: tickAborting(); break;
739      default: break;
740    }
741
742    // During TAKEOFF do NOT publish position targets: ArduPilot's Guided Takeoff
743    // handles the ascent autonomously. Streaming setpoints cancels takeoff.
744    if (isStreaming(state_)) {
745      if (state_ != State::TAKEOFF) {
746        publishSetpoint();
747      }
748      appendFlownPath();
749    }
750    writeLogRow();
751  }
752
L703–752

tick(): the 30 Hz loop

dt (clamped), status line once per second, stop in terminal states, service timeout (retry instead of hanging), safety checks, then the state's tick function. Setpoints are published only in streaming states and not during TAKEOFF (same reason as A1.2: a setpoint cancels the takeoff). Then the flown path and a CSV row.

753  /// Returns false if the tick must stop here (state already changed).
754  bool safetyChecks()
755  {
756    if (origin_valid_ && !fresh(pose_time_, p_.telemetry_timeout) &&
757        (airborne() || state_ == State::SET_MODE || state_ == State::ARM)) {
758      if (airborne()) stopSilently("local position telemetry stale (MAVROS dropped out?)");
759      else fault("local position telemetry stale");
760      return false;
761    }
762    if (abort_requested_ && state_ != State::ABORTING) {
763      abort_requested_ = false;
764      if (airborne()) beginAbort("abort service called");
765      else { RCLCPP_WARN(get_logger(), "abort requested while on the ground: stopping"); transition(State::FAULT); }
766      return false;
767    }
L753–767

safetyChecks() 1: telemetry and abort

Stale pose: in the air → STOPPED (we cannot control safely), on the ground → FAULT. The abort flag from the service: airborne → ABORTING, on the ground → FAULT.

768    if (isStreaming(state_)) {
769      // The safety pilot / link state always wins: notice and go quiet.
770      if (!vehicle_state_.connected) { stopSilently("MAVROS lost the FCU connection"); return false; }
771      if (!vehicle_state_.armed) { stopSilently("vehicle is no longer armed"); return false; }
772      if (vehicle_state_.mode != "GUIDED") {
773        stopSilently("flight mode is " + vehicle_state_.mode + ", not GUIDED (RC override?) -- not fighting the pilot");
774        return false;
775      }
776      // Our own fence, inside the vehicle's: horizontal radius and altitude relative to launch.
777      const Vec3 p = posePos();
778      const double h = std::hypot(p.x - launch_.x, p.y - launch_.y);
779      const double up = p.z - launch_.z;
780      if (h > p_.max_horizontal) { beginAbort("geofence: " + std::to_string(h) + " m from launch"); return false; }
781      if (up > p_.max_altitude) { beginAbort("geofence: altitude " + std::to_string(up) + " m"); return false; }
782      if (state_ != State::TAKEOFF &&
783          outdoor::dist3(p, cmd_) > p_.max_tracking_error) {
784        beginAbort("tracking error " + std::to_string(outdoor::dist3(p, cmd_)) + " m");
785        return false;
786      }
787    }
L768–787

safetyChecks() 2: pilot, link, fence

Not connected / not armed / mode not GUIDED → STOPPED (the pilot wins). Our own geofence (4 m horizontally, 5.5 m up) and a tracking error over 2 m → ABORT.

788    // Per-state timeouts
789    const double in_state = secondsIn(state_);
790    double limit = 0.0;
791    switch (state_) {
792      case State::SET_MODE: case State::ARM: limit = p_.handshake_timeout; break;
793      case State::TAKEOFF: limit = p_.takeoff_timeout; break;
794      case State::TO_START: case State::SQUARE: case State::TO_CENTER: case State::RETURN:
795        limit = p_.segment_timeout; break;

798      case State::LAND: case State::ABORTING: limit = p_.landing_timeout; break;
799      default: break;
800    }
801    if (limit > 0.0 && in_state > limit) {
802      if (airborne()) beginAbort("state timeout after " + std::to_string(limit) + " s");
803      else fault("state timeout after " + std::to_string(limit) + " s");
804      return false;
805    }
806    return true;
807  }

L788–809

safetyChecks() 3: timeouts

Every state has a limit (the figure-8's depends on ω, laps and ramp). Exceeded: abort in the air, fault on the ground.

810  void tickWaitGps()
811  {
812    std::string why;
813    if (!vehicle_state_.connected) { resetFixAveraging(); logWaiting("MAVROS/FCU not connected"); return; }
814    if (!have_pose_) { logWaiting("no local pose yet"); return; }
815    if (!fixGood(&why)) { resetFixAveraging(); logWaiting("GPS not usable: " + why); return; }
816    if (!stable_since_valid_) {
817      resetFixAveraging();
818      stable_since_ = SteadyClock::now();
819      stable_since_valid_ = true;
820      return;
821    }
822    accumulateFix();                                      // average the fixes during the stable window
823    if (std::chrono::duration<double>(SteadyClock::now() - stable_since_).count() < p_.fix_stable_s) return;
824    if (fix_n_ == 0) return;
825    RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "GPS fix usable and stable for %.1f s (%u satellites, %d fixes averaged)",
826      p_.fix_stable_s, satellites_, fix_n_);
827    if (!captureOrigin()) return;
828    transition(State::SET_MODE);
829  }
830
L810–830

WAIT_GPS

Connected, pose, usable fix; the stable window starts and fixes are averaged; after 3 s of an uninterrupted good fix the origin is captured and SET_MODE follows. Any bad fix restarts the window.

831  void tickTakeoff(double)
832  {
833    cmd_ = {launch_.x, launch_.y, z_fly_};        // consistent with the takeoff command; yaw held
834    if (!takeoff_acknowledged_) { tryTakeoff(); return; }
835    const double climb = posePos().z - launch_.z;
836    const bool at_alt = std::fabs(climb - p_.flight_altitude) <= p_.takeoff_tol;
837    const bool calm = have_vel_ && fresh(vel_time_, 0.5) && speed() <= p_.stabilize_speed;
838    if (!(at_alt && calm)) { stable_since_valid_ = false; return; }
839    if (!stable_since_valid_) { stable_since_ = SteadyClock::now(); stable_since_valid_ = true; return; }
840    if (std::chrono::duration<double>(SteadyClock::now() - stable_since_).count() < p_.stabilize_time) return;
841    RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "Takeoff confirmed: local climb=%.2f m, rel_alt=%.2f m (target %.2f m), speed=%.2f m/s",
842      climb, have_rel_alt_ ? rel_alt_ : std::nan(""), p_.flight_altitude, speed());
843    carrot_ = {posePos().x, posePos().y, z_fly_};
844    transition(State::TO_START);
845  }
846
L831–846

TAKEOFF

Send the takeoff once; confirmed when within 0.2 m of 5 m and slower than 0.15 m/s for 2 s (no fixed sleep, as the assignment requires). Then the carrot starts at the current position.

847  /// Transit from the launch point to W1: nose along the direction of travel; near W1 the drone
848  /// turns to the bearing of the POI, and the square only starts once it is locked on (< 5 deg).
849  /// Starting the square with a wrong heading (e.g. 97 deg off) would pollute the POI-tracking error.
850  void tickToStart(double dt)
851  {
852    const Vec2 w1 = corners_[0];
853    const Vec3 p = posePos();
854    updateYawForTransit(w1, outdoor::bearingYaw(w1, poi_), dt);
855    const bool at_goal = flyCarrotTo({w1.x, w1.y, z_fly_}, p_.square_speed, dt);
856    const double poi_yaw = outdoor::bearingYaw({p.x, p.y}, poi_);
857    const bool locked = std::fabs(outdoor::wrapPi(poseYaw() - poi_yaw)) < 5.0 * outdoor::kDeg2Rad;
858    if (at_goal && locked) {
859      square_index_ = 1;
860      RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "At W1, nose locked on the POI");
861      transition(State::SQUARE);
862    }
863  }
864
L847–864

TO_START

Fly to W1 with the nose along the direction of travel, turn to the POI near W1, and start the square only once locked on (< 5°), so the POI error is not polluted by the arrival turn.

865  void tickSquare(double dt)
866  {
867    updateYawToPoi(dt);
868    const Vec2& g = sq_[square_index_];
869    if (flyCarrotTo({g.x, g.y, z_fly_}, p_.square_speed, dt)) {
870      RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "Reached square corner %zu/%zu", square_index_ + 1, sq_.size());
871      if (++square_index_ >= sq_.size()) transition(State::TO_CENTER);
872    }
873  }
874
L865–874

SQUARE

Nose on the POI every tick, carrot to the next corner; the last corner leads to TO_CENTER.

875  void tickToCenter(double dt)
876  {
877    // Flying W1 -> POI the nose points along the direction of travel; only the last
878    // transit_turn_radius metres turn to the tangent heading of the figure-8 at theta = 0
879    // (atan2(cos 0, cos 0) = 45 deg), which the lap needs when it starts.
880    const double tangent = outdoor::figure8Yaw(0.0);
881    updateYawForTransit(poi_, tangent, dt);
882    const bool there = flyCarrotTo({poi_.x, poi_.y, z_fly_}, p_.square_speed, dt);
883    const bool yaw_ok = std::fabs(outdoor::wrapPi(poseYaw() - tangent)) < 10.0 * outdoor::kDeg2Rad;
884    const bool calm = have_vel_ && fresh(vel_time_, 0.5) && speed() <= p_.stabilize_speed;
885    if (there && yaw_ok && calm) {
886      RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "Centred on POI with tangent heading -- starting figure-8 timer");
887      transition(State::FIGURE8);
888    }
889  }
890
L875–890

TO_CENTER

Fly to the POI, turn to the figure-8's starting tangent (45°), and wait until the drone is there, aligned (< 10°) and calm. This avoids the "lunge" warned about in the assignment.

891  void tickFigure8(double)
892  {
893    const double s = std::chrono::duration<double>(SteadyClock::now() - f8_started_).count();
894    const double th_end = 2.0 * outdoor::kPi * p_.laps;
895    // theta = omega * t, with a soft start and a soft stop (rate ramps 0 -> omega -> 0)
896    const double theta = outdoor::figure8Phase(p_.omega, p_.ramp_s, s, th_end);
897    if (theta >= th_end) {
898      // The commanded curve is finished: hold its END point until the drone has actually
899      // reached it (any remaining lag), so the lap is not cut short.  A timeout keeps this
900      // from hanging.
901      if (!f8_done_) { f8_done_ = true; f8_done_at_ = SteadyClock::now(); }
902      const auto f_end = outdoor::figure8(p_.amplitude, th_end);
903      cmd_ = {poi_.x + f_end.x, poi_.y + f_end.y, z_fly_};
904      cmd_vel_ = {0.0, 0.0, 0.0};
905      carrot_ = cmd_;
906      const bool arrived = outdoor::dist3(posePos(), cmd_) <= p_.waypoint_tol;
907      const bool timeout =
908        std::chrono::duration<double>(SteadyClock::now() - f8_done_at_).count() > p_.figure8_close_timeout;
909      if (arrived || timeout) {
910        RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "Figure-8 complete (%.2f laps in %.1f s)%s", p_.laps, s,
911          arrived ? "" : " [closing timed out]");
912        transition(State::RETURN);
913      }
914      return;
915    }
916    const auto f = outdoor::figure8(p_.amplitude, theta);
917    cmd_ = {poi_.x + f.x, poi_.y + f.y, z_fly_};
918    const double rate = outdoor::figure8PhaseRate(p_.omega, p_.ramp_s, s, th_end);   // d(theta)/dt
919    cmd_vel_ = {f.dx * rate, f.dy * rate, 0.0};                              // analytic velocity
920    // Tangent heading evaluated yaw_lead_s ahead (compensates the yaw controller lag), unwrapped.
921    cmd_yaw_ = outdoor::unwrapNear(cmd_yaw_, outdoor::figure8Yaw(theta + p_.yaw_lead_s * rate));
922    carrot_ = cmd_;
923  }
924
L891–924

FIGURE8: the continuous trajectory

  • Time since start → phase θ with soft start and soft stop (figure8Phase).
  • Position = POI + (A sin θ, A sin θ cos θ); velocity = derivative × dθ/dt (analytic feed-forward).
  • Yaw = tangent atan2(cos 2θ, cos θ), evaluated slightly ahead and unwrapped.
  • At the end: hold the end point until the drone arrives (or a timeout), so the lap is not cut short, then RETURN.
925  void tickReturn(double dt)
926  {
927    // Transit back to the launch point: nose along the direction of travel; the last
928    // transit_turn_radius metres hold the last heading (no pointless spin before landing).
929    const Vec3 p = posePos();
930    const Vec2 here{p.x, p.y}, home{launch_.x, launch_.y};
931    if (outdoor::dist2(here, home) > p_.transit_turn_radius) slewYawTo(outdoor::bearingYaw(here, home), dt);
932    if (flyCarrotTo({launch_.x, launch_.y, z_fly_}, p_.square_speed, dt)) {
933      RCLCPP_INFO(get_logger(), "Back above the launch point -> landing");
934      transition(State::LAND);
935    }
936  }
937
L925–937

RETURN

Fly back above the launch point (nose along travel), then LAND.

943    stopSilently("flight mode changed to " + vehicle_state_.mode + " (RC override?) -- not fighting the pilot");
944    return true;
945  }
